                                       ORDINANCE NO. 2026-10-01

 2   An ordinance relating to land use; adopting an updated Growth Management Comprehensive
 3   Land Use Plan, zoning maps and zoning ordinances; providing for severability; providing an
 4   effective date; and requiring notice.

 5           WHEREAS,Clark County was required to review and, if needed, revise its comprehensive
 6   plan, in accordance with the goals and requirements of RCW 36. 70A (the Growth Management
 7   Act, or GMA) by December 31, 2025; and

 8          WHEREAS, on August 13, 2026, the Growth Management Hearings Board adopted an
 9   amended compliance schedule for the county's completion of the plan with a compliance
10   deadline of October 29, 2026; and

11          WHEREAS,the county's comprehensive plan is required to include maps and descriptive
12   text coveringthe objectives, principles and standards addressingthe essential elements of the
13   plan required by RCW 36. 70A. 070 along with any additional elements and/or subarea plans
14   prepared pursuant to RCW 36. 70A.080; and

15         WHEREAS,GMA directs counties to adopt urban growth areas (areas within which urban
16   growth is encouraged and outside of which only non-urban growth can occur) ("UGAs") and to
17   address these areas in the countywide planning policies; and

18          WHEREAS, RCW 36. 70A. 060 and . 170 mandate that the county designate, where
19   appropriate, critical areas and agricultural, forest, and mineral resources lands, and adopt
20   development regulations to assure the conservation of such lands and areas; and

21          WHEREAS, GMA mandates that there be early and continuous public involvement, and
22   the county adopted the Clark County Public Participation Plan by Resolution 2023-03-05; and

23         WHEREAS, the county is required under Chapter 43. 21C RCW to evaluate environmental
24   impacts that could result from actions it approves or undertakes. As part of the 2025
25   comprehensive plan update, the county established alternatives for study (Resolutions 2025-
26   01-11 and 2025-04-02), selected a preferred alternative ("Exhibit B 2026-0108 Preferred
27   Alternative Selection Table, " adopted April 28, 2026), and issued both a draft EIS (DEIS) and a
28   final EIS (FEIS) containing a detailed analysis of potential environmental impacts of the
29   proposed comprehensive plan and development revisions; and

30           WHEREAS,the county: (1) pursuant to RCW 36. 70A. 110, adopted a population
31   projection based on the range of estimates provided by OFM (Resolution 2023-05-03); and (2)
32   examined the boundaries of its UGAs to ensure that a 20-year land supply (out to 2045)to
33   accommodate population and job growth is available (Resolution 2024-05-05); and

34          WHEREAS,the county, pursuant to RCW 36. 70A.215, submitted by June 30, 2022, to
35   Washington State Department of Commerce the 2022 Buildable Lands Report (Resolution 2022-
36   06-01), satisfying the GMA requirement to review and evaluate the adequacy of suitable
37   residential, commercial and industrial lands inside the Urban Growth Area for accommodating
38   projected 2045 population and employment growth during the 20-year GMA planning horizon; 1
 1   and


 2          WHEREAS, the county, pursuant to Washington State 2023 House Bill 1181 (HB 1181)
 3   codified in RCW 36. 70A. 020(14), RCW 36. 70A. 070(9), RCW 36. 70A. 095, and RCW 70A. 45. 120,
 4   adopted a public participation plan for the Climate Changeand Resiliency Element (Resolution
 5   2023-11-14); and

 6          WHEREAS, pursuant to RCW 36. 70A. 096, on June 27, 2025 the county submitted a
 7   notice of intent to apply for Washington Department of Commerce approval of the greenhouse
 8   gas emissions reduction subelement; and

 9          WHEREAS, on August 8, 2025 the county submitted a draft greenhouse gas emissions
10   reduction subelement for Department of Commerce initial review; and

11          WHEREAS,on November 14, 2025 the county received initial feedbackfrom the
12   Department of Commerce which did not include advice of any county actions necessaryto
13   incorporate into the subelement to receive greenhouse gas emissions reduction subelement
14   approval; and

15           WHEREAS,the county, on March 17, 2022, adopted the Housing Options Study and
16   Action Plan (Resolution 2022-05-11), with implementation of middle housing and small single-
17   family strategies through housing code modifications adopted by approval of Ordinance 2024-
18   03-02 and Ordinance 2024-06-04; and

19           WHEREAS,the county, at the request of certain fire districts within the county,
20   adopted Ordinance 2024-02-03, providingfor county code amendments that authorized fire
21   districts to request review of their capital facilities plans and approval of associated impact
22   fees; and

23          WHEREAS,copies of the county's proposed updated comprehensive plan were
24   submitted to the Washington Department of Commerce on July 24, 2026, more than sixty days
25   (60) prior to final adoption; and

26           WHEREAS,it is expected that the cities will complete the updates to their land use and
27   capital facilities plans to make them consistent with the county plan; and

28          WHEREAS, the county suspended the annual review comprehensive plan
29   amendment cycle from October 1, 2023, through September 30, 2025 (Resolution 2022-09-
30   03), from October 1, 2025, through September 30, 2026, (Resolution 2025-09-01) and from
31   October 1, 2026, through October 30, 2026 (Resolution 2026-09-01) to be consistent with
32   RCW36. 70A. 130(2)(a);and

33          WHEREAS,the Clark County Council and the Clark County Planning Commission held
34   a duly advertised joint hearing on January 8, 2026 and continued to January 15, 2026 on
35   selection of a preferred alternative; and

36          WHEREAS, the Planning Commission held a duly-advertised public hearing on
37   August 20 and September 3, 2026, to deliberate and make its recommendation to the 2
 1   County Council; and

 2        WHEREAS, the County Council held a duly-advertised public hearing on October 1,
 3   October 2/ and October 5, 2026, to consider the Planning Commission recommendation
 4   and to deliberate; and

 5          WHEREAS, at the October 1, October 2, and October 5, 2026, public hearing the
 6   County Council directed the county to form a Housing Commission, form a Mineral
 7   Resources Commission, and commence work on a Public Benefit Rating System (PBRS); and

 8          WHEREAS,the County Council held a properly noticed public meeting on October
 9   13, 2026, to consider this adopting ordinance for the 2025 Comprehensive Plan periodic
10   update (ORD 2026-10-01), and;

11          WHEREAS,at the October 13, 2026, public meeting the County Council voted to 1.
12   Remove Exhibit 10 from the ordinance, 2. Immediately adopt and impose an emergency
13   development moratorium on all land divisions allowed by the resource zone cluster
14   provisions of CCC 40. 210. 010(E) pursuant to RCW 36. 70A. 390 and WAC 197-11-880, and 3.
15   To hold a public hearing on the moratorium within at least sixty days of adoption of this
16   ordinance per RCW 36. 70A. 390.

19   I. FINDINGS

20   The dark County Council (the Council) finds that all GMA prerequisites for the revision of the
21   county's comprehensive plan have been met and that the plan adopted herein achieves the
22   goals and satisfiesthe requirements of the GMA, as follows:

23      1. Corn liance with RCW 36. 70A. 070: Mandator Elements of the Corn rehensive Plan. The
24          20-year Comprehensive Growth Management Plan ("Plan") adopted herein includes all
25          of the following mandatory elements: Land Use, Housing, Capital Facilities and Utilities,
26          Rural and Natural Resources, Transportation, Economic Development, Parks, Recreation
27          and Open Space, Climate and Resiliency, and Shoreline Policies. In addition, the plan also
28          contains the following optional elements: Environment, Historic Preservation, Schools,
29          Community Design, Annexation, and Procedural Guidelines.

30      2. Corn liance with Resource and Critical Areas Desi nations and Develo ment Re ulation
31         Re uirements. The Plan includes maps designating agriculture, forest and mineral
32          resource lands in compliance with RCW 36. 70A. 170, and this ordinance adopts
33          development regulations in dark County Code (CCC) Chapter 40. 210, Resource and
34          Rural Districts, to adequately protect resource lands, and in CCC Chapter 40. 4, Critical
35          Areas, in compliance with RCW 36. 70A. 060. Critical Areas and Shorelines regulations are
36          not being amended or affected in this plan update.

37              a. In early 2025, as part of the 2025 Comprehensive Plan Update, the dark County
38                 Council directed staff to complete a county-wide agricultural resource lands
39                 study. The county, with help from consultants, completed an evaluation of land
 1             within the unincorporated areas, outside of the current urban growth areas. The
 2             general purpose of the study was to help inform the county's decisions regarding
 3             the comprehensive plan update and ensure that the county's agricultural
 4             resource lands designations comply with applicable county code and state
 5             statutes, specifically the Growth Management Act (GMA).
 6          b. At the October 1-5, 2026, public hearing, the County Council considered Planning
 7             Commission recommendations for agricultural de-designation of certain
 8             properties in the preferred land use alternative and made findings in the record
 9             per RCW 36. 70A. 130(5), citing criteria found in WAC 365-190-050and made the
10              following decisions:
11                   i. No. 5 - Camas UGA: Include de-designation of agricultural resource land
12                     and UGA expansionto the east for housing (Nevin property). Motion to
13                     APPROVE the Planning Commission recommendation to approve was
14                     DENIED (resulting in NO de-designation of agricultural resource lands).
15                 ii. No. 6 - La Center UGA north: De-designateagricultural resource lands and
16                     expand the UGA to the north for additional employment capacity. The
17                     Council DENIEDthe Planning Commission recommendation to approve
18                     the de-designation. In addition to making findings based on the WAC
19                     criteria, the council also relied upon information in the record regarding
20                     an interlocal agreement between the City of La Center and the Cowlitz
21                      Tribe involving the affected properties.
22                 iii. No. 7 - La Center UGA: De-designate agricultural resource lands and
23                     expand the UGA to the southwest for additional employment capacity.
24                     The Council APPROVEDthe Planning Commission recommendation to
25                     approve the de-designation as amended by the Council to exclude the
26                     southerly 20-acre equestrian parcel # 211287000from de-designation. In
27                     the decision to exclude parcel # 211287000from de-designation, the
28                     council relied in part on the current equestrian use of the site as a factor
29                 iv. No. 8 - Ridgefield UGA: De-designateagricultural resource lands and
30                     expand the UGAto the north for additional employment and housing
31                     capacity. The Council APPROVED the Planning Commission
32                     recommendation to approve the de-designation. In addition to making
33                     findings based on the WAC criteria, the council also relied upon
34                     information in the record provided by the City of Ridgefield, includingthe
35                     Johnson Economics Study, to support the de-designation decision.
36                 v. No. 9 - Ridgefield UGA: De-designateagricultural resource land and
37                    expand the UGA to the southwest for additional housing capacity (Maul
38                    property). The Council APPROVEDthe Planning Commission
39                    recommendation to approve the de-designation. In particular, the council
40                     relied upon the unique geographical constraints of that property to
41                     support the de-designation decision, noting that it is less than 20 acres in
42                     size and completely surrounded by other City of Ridgefield lands, making
43                     it wholly non-contiguous with other agricultural lands.

45   3. Public Partici ation. The public participation requirements of the GMA (RCW
46      36. 70A. 140) have been met through an extensive public involvement processthat 4
 1      included but is not limited to the following:
 2      a. A Technical Advisory Committee comprised of planners from the cities, who met
 3         monthly to discuss planning issues of a technical nature;
 4      b. Four work sessions and two public hearings related to the Housing Options Study
 5           and Action Plan;
 6      c. Three rounds of public meetings (May 2024 (scoping); December 2024 (alternatives);
 7         and January2026 (preferred alternative)) on the environmental impact statement;
 8      d. 33 work sessions and 6 public hearings before the Planning Commission throughout
 9         the update process;
10      e. 36 work sessions and 17 public hearings before the Council throughout the update
11         process to discuss/adopt population and jobs targets, planning assumptions, the
12           vacant and buitdable lands model, EIS alternatives, and various other plan-related
13           items. This included a joint hearing with the Planning Commission to discuss the
14           preferred alternative.
15      f.   Extensive use of the dark County Community Planning website,
16           htt s: www.clark. wa. ov communit - lannin 2025-u date,
17           that included information on the GMA; an outline of the comprehensive plan update
18           process; notices of meetings and hearings; maps of alternatives considered in the
19           SEPA process; meeting summaries and issue discussions; the DEIS; and the FEIS. In
20           addition, a dedicated email address was included to comment on the comprehensive
21         plan as well as an electronic comment form;
22      g. Periodic email announcements sent to comprehensive plan e-mail lists
23         containing over 2, 250 addresses;
24      h. Periodic press releases on the status of the update process;
25      i. Outreach to neighborhood associations and community groups;
26      j. Several 'Clark County Close-up' segments on the update process on CVTV;
27      k. Televised public hearings held by the Planning Commission and the County Council
28           on plan adoption.
29
30   4. State Environmental Polic Act SEPA . For the 2025 comprehensive plan update, the
31      county prepared a full environmental impact statement (EIS) covering a maximum study
32      area. The county has complied with the environmental review process required by SEPA,
33      as follows:
34      a. A determination of significancewas issued on May 15, 2024. (WAC 197-11-330and
35         197-11-360)
36      b. A 21-dayscoping period was held from May 15 to June 5, 2024 to invite agency,
37         affected tribes, and public to comment. Public input was provided at three in-person
38         public meetings, an online comment submittal webpage, through a web-accessible
39         open house-style presentation, via email and postal mail. (WAC 197-11-408, 197-11-
40         502, 197-11-792).
41      c. The County Council held a hearing and approved three alternatives to study in a
42         draft environmental impact statement (DEIS)on December 6, 2024. The County
43           Council adopted a resolution documenting the alternatives to study in the DEIS on
44         January 28, 2025 (Resolution 2025-01-11).The County Council amended Resolution
45         2025-01-11 on April 15, 2025 (Resolution 2025-04-02).
46      d. Three in-person open houses and one web-accessibleself-paced open-house style
47         presentation was provided to explain the three proposed alternatives in May-June,
48           2025.                                                                5
 1       e. The DEIS analyzing three alternatives was issued on October 1, 2025 and a 60-day
 2            comment period was held. (WAC 197-11-405, 197-11-440, 197-11-442, 197-11-455,
 3          197-11-502, 197-11-510)
 4       f. A joint Planning Commission and County Council hearing was held on January8,
 5            2026 and continued to January 15, 2026 on selection of a preferred alternative.
 6       g. The Planning Commission continued deliberations and made a recommendation on a
 7          preferred alternative on January29, 2026.
 8       h. The County Council held a public hearing on selection of a preferred alternative April
 9            27 to 28, 2026 and decided on a preferred alternative.
10       i.   A Final EIS (FEIS) was issued on July 24, 2026. The FEIS focused on a preferred
11            alternative, which subsequently became the proposal that was the subject of public
12            hearings before the Planning Commission and the County Council. The FEISalso
13            responded to comments received on the DEIS. (WAC 197-11-405, 197-11-440, 197-
14            11-442, 197-11-460, 197-11-510, 197-11-560)
15       j.   An addendumto the FEISwas issued on August 12, 2026. (WAC 197-11-706)
16       k. The FEIS was a major decision-support tool in the comprehensive plan update
17            process. (RCW 43. 21C. 030(b), 43. 21C. 060).

18   5. Amendment. The Comprehensive Plan provides adequate monitoring and amendment
19      procedures.
20       a. The Comprehensive Plan provides for a procedure to monitor internal and
21            inter-jurisdictional consistency.
22       b. The Comprehensive Plan contains provisions governing its amendment.
23            Amendments will not be considered more than once each year.
24       c. The Comprehensive Plan provides for an automatic comprehensive review of urban
25            growth areas at least once every ten years.

26   6. Record of Process.
27       a. The dark County Planning Commission conducted a duly-advertised public meeting
28            concerning recommendations for adoption of the Comprehensive Plan on August 20
29            and September 3, 2026.
30       b. The County Council conducted a duly-advertised public meeting on the Planning
31            Commission recommendations and adoption of the Comprehensive Plan on October
32            1, October 2, and October 5, 2026.
33       c. All public hearings before the Planning Commission and the County Council included
34          opportunities for public comment.
35       d. All public hearings before the Planning Commission and the County Council were
36            transcribed, digitally-recorded, and televised locally on cable television. Written
37            transcripts, and video and audio recordings are on file.
38       e. Copies of all news releases, and other selected publications are on file.
39       f. Copies of all written correspondence received by the county are on file.

40   7   Internal Consistenc . The Comprehensive Plan is internally consistent.
41       a. The policies within and among elements are complementary, not contradictory. Both
42          separately and together, they further the goals of the GMA.
43       b. The land use map represents a detailed analysis carried out in cooperation with
44            individual cities and the business and environmental communities, including
45            overburdened and vulnerable population groups. The analysis and draft land use 6
 l          maps were subject to public scrutiny for several months as the analysis progressed.
 2       c. The Comprehensive Plan contains policies, implementation measures, and
 3          procedures which provide for its review and adjustment if internal conflicts are
 4          discovered.


 5   8. Individual Site-S ecific Re uests. dark County accepted site-specific comprehensive
 6      plan and zoning amendment requests through March 15, 2024. The county
 7      considered individual site-specific requests for a change in designation and/or
 8      zoning as part of the plan update and as part of the public hearing process, and
 9      selected requests were included in the preferred alternative.

10   9. Concurrenc . The Comprehensive Plan meets the concurrency requirement of the GMA.
11      The plan requires direct concurrency for transportation (implemented by the
12      concurrency ordinance which specifies levels of service for identified corridors), for
13      water and for sewer, and the plan includes six and twenty-year project lists for these.
14      The plan also includes project lists for indirect concurrency elements (schools, parks, and
15      stormwater).

16   10. Inter- Jurisdictional Coordination. The Comprehensive Plan update was accomplished in
17      coordination with the cities of Battle Ground, Camas, La Center, Ridgefield, Vancouver,
18      Washougal,and Woodland, and the Town of Yacolt. The county formed a coordinating
19      committee that included each city planning director; that group met monthly
20      throughout the entire update process.

21      The Comprehensive Plan is consistent with adopted county-wide planning policies. The
22      Community Framework Plan introduces the relevant element chapters of the plan.
23      County-wide planning policies are included in each plan element.

24   11. Coordination with other   lans.
25      a. The Comprehensive Plan is coordinated with those of neighboringjurisdictions.
26      b. Environmental documents were developed jointly by the county and the cities within
27         it, except that the City of Vancouver issued documents separately from the county.
28      c. Capital facilities plans were produced in cooperation with cities, C-Tran, service
29         providers, public safety agencies, school districts, and metropolitan area planning
30          agencies.
31      d. An updated parks, recreation and open space plan was adopted by the county in
32         2022 for the unincorporated portion of the county, includingthe Vancouver UGA.
33      e. Preliminary review of the Comprehensive Plan meets the Southwest Washington
34         Regional Transportation Council (RTC) coordination certification process, which
35         requires RTCto indicate that the County's Transportation Element has demonstrated
36         consistency with the RegionalTransportation Plan for dark County (RTP, February
37         2024), reflects the guidelines and principles under RCW 47. 80.026, and satisfythe
38         requirements of RCW 36. 70A.070 (6).

39   12. Anal sis of Potential Environmental Im acts. The county prepared a Final Environmental
40      Impact Statement (FEIS)for the 2025 Comprehensive Plan Update. The county issued
41      the Draft Environmental Impact Statement (DEIS)on October 1, 2025. The county issued
42      the FEIS on July 24 2026, with an Addendum on August 12, 2026. The DEIS and FEIS wer^
43      issued jointly for the comprehensive plans of the county and its cities and Town of
 1      Yacolt, except for the City of Vancouver, which prepared its own, independent SEPA
 2      documents. Affectedjurisdictions and the public have had an opportunity to and did
 3      comment on this analysis.

 4   13. GMA Goals. The Comprehensive Plan addresses the goals of the GMA through its
 5      policies and implementation measures. The GMA contains 15 goals listed as follows,
 6      with corresponding policies of the plan noted. The plan is also in compliance with more
 7      specific citations contained within the GMA. Plan goals and policies which achieve
 8      compliance with the GMA in its entirety are also not limited to those cited below in
 9      association with the 15 basic goals.
10      a. Urban Growth: Encourage development in urban areas where adequate public
11         facilities and services exist or can be provided in an efficient manner.

12         The Community Framework Plan contains policies that direct growth within the
13         region into existing urban areas. The plan addressesthis objective most directly
14         through the goals and policies in the Land Use Element, as well as the adoption of
15         new urban growth boundaries surrounding local cities. Supporting policies and
16         implementation measures are contained throughout the document.

17      b. Reduces rawl: Reduce the inappropriate conversion of undeveloped land into
18         sprawling, low-density development.

19         The plan addresses this objective directly through the goals and policies in the Land
20         Use Element, as well as the adoption of new urban growth boundaries surrounding
21         local cities.

22      c. Trans ortation: Encourage efficient multimodal transportation systems that will
23         reduce greenhouse gas emissions and per capita vehicle miles traveled, and are
24         based on regional priorities and coordinated with county and city comprehensive
25         plans.
26
27         The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
28         in the Transportation Element.
29
30      d. Housin : Plan for and accommodate housing affordable to all economic segments of
31         the population of this state, promote a variety of residential densities and housing
32         types, and encourage preservation of existing housing stock.

33         The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
34         in the Housing Element, as well as the variety of residential designations contained
35         in the Comprehensive Plan map.

36      e. Economic Develo ment: Encourage economic development throughout the
37         state that is consistent with adopted comprehensive plans, promote economic
38         opportunity for all citizens of this state, especially for unemployed and for
39         disadvantaged persons, promote the retention and expansion of existing
40         businesses and recruitment of new businesses, recognize regional differences
41         impacting economic development opportunities, and encourage growth in areas
42         experiencing insufficient economic growth, all within the capacities of the state's
          natural resources, public services, and public facilities.

 2        The Comprehensive Plan addressesthis most directly through the goals and policies
 3        in the Economic Development Element, and through the designation of industrial
 4        and commercial lands on the Comprehensive Plan map.

 5   f.   Property Rights: Private property shall not be taken for public use without just
 6        compensation having been made. The property rights of landowners shall be
 7        protected from arbitrary and discriminatory actions.

 8        It is the intent of dark County in administeringthe Comprehensive Plan to ensure
 9        that regulatory actions affecting private property are not arbitrary or discriminatory
10        in any way. The rights of private property owners and the avoidance of any taking of
11        private property withoutjust compensation have been given due consideration in
12        the development of the Comprehensive Plan policies and implementation measures.

13   g. Permits: Applications for both state and local government permits should be
14        processed in a timely and fair manner to ensure predictability.

15        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
16        in the Housing Element, Rural and Resource Element and the Economic
17        Development Element, and through ordinance languagethat outlines specific
18        standardized processes for permit issuance.

19   h. Natural Resource Industries: Maintain and enhance natural resource-based
20        industries, including productive timber, agricultural, and fisheries industries.
21        Encourage the conservation of productive forestlands and productive agricultural
22        lands, and discourage incompatible uses.

23        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
24        in the Rural and Resource Element and the Economic Development Element, and
25        through specific identification and designation of agricultural, forest, and mineral
26        resource lands. The county's surface mining overlay policies and code were updated
27        in 2014, pursuant to RCW 36. 70A. 170.

28   i.   0 en S ace and Recreation: Retain open space and green space, enhance
29        recreational opportunities, enhance fish and wildlife habitat, increase access to
30        natural resource lands and water, and develop parks and recreation facilities.

31        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
32        in the Environmental Element and the Parks, Recreation, and Open Space Element. A
33        parks, recreation and open space plan was adopted by the county in 2015 for the
34        unincorporated portion of the Vancouver UGA. The plan was updated in 2022.

35   j.   Environment: Protect and enhance the environment and enhance the state's high
36        quality of life, including air and water quality, and the availability of water.

37        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
38        in the Environmental Element and the Parks, Recreation, and Open Space Element, 9
 1        the Climate Element, and related ordinances relating to protection of critical areas
 2        and shorelines. The count's critical areas ordinances have been updated based on
 3        best available science pursuant RCW 36. 70A. 172.

 4   k. Citizen Partici ation and Coordination: Encourage the involvement of citizens in
 5        the planning process, including the participation of vulnerable populations and
 6        overburdened communities, and ensure coordination between communities and
 7        jurisdictions to reconcile conflicts.

 8        Throughout the plan update process dark County has provided extensive
 9        opportunities for citizen involvement and coordination, ranging from community-
10        wide open houses to surveys to formal hearing testimony. The Community
11        Involvement Process is detailed in the Introduction to the Comprehensive Plan, and
12        within this ordinance, including Appendix F.

13   I.   Public Facilities and Services: Ensure that those public facilities and services
14        necessaryto support development shall be adequate to serve the development at
15        the time the development is available for occupancy and use without decreasing
16        current service levels below locally established minimum standards.

17        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
18        in the Capital Facilities and Utilities Element and the Schools Element, Appendix E
19        and the county's transportation concurrency ordinance.

20   m. Historic Preservation: Identifyand encourage the preservation of lands, sites, and
21        structures, that have historical or archaeological significance.

22        The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through the goals and policies
23        in the Historic, Archaeological and Cultural Preservation Element.

24   n. Climate Chan e and Resilienc : Ensure that comprehensive plans, development
25        regulations, and regional policies, plans, and strategies under RCW 36. 70A. 210 and
26        chapter 47. 80 RCW adapt to and mitigate the effects of a changing climate; support
27        reductions in greenhouse gas emissions and per capita vehicle miles traveled;
28        prepare for climate impact scenarios; foster resiliency to climate impacts and natural
29        hazards; protect and enhance environmental, economic, and human health and
30        safety; and advance environmental justice.

31        The Comprehensive Plan addressesthis most directly through the inclusion of a new
32        Climate Element (Chapter 14 of the plan), and Appendix I (Climate Element Resource
33        Documents), and through integration of and cross-references to other affected
34        Elements and appendicesof the plan. The Climate Element includes a resilience sub
35        element and, following review and approval by the Washington Department of
36        Commerce, will also include a greenhouse gas emissions reduction sub element.

37   o. Shorelines of the State: For shorelines of the state, the goals and policies of the
38        shoreline management act as set forth in RCW 90. 58. 020 shall be considered an
39        element of the county's or city's comprehensive plan.                                  10
 1             The Comprehensive Plan addresses this most directly through Chapter 13, Shoreline
 2             Master Program. In addition, the county's shoreline policies, are included in the
 3             Shoreline Element of the comprehensive plan.

 4      14. Com liance with count wide lannin         olicies. As required by the GMA, dark County
 5         participated in discussionswith cities within the county to revise urban growth area
 6         boundaries, and adopted such boundariesfor each city consistent with the countywide
 7         planning policies. Further, the county provided notification to surroundingjurisdictions
 8         of its comprehensive plan development process. The county has achieved consistency
 9         with adopted countywide planning policies.

10     15. Com liance with submission re uirements. The required notice of intent to adopt was
11         filed with the Washington Department of Commerce within the required time frame.
12         a. Commerce received notice of the county's intent to adopt a comprehensive plan
13            under the GMA on July 24, 2026.
14         b. Comments were received from Commerce on September 22, 2026. Commerce's
15            comments were considered in the update of the Comprehensive Plan.

16     16. Im lementation measures. The county has adequate development regulations in place
17         through dark County Code Title 40 Unified Development Code. Adoption of updates to
18         zoning ordinances and other measures necessary to implement the Comprehensive Plan
19         are being adopted as part of this ordinance.

20     17 Ca ital Facilities Plans. Capital facilities plans for service providers (including school
21        districts, public safety, parks, water, sewer, and transportation) satisfy GMA
22        requirements are incorporated into the comprehensive plan.

23   II. COMPREHENSIVEPLANADOPTION

24        Adoption of the updated dark County Comprehensive Plan. The 2025 Comprehensive
25        Growth Management Plan is hereby adopted as the 20-year land use plan as the GMA
26        Comprehensive Plan for dark County. The plan consists of the following documents:
27        a. The 20-year Comprehensive Growth Management Plan document and all text and
28            policies contained therein, including all Appendices A - 0 (Exhibit 1), except for the
29           greenhouse gas subelement which is adopted in Section 2 of this ordinance herein
30           for submission to the Washington Department of Commerce.
31        b. An updated map showing plan designationsfor unincorporated rural and resource
32           lands as well as lands within urban growth boundaries in dark County (Exhibit 2).
33        c. An updated map showing the corresponding zoning that implements the plan
34           designations (Exhibit 3).
35        d. The 2025 Comprehensive Plan parcel number detail (Exhibit3a).
36        e. The Clark County Arterial Atlas (Exhibit4).
37        f. The following items, incorporated by reference:
38            i.   Vacant and Buildable Lands Analyses for urban growth areas;
39           ii.   School Districts and Fire Districts capital facilities plans.
40        Approval of Greenhouse Gas Emissions Reduction subelement for submission to
41        Washington Department of Commerce. The Greenhouse Gas Emissions Reduction
42        subelement of the Climate Change and Resiliency Element included in the Plan adopter
43        in Section 1 of this ordinance is hereby adopted for submission to and approval by the
 1        Washington State Department of Commerce ("Commerce") pursuant to RCW
 2        36. 70A.096(3)(a). The Greenhouse Gas Emissions Reduction subelement shall be
 3        effective upon the date of issuance of a written decision of approval by Commerce
 4        pursuant to RCW 36. 70A.096(1) and .096(4)(b) and, upon such approval, shall be
 5        deemed incorporated into the Plan adopted in Section 1 of this ordinance without the
 6        necessity of further action by the County Council.

12    V. MISCELLANEOUS

13        1. Effective Date. This ordinance shall go into effect 10 days after adoption, at 12:01 a. m.
14           on October 23, 2026, except for:

15            a.    School, fire, and traffic impact fees (Section IV, Parts 1, 2, and 3), which will take
16                  effect 30 days after adoption at 12:01 a. m. on November 12, 2026; and
17 b. The greenhouse gas emissions reduction sub-element of the Climate Change and
18    Resiliency Element becomes effective on the date of issuance of a written decision
19    of approval by the Department of Commerce, pursuant to RCW36. 70A.096(1) and
20 . 096(4)(b); and
21 c. Urban growth area expansionsand resource zone de-designationsbecome effective
22    60 days after the date of publication of notice of adoption of the comprehensive
23    plan.

24       2. Severability. If any section, clause, or phrase of this ordinance should be held invalid or
25            unconstitutional by the Growth Management Hearings Board or a court of competent
26            jurisdiction, such invalidity or unconstitutionality shall not affect the validity or
27            constitutionality of any other section, sentence, clause or phrase of this ordinance.

     Aboveground storage tanks "Aboveground storage tank" means any one (1) or combination of tanks

                                    (2) or more lots share a common boundary line only at a comer or corners,
                                    they shall not be considered as abutting unless the common boundary line
                                    between the two (2) parcels measures not less than eight (8) feet in a single
                                    direction.

                             use, as required by this title. Residential lots shall be provided a minimum

                             "adjacent."

Agricultural market          "Agricultural market" means a permanent building or accessory structure

                             other farms in the local agricultural area, and may include incidental retail
                             sale of items or fee-basedactivities, both as accessoryto the primary on-

                             distinguished from a roadside fann stand by a larger scale of activity and a

                             winery.

uses or agricultural         of the land for agricultiu-al purposes, including, but not limited to, farming,
activities                   dairying, pasturage, agriculture, horticulture, floriculture, viticulture and

Alley, public                "Public alley" means a right-of-way not over thirty (30) feet wide which

                             intended for general use.
All-weather driving surface "All-weather driving surface" means any road or driveway surface that
                            when reasonably maintained will provide all-weather driving capabilities
                            for fire and other emergency vehicle apparatus.
Alteration, structural       "Structural alteration" means any change or repair which would tend to
                             prolong the life of the supporting members of a building or structure. Any
                             change in the external dimensions ofthe building is a structural alteration.
Altered                      "Altered," whenreferringto wetlands, means a wetlandofwhichat least
                             fifty percent (50%) has been graded, drained, devegetated, or replanted
                             with nonwetland plants.
Ambulancedispatchfacility "Ambulancedispatchfacility"means a public or private structure usedto
                             house a crew and ambulanceto be dispatchedfrom a central ambulance

                             location to serve a specific area of the county with a quick response time.
Ambulance service facility   "Ambulance service facility" means a public or private structure used to
                             house ambulance service administrative personnel, equipment and supply
                             storage, and repair and radio dispatch equipment. This facility may also
                             include an ambulance dispatch facility.

                             or multiple indooror outdoor amusements such as battingcages,video
                             arcades, go-cart tracks, bumper cars or amusement rides. Traveling
                             carnivals and circuses shall not be considered amusement centers, l
Anadromous                    "Anadromous" means fish that migrate up rivers and streams from the
                              ocean to breed in fresh water.

Animal boarding facility      "Animal boarding facility" means any premises used to conduct a business
                              that involves overnight boarding of domestic animals for any purpose, but
                              not to include animal hospitals, veterinary clinics, or kennels.
                                       (Amended: Ord. 2011-03-09; Ord. 2012-02-03)

Animal day use facility       "Animal day use facility" means any premises usedto conduct a daytime-

                                       2012-02-03)

Animal hospital or            "Animal hospital" or "veterinary clinic" means a place where animals or
veterinary clinic             pets are given medical or surgical treatment, and are cared for during the

Animal husbandry              "Animal husbandry" means the care and raising of domesticated animals,
                              such as cattle, horses, sheep, llamas, and poultry, but not including
                              household pets, such as dogs and cats.
                                   (Amended: Ord. 2006-07-09; Ord. 2006-08-03)

Applicant                     "Applicant"meansthe person, party, firm, corporation, legal entity, or
                              agent thereof who submits an application for an activity regulated by this
                              title.

Aquifer                       "Aquifer" means a groundwater-bearing geologic formation or formations
                              that contain enough saturated penneable material to yield significant
                              quantities of water to wells or springs (source: Chapter 173-100 WAC).
Archaeological site          "Archaeological site" means a site containing significant physical evidence
                             or ruins of human occupation or activity that are located on or below the
                             surface ofthe ground and are at least one hundred(100) years old.
                             Archaeological resources on these sites include, but are not limited to, the
                             remains of houses, villages, camp and fishing sites, and cave shelters; rock
                             art such as petroglyphs and pictographs; artifacts such as arrowheads,
                             utensils, tools, fragments of tools and utensils, obsidian flakes or other
                             material byproducts from tool and utensil-making activities; and graves,
                             humanremains, and associatedartifacts.
Areaofspecial flood           "Areaofspecialflood hazard"or "floodhazardarea"meansthe land in the
hazard, or flood hazard area floodplain subject to the base flood.
Arterial                      "Arterial" means any urban parkway arterial, urban principal arterial, or
                             urban minor arterial, as defined in Tables 40. 350. 030-2 and 40. 350. 030-3.
                             This term is usedto differentiate arterials from collectors,
                             commercial/industrial, and access roads.

Arterial Atlas               "Arterial Atlas" means the current dark County Arterial Atlas as adopted
                             by Council, or hereafter amended.

                             Washington as a boarding home, where staff assumes responsibility for the
                              safety and well-beingofthe adult residents. Housing,meals, laundry,
                              supervision and varying levels of assistance with daily activities are
                              provided by staff and may include nursing care. An assisted living facility
                              contains seven (7) or more assisted living units.

clubs                         are charged to engage in physical activities including aerobics, racket

                              irrespective of any overlay district.
Basin                         "Basin" means a watershed.

establishment                 or family resides and rents up to six (6) bedrooms to guests and provides

(BMPs)                  andmanagerialpractices, andprohibitionsofpractices, that, whenused

                              property; and placement of manufactured homes or mobile homes on
                              leased sites.

Bioretention facility         "Bioretention facilities" are shallow landscapeddepressions, with a

                              SWMMWW).)

Block                         "Block" means an area of land whose boundaries are defined by public or
                              private streets, excluding alleys.
Block length                  "Block length" means the distance between intersections with other public
                              or private roads as measuredalongthe nearsideright-of-wayline.
Block perimeter               "Block perimeter" means the perimeter of a block as measured along the

                              exclusive of driveways.
Boarding house                "Boarding house" means a building other than u hotel with furnishrd

Branch bank         "Branch bank" means a service outlet providing primarily personal
                    banking andrelated financial services to the general public.
Bridge              "Bridge" means a structure including supports erected over a depression or
                    an obstruction, such as water, highway, or railway, and characterized by
                    the following:
                    . A track or passageway for carrying traffic or other moving loads; and

                    .   An opening measured along the center of the roadway of:

                    o   Single-span steel or concrete with spans of six (6) feet or greater;

                    o   Steel corrugated pipes with spans of eight (8) feet or greater;

                    o   Timber with spans of four (4) feet or greater;

                    o Multiple pipes with spans often (10) feet or greater with multiple
                    spans of eight (8) feet or greater between undercopings of abutments or
                    spring lines of arches, or extreme ends of openings for multiple boxes; or

                    o Multiple pipes, where the clear distance between openings is less than
                    half of the smaller contiguous opening.

                    Bridges do not include structures with the depth of fill more than D/2,
                    where D is the maximum span.

                        official, an expert approved by dark County (such as, but not limited to, a
                        professional forester or landscape architect), or a similar expert employed
                        by another public agency or utility, has a strong likelihood of causing a 3^
                                hazard to life or property.

Hazardous substances            "Hazardous materials" or "hazardous substances" means such material as
                                flammable solids; corrosive liquids; radioactive material; oxidizing
                                material; highly toxic material; poisonous gases; reactive material;
                                unstable material; hyperbolic material; pyrophoric material as defined in
                                Chapter 2 ofthe International Fire Code; and substances, or mixture of
                                substances, that are an irritant or strong sensitizer or which generate
                                pressure through exposure to heat, decomposition, or other means.
                                "Hazardoussubstances" shall also meanhazardouswaste as designatedin
                                Chapter 173-303 WAC as dangerous or extremely hazardous waste.
                                "Hazardoussubstances" also means any dangerous waste or extremely
                                hazardous waste as defined in RCW 70. 105. 010(5) and (6), or any
                                dangerous or extremely dangerous waste as designated by rule under
                                Chapter 70. 105 RCW; any hazardous substance as defined in RCW
                                70. 105.010(14) or any hazardoussubstance as definedby rule under
                                Chapter 70. 105 RCW; and any substance that, on the effective date of the
                                ordinance codified in this section, is a hazardous substance under Section
                                101(14) ofthe FederalCleanupLaw,42 U. S.C., Section9601(14);
                                petroleum products; and any substance or category of substances including
                                solid waste decompositionproducts, determined by the Washington
                                Department of Ecology's director to present a threat to human health or the
                                environment if released into the environment. The term "hazardous
                                substances" does not include any of the following when contained in an
                                underground storage tank from which there is not a release of crude oil or
                                any fraction thereof or petroleum, if the tank is in compliance with all
                                applicable federal, state, and local laws.
                                    (Amended: Ord. 2006-09-13)

Hazardous waste                 "Hazardous waste" means all dangerous and extremely hazardous waste as
                                defined in RCW 70. 105. 010.
Hazardous waste storage         "Hazardous waste storage" means the holding of dangerous waste for a
                                temporary period as regulated by the State Dangerous Waste Regulations,
                                Chapter 173-303 WAC.
Hazardous waste treatment       "Hazardous waste treatment" means the physical, chemical or biological
                                processing of dangerous waste to make such wastes nondangerous or less
                                dangerous, safer for transport, amenable for energy or material recovery,
                                amenable for storage, or reduced in volume.

High intensity land use   "Highintensity land use," whenreferringto the regulationofwetlands,
                          means the following uses of land: roadways, commercial, industrial, and
                          multifamily exceeding four (4) units per parcel.
High-impactuse            "High-impactuse," whenreferringto critical aquiferrechargeareas, means
                          a business establishment that is regulated due to the probability and/or
                          magnitude of its effects on the environment. For purposes of this chapter,
                          these uses possess certain characteristics posing a substantial potential
                          threat or risk to the quality of groundwater and surface waters within
                          Category I CARAs. High-impact uses shall include, but are not limited to,
                          the following:
                          .     Landfills;

                          .     Class V injection wells:

                          o      Agricultural drainage wells,

                          o      Untreated sewagewaste disposal wells,

                          o      Cesspools,

                          o     Industrial process water and disposal wells, and

                          o     Radioactivewaste disposal;

                          .     Radioactive disposal sites; and

                          .     Activities in Section 40. 410. 020(8) that are not connected to public
                          sewer.

Hospital                   "Hospital" means any institution, place, building or agency which
                           maintains and operates organized facilities for twenty (20) or more persons
                           for the diagnosis, care and treatment ofhiunan illness, including
                           convalescence and also including care during and after pregnancy; or
                           which maintains and operates organized facilities for any such purpose,
                           andto whichpersons may be admitted for overnight stay or for a longer
                           period.
Hotel                      "Hotel" means a place of lodging that provides sleeping accommodations,
                           restaurants, cocktail lounges, meeting and banquet rooms or convention
                           facilities, and other retail and service shops.
Hydric soil                "Hydric soil" means a soil that is saturated, flooded or ponded long enough
                           during the growing season to develop anaerobic conditions in the upper
                           part. The presenceofhydric soil shall be detemiinedfollowingthe
                           methods described in the Wetlands Delineation Manual.

Hydroperiod                "Hydroperiod" means a seasonal occurrence of flooding and/or soil
                           saturation; it encompasses depth, frequency, duration, and seasonal pattern
                           of inundation.

Hydrophytic vegetation     "Hydrophytic vegetation" means macrophytic plant life growing in water
                           or on a substrate that is at least periodically deficient in oxygen as a result
                           of excessive water content. The presence of hydrophytic vegetation shall
                           be determined following the methods described in the Wetlands
                           Delineation Manual.
Intermittent stream        "Intermittent stream" means surface streams with no measurable flow
                           duringthirty (30) consecutive days in a normal wateryear.

                           Code as adopted by dark County.

significance               least three (3) approaches have a comprehensive plan functional
                           classification of collector or higher.

                           Section 40. 445. 020(0) or shoreline jurisdiction as defined in Section 3
                           40. 460. 210(A) and are separated from other wetlands by a distance greater
                           than the largest wetland buffers required under Section 40. 445. 020(B)(5)
                           for all adjacent wetlands.

                           . Any premises used to conduct a commercial business involving the

                           clinics where animals are kept only for treatment by licensed veterinarians;

                           * Any premises where offspring puppies or adult dogs are sold to
                           commercial outlets or are sold for research or experimental purposes;

                           . Any premises where offspring from three (3) or more litters per twelve
                           (12) month period are sold or traded, exchanged or bartered for a valuable

Land-disturbing activity   "Land-disturbing activity" means any activity that results in a change in
                           the existing soil cover (both vegetative and nonvegetative) and/orthe
                           existing soil topography. Land-disturbing activities include, but are not
                           limited to, clearing, grading, filling, and excavation. Compaction that is
                           associated with stabilization of structures and road construction is also
                           considered a land-disturbingactivity. Vegetationmaintenance practices,
                           including landscape maintenance and gardening, are not considered land-
                           disturbingactivity. Stormwaterfacility maintenanceis not consideredland-
                           disturbing activity if conducted according to established standards and
                           procedures.

Lot coverage              "Lot coverage" means that percentage of the total lot area covered by
                          structures, including all projections except eaves.
Lot depth                 "Lot depth" means the horizontal distance between the midpoint of the
                          front and opposite, usually the rear lot line. Average lot depth shall bethe
                          average of the side lot lines. In the case of a comer lot, the depth shall be
                          the average length of the longer dimension of the lot. In the case of flag
                          lots, lot depth shall be the averagelength ofthe longer dimensionofthe
                          mainbody ofthe lot. SeeFigure 40. 100.070-2.
                              (Amended: Ord. 2007-06-05; Ord. 2018-01-09)

Lot line                  "Lot line" means any line bounding a lot as herein defined. Lot lines for
                          unusual lot configurations may be determined by the responsible official.
Lot line, front           "Front lot line" means that portion of the property line abutting a street
                          right-of-way, street easement, street tract, or private driveway easement.
                          . For corner lots, the front lot line is that which provides vehicular
                          access. In the case where vehicular access is provided on more than one (1)
                          street, one (1) front lot line and one (1) street side lot line shall be
                          designated. Within a street side setback, entrances to garages, carports, or
                          similar vehicular shelters shall maintain a minimum eighteen (18) foot
                          setback from the property line, street easement, street tract or inside edge
                          of any pedestrian easement, when the street side setback for the applicable
                          zoning district is less than eighteen (18) feet. If access is provided to a
                          comer lot by an alley, the front lot line is that which is most opposite the
                          alley.

                          .     For a flag lot, the front lot line is the juncture between the pole and the
                          flag. (SeeFigure40. 100.070-2.)
                          . For through lots, all lot lines abutting a street or approved private road
                          or easement, except for alleys, shall be front lot lines unless vehicular
                          access is prohibited, in which case they shall be considered rear lot lines.
                            (Amended: Ord. 2007-06-05; Ord. 2007-11-13; Ord.
                            2008-06-02; Ord. 2016-09-04)

Lot line, rear          "Rear lot line" means a lot line not abutting a street which is opposite and
                        most distant from the front lot line.

Lot line, side          "Side lot line" means any lot line which is not a front lot line or a rear lot
                        line. In the case offlag lots, all lot lines other than the front lot line are
                        considered side lot lines.
                            (Amended: Ord. 2007-06-05)

Lot line, street side   "Street side lot line" means a side property line abutting a public right-of-

                        originally created or segregated, or which is otherwise determined to be

                        Parcels segregated for tax purposes are not lots of record unless they
                        comply with both platting and zoning laws in existence at the time that an
                        application for segregation is received by the County Assessor, or are
                        otherwise determined to be consistent with the criteria of the UDC.
Lot width               "Lot width" means the horizontal distance measured at the building
                        setback line between the two (2) opposite side lot lines. Average lot width
                        shall be the average of the front and rear lot lines. For a corner lot, the lot
                        width shall be the average distance ofthe narrower dimension ofthe lot.
                            (Amended: Ord. 2007-06-05)

Lot, comer              "Lot, corner" means a lot abutting upon two (2) or more streets at their
                        intersection, or upon two (2) parts of the same street, such street or parts of
                        the same street forming an interior angle of less than one hundred thirty
                        (130) degrees within the lot lines. For the purposes of this definition,
                        drivewayeasements or drivewaytracts do not qualify as streets in urban
                        areas.

Lot, flag               "Flag lot" means a lot or parcel that has access to a road, street or easement
                        by means ofa narrow strip of lot or easement.

Normal water year         "Nonnal water year" means a twelve (12) month period (October 1st -
                          September 30th) with average precipitation based upon data from the past
                          fifty (50) years.
NPDES                     "NPDES" means the National Pollutant Discharge Elimination System.
Nuisance                  "Nuisance"shall includethose definitionscontainedin Chapters 7.48 and
                          9.66 RCW. Any violation ofthis title shall constitute a nuisance, per se.
Nuisancevegetation        "Nuisance vegetation" means noxious weeds such as tansy ragwort, purple
                          loosestrife, poison hemlock, Eurasian milfoil, nonnative blackberries, or
                          other plants listed as noxious by the county pursuant to Section 7. 14. 070 or
                          any plant which when established is highly destructive, competitive or
                          difficult to control by manual, mechanical or chemical practices.
                              (Amended: Ord. 2006-05-01)

Nursinghome or            "Nursing home" or "convalescent home" means a facility licensed by the
convalescent home         state of Washington providing twenty-four (24) hour supervised nursing
                          care, personal care, therapy, nutritionmanagement, organizedactivities,
                          social services, room, board, and laundry for persons requiring regular
                          medical attention by reason of chronic illness or infirmity, but excluding
                          surgical or emergency medical services. This definition excludes hospitals
                          and sanitariums.
                              (Amended: Ord. 2010-08-06)

Obligateplants            "Obligateplants" mean plants that almost always (ninety-nine percent
                          (99%) probability) occur in wetlands under natural conditions. Such
                          groupings are more fully defined in the Wetlands Delineation Manual.
Official controls         "Official controls" means legislatively defined and enacted policies,
                          standards, precise detailed maps, and other criteria, all of which control the
                          physical development of the county, and are the means of translating into
                          regulations and ordinances all or any part of the general objectives of the
                          comprehensive plan. Such official controls may include, but are not limited
                          to, ordinances establishingzoning, subdivision control, platting, and
                          adoption of detailed maps.
Open space/commons        "Open space/commons, " when referring to density transfer, means an area
                          left open and undeveloped, for use by the public, but is not a formal park 4
                           or recreation area managed or owned by the county. The land shall be
                           identified as a separate tract and shall be managed and maintained in
                           perpetuity for the intendeduse ofthe homeownersofthe relevant
                           development.
Open space, usable         "Usable open space"means an open areathat is not covered in impervious
                           surface and that exceeds four hundred (400) square feet with all
                           dimensions a minimum of twenty (20) feet.
Open water                 "Open water, " when referring to wetlands and when not specifically
                           definedby the wetlandrating criteria, means a proportion of open water to
                           vegetative cover equal to twenty-five percent (25%) to seventy-five
                           percent (75%) ofthe total wetlandareaduring a majority ofa normal water
                           year.

Operating level            "Operatinglevel, " whenreferringto transportationfacilities, meansthe
                           performance of a transportation corridor or intersection of regional
                           significance, pursuant to Section 40. 350. 020(F).
Ordinance                  "Ordinance" means the ordinance, resolution, or other procedure used by
                           the county to adopt regulatory requirements.
Ordinary high water mark   "Ordinary high water mark" means the point on the sides of streams or
                           lakes which is historically or normally at water's edge, as identified by a
                           visible change in vegetation and/or soil.
Owner                      "Owner"means any personhavingproperty rights as a fee owneror
                           contract purchaser, or one duly authorized by the power of attorney to
                           representthe owner. For the purposes ofthis title, in terms ofviolations
                           and binding agreements between the county and the owner, "owner" shall
                           also mean a leaseholder, tenant, or person in possession or control of the
                           premises or property at the time ofagreement, violations ofagreement, or
                           the provisions ofthis title. For the purpose ofprocessingan applicationfor
                           a land use approval or permit under this title, where such application or
                           permit must be filed by an owner, the tenn "owner" also includes a
                           governmental entity contemplating acquisitionofa parcel for a use which
                           would require such permit or approval. Where property is proposed to be
                           developedpursuantto an easementright, "owner"meansthe person whois
                           the holder of the easement.

Park, community            "Community park" means a public park that provides a focal point and
                           gathering place for broad groups of users. Community parks are used by all
                           segments ofthe populationandgenerally serve residents from a one (1) to
                           three (3) mile service area. Community parks often include recreation
                           facilities for organized activities such as sports fields, skate parks, and play
                           courts.
                               (Added: Ord. 2016-06-12)

Park, neighborhood         "Neighborhood park" means a public park designed to provide
                           nonorganizedrecreational opportunities for residents living within a one-
                           half (1/2) mile radius, and are located such that they are within walking
                           and bicycling distance of most users. These parks generally contain three
                           (3) to five (5) acres but may vary in size dependingupon unique site
                           characteristics, opportunities and land availability
                               (Added: Ord. 2016-06-12)

Park, regional             "Regional park" means a public recreational area that serves residents
                           throughout dark County, as well as outside the county. Facilities may
                            include sports fields, extensive trail systems, or large picnic areas. Because
                            of their large size and broad service area, regional parks typically require
                            more support facilities, such as parking and restrooms. These parks are
                            designed to accommodate large numbers of people.
                                (Added: Ord. 2016-06-12)

Party of record             "Party of record" means all persons, agencies or organizations who have
                            submitted written comments in response to a notice of application, made
                            oral comments in a fonnal public hearing conducted on the application, or
                            requested in writing to be a "party of record. " In the case of Type I
                            decisions, the party of record shall include the applicant and any person
                            who files a written request prior to the issuance of the decision. In the case
                            of Type II decisions, the party of record shall include the applicant, and
                            any person submitting to the responsible official written testimony, or a
                            writtenrequest to be a "party ofrecord," that is specificto a particular
                            applicationprior to the issuanceofthe decision. In the case ofType III
                            decisions, the party of record shall include the applicant and persons
                            submitting written testimony before, during, or prior to the close of a
                            public hearing; providing oral testimony at a public hearing; signing the
                            sign-in sheet noting the person's name, address and the subject matter in
                            whichthey are interested; or by submitting a written request to the
                            responsible official to be a "party of record" that is specific to a particular
                            application prior to the close of the subject public hearing.
                            Notwithstanding any of the foregoing, no person shall be a party of record
                            who has not furnished an accurate post office mailing address.

                           of Washington providing twenty-four (24) hour care for six (6) or fewer

                           children or expectant mothers. It may or may not be a family residence.

Start of construction      "Start ofconstruction" includes substantial improvement, andmeans the
                           date the building pennit was issued; provided, the actual start of
                           construction, repair, reconstruction, placement or other improvement was
                           within one hundred eighty (180) days of the permit date. The "actual start"
                           means either the first placement of permanent construction of a structure
                           on a site, such as the pouring of slab or footings, the installation of piles,
                           the construction of columns, or any work beyond the stage of excavation; 5
                            or the placement of a manufactured home on a foundation. Permanent
                            construction does not include land preparation, such as clearing, grading
                            and filling; nor does it include the installation of streets and/or walkways;
                            nor does it include excavation for a basement, footings, piers, or
                            foundation or other erection of temporary forms; nor does it include the
                            installationonthe property ofaccessorybuildings,suchas garagesor
                            sheds not occupied as dwelling units or not part of the main structure.

Story                       "Story," whenreferring to structures, means that portion of a building
                            included between the upper surface of any floor and the upper surface of
                            the floor next above, except that the topmost story shall be that portion of a
                            building included between the upper surface of the topmost floor and the
                            ceiling or roof above. If the finished floor level directly above a basement
                            or unusedunder-floorspaceis more than six (6) feet abovegradefor more
                            than fifty percent (50%) of the total perimeter or is more than twelve (12)
                            feet above grade at any point, such basement or unused under-floor space
                            shall be considered as a story.
Stream or streams           "Stream" or "streams" means those areas where surface waters flow
                            sufficiently to produce a defined channel or bed. A defined channel or bed
                            is indicated by hydraulically sorted sediments or the removal of vegetative
                            litter or loosely rooted vegetation by the action of moving water. The
                            channel or bedneednot containwater year round. This definition is not
                            meant to include irrigation ditches, canals, stormwater runoff devices or
                            other entirely artificial watercourses unless they are usedto convey
                            streams naturally occurring prior to construction. Those topographic
                            features that resemble streams but have no defined channels (i. e., swales)
                            shall be considered streams whenhydrologic and hydraulic analyses done
                            pursuant to a development proposal predict formation of a defined channel
                            after development.
Stream bank stabilization   "Stream bank stabilization" means those approved bioengineered projects.
                            The projects can include both passive and active types of methods for
                            stabilizing the stream bank.

                            leader that can be used as a landscape element for buffering/screening
                            along front, side, and rear setbacks. Unique site features shall determine
                            the most appropriate streettree to use. A street tree shall be broadbranched
                            and characteristically have lower branches that are able to be removed to
                            meet clearance standards.

5      1. OriginalMaps. The designations,locations andboundariesofthe districts set forth in Chapter

 1   notations, references, data and other information shown thereon shall be and are hereby adopted and
 2   made a part ofthis title. The signed copies of the zoning maps containing the zoning districts
 3   designated at the time of adoption of ordinance codified in this title shall be maintained without
 4   change on file in the office of the County Auditor. Any land or property not specifically identified
 5   with a zone designation shall be considered to be zoned as is the most restrictive zone classification
 6   designated on adjoining and/or abutting properties, until such time as it is determined otherwise by a
 7   rezone action.

 8   2.   Revised Maps. Council may, from time to time, direct the County Auditor to replace the official
 9   zoningmaps, or portions thereof, witha map or maps, or portions thereof, whichinclude all lawful
10   changes of zone to date. Such maps, or portions thereof, filed as replacements, shall bear dated,
11   authenticatingsignaturesofCouncil and County Auditor. Any maps or portions thereofthereby
12   replaced shall be retained in a separate file by the County Auditor. Any revisions or replacements of
13   said maps, when duly entered, signed, and filed with the County Auditor as authorized by this
14   chapter, are part of this title.

15   3. Copies of Maps. The responsible official shall maintain up-to-date copies of the zoning maps.
16   The responsible official shall cause the copies of the zoning maps to be revised so that they
17   accurately portray changes of zone boundaries.
