 2   40.200.040 Minimum and Maximum Calculations
 3   A. General Rule.

 4        1. When determining maximum standards for planning related calculations (including density,
 5        parking setbacks, etc. ), the final number in any calculation shall be rounded down to the nearest
 6        whole unit. However, if two (2) or more amounts must be added to figure a total, applicants shall use
 7        numbers accurate to two (2) decimal places (hundredths) when adding the amounts and round off
 8        only the total. For example, if the density for a project were determined to be fifteen and eighty-nine-
 9        hundredths (15. 89) units, only fifteen (15) units would be permitted.

10        2. When determining minimum standards for planning related calculations (including density,
11        parking setbacks, etc. ), the final number in any calculation shall be rounded up to the nearest whole
12        unit. However, if two (2) or more amounts must be added to figure a total, applicants shall use
13        numbers accurate to two (2) decimal places (hundredths) when adding the amounts and round off
14        only the total. For example, if the required parking for a project were determined to be fifteen and
15        eighty-nine-hundredths (15. 89) spaces, atotal of sixteen (16) spaces would be required.

16        3. These standards shall not apply for the purposes of engineering related calculations (surveying
17        information, stormwater calculations, road construction infonnation, etc. ).

18   B.   Density Calculations.

19        1. For all urban zoning districts with minimum densities except Rl-5, Rl-6, Rl-7. 5, Rl-10 and Rl-
20        20, the following shall apply:

21            a. Minimum density or floor area ratio will be based on the developable area of the lot that
22            remains after subtracting:

23               (1) Land devoted to public right-of-way or private street easements indu din allc s, public
24               parks and trails, required landscapin includin re uired landsca in within setbacks and
25               drainageways;

26               (2) Land designated by covenant or public dedication to be permanently maintained in an
27               undeveloped statebecausethe land is identifiedas sensitive due to the presenceofsteep
28               slopes, unstable land, historical or archaeological sites, wetlands and buffers, or other
29               permanent physical development limitations as may be determined by the responsible
30               official. All other lands shall be considered in the calculation of minimum density including
31               required setbacks not counted towards re uircd landsca in private rrcrvation or
32               common ureas or ameni          areas under Section 40.260. 150.

33            b. Maximum density or floor area ratio shall be calculated based upon the gross area of the site
34            lot, excluding public right-of-way or private street easements includin allr

35        2. For the Rl-5, Rl-6, Rl-7.5, Rl-10 andRl-20 zoning districts, minimum and maximum densities
36        shall be calculated pursuant to the standards in Chapter 40.220.

37   (Amended: Ord. 2009-03-02)
38   C.   Lot Area Calculations.

39        1. Lot area is the computed area contained within the lot lines.

40            a. In the urbanarea, except for the UH zones, lot areaexcludes street andalley rights-of-way,
41            street easements, and street tracts.
                                                                                                                   62
42            b. In the Urban Reserve (UR-10 and UR-20), Urban Holding (UH-10 and UH-20), Rural (R-5,
 1       R-10 and R-20), Rural Center (RC-1 and RC-2. 5), agricultural (AG-20 and AG-WL) and forest
 2       resource (FR-40 andFR-80) districts, lot areaincludes on-site road easements, and one-half(l/2)
 3       the width, or thirty (30) feet, whichever is less, of abutting public rights-of-way for perimeter
 4       streets, excluding limited access state or interstate highways.

         c. Driveways are included in lot area in all zones.
 6   2. One (1) lot within a proposed subdivision, short plat or exempt division shall be considered in
 7   compliance with the minimum lot area requirements if it is within ten percent (10%) of the required
 8   lot area for the zone. To utilize this provision in the Rl-5 and Rl-6 zones, one lot may be excluded
 9   from the average minimum lot calculations and the ten percent (10%) lot areareduction may be
10   applied to the excluded lot. The provisions of this section shall not apply to developments utilizing
11   the following:

12       a. Density transfer (Section 40. 220.010(C)(5));

13       b. Rural cluster (Section 40. 210. 020).

 3   40.200. 060 Exceptions to Height Limitations.
 4   A. Agricultural structures, water towers and tanks shall not exceed thirty-five (35) feet
 5   in height in all zones, unless they are located at least fifty (50) feet from all property
 6   lines.

 7   B. No height limit shall apply in any zone to chimneys, church spires, belfries, cupolas,
 8   domes, smoke-stacks, flagpoles, grain elevators, cooling towers, solar energy systems,
 9   monuments, fire house towers, masts, aerials, elevator shafts, and outdoor theater screens.

10   C. Subject to Section 40. 310. 010, signs are prohibited above thirty-five (35) feet in
11   height; provided, that the name of the theater may be allowed on outdoor theater screens
12   higher than thirty-five (35) feet.

 3   40.200.070 Exceptions to SetbackRequirements.
 4   A. Projections Into Required Setbacks. (Also see Sections 40. 200. 070(A)(7), 40. 200. 070(A)(8), and
 5   40.320.010(C)(9) for limitations.)
 6   Certain architectural features and structures may project into required setbacks, subject to applicable
 7   building and fire codes as follows:

 8       1.   Front Setback.

 9            a. Fire escapes, porches, balconies, decks, landing places, or outside stairways over thirty (30)
10            inches in heightmay project not more than six (6) feet into the requiredfront setback, provided
11            such features are open-sidedand are uncovered.

12            b. Projections including bay windows, overhangingbreakfastnooks, cornices, canopies, eaves,
13            belt courses, sills or other similar architectural features and fireplaces may extend up to two (2)
14            feet into the required setback.

15            c. Porches, decks, landings or stairways not more than thirty (30) inches in height above
16            finished grade are allowed to within eighteen (18) inches of the front property line, provided
17            such features are open-sidedand uncovered.

18       2.   Rear Setback.

19            a. Projections such as bay windows and overhanging breakfast nooks may extend up to two (2)
20            feet into the required setback, provided such features are limited to ten (10) horizontal feet per
21            projection and limited to thirty percent (30%) of the linear dimension of the rear building
22            elevation.

23            b. Cornices, canopies,eaves, belt courses, sills or other similar architecturalfeatures and fire-
24            places may extend up to two (2) feet into the required setback.

25            c. Porches, decks, landings or stairways not more than thirty (30) inches in height above
26            finished grade are allowed to within eighteen (18) inches of the rear property line, provided such
27            features are open-sided and uncovered.

28       3.   Side Setback.

29            a. The following features and structures may project into the required side setback not more
30            than two (2) feet; provided, that no portion of the structure may be less than three (3) feet to the
31            property line:

32                 (1) In the Rl-5 and Rl-6 zones only, projections such as bay windows and overhanging
33                 breakfast nooks may extend up to two (2) feet into the required setback, provided such
34                 features are limited to ten (10) horizontal feet per projection and limited to thirty percent
35                 (30%) of the linear dimension of the side building elevation;

36                 (2) Cornices, canopies, eaves, belt courses, sills or other similar architectural features;
37                 and

38                 (3) Fireplaces.


                                                                                                                   65
 1           b. Porches, decks, landingsor stairwaysnot more thanthirty (30) inches in heightabove
 2           finished grade are allowed to within eighteen (18) inches of the side property line, provided such
 3           features are open-sidedanduncovered.
 4       4. Garden Sheds, Gazebos and Playhouses. One (1) garden shed, or one (1) gazebo or one (1)
 5       playhouse meeting all of the following requirements may be located in either the side or rear setback
 6       ofsingle-family residential districts (Rl-5, Rl-6, Rl-7. 5, Rl-10 and Rl-20):
 7           a. The structure contains no more than two hundred (200) square feet of floor area, with
 8           overhangs that do not exceed sixteen (16) inches;

 9           b. The structure is set back from property lines a minimum of two (2) feet;

10           c. The floor elevation is eighteen (18) inches or less in height;

11           d. The structure is less than twelve (12) feet in height;

12           e. Roofdrainageis containedon site; and
13           f.   No utilities are connected to the structure.

14           g. If the structure is located within a utility easement, the property owner must obtain a waiver
15           letter from all applicable utilities.

16       5. Aboveground rainwater cisterns in all urban residential districts are allowed reduced setbacks
17       subject to the following:

18           a. Cisterns six (6) feet in height or less are allowed a setback of three (3) feet to an interior side
19           or rear property line;

20           b. Other reductions from standard setbacks shall be limited to the height ofthe cistern. For
21           example, a cistern eight(8) feet highis allowed a setbackofno lessthaneight(8) feet to a rear
22           property line in the Rl-20 zone; provided, thatthe maximum height ofany cisternunderthis
23           provision shall not exceed twelve (12) feet;

24           c. If the cistern is located within a utility easement, the property owner must obtain a waiver
25           letter from all applicableutilities;

26           d. Appropriate designandsupportto ensurethe cistern doesnot affect on-site or off-site
27           foundations, retaining walls, or other structures are required;

28           e. All other applicable building andplumbing codes shall apply.

29   6. Aboveground utilities.

              The allowed projections noted above may not apply to landscape buffers. (See Section                         66

 1       89. The allowed projections noted above apply to building and development envelopes except
 2       when limited by the following:

 3            a. The applicable envelope line reflects a critical area or an easement;

 4            b. The applicable envelope line reflects a landscape buffer, except as allowed by Section
 5            40.320.010(C)(9);and

 6            c. Information on a site plan or plat specifically prohibits such projections.

 7       ^ J^). Fences and retaining walls, except as specified in Section 40. 320. 010(F) may be located in
 8       any setback.

 9   B. Exceptions to Front Setback Requirements.

10       1. If there are dwellings on both abutting lots with front setbacks less than the required depth for
11       the district, the front setback for the lot need not exceedthe average front setback ofthe abutting
12       dwellings.

13       2. If there is a dwelling on one (1) abutting lot with a front setback less than the required depth for
14       the district, the front setbackneed not exceed a depth ofhalfwaybetweenthe depth ofthe front
15       setback on the abutting lot and the required front setback depth.

16       3. In residential zoning districts, where a temporary turnaround easement constitutes all or part of
17       the front lot line, front setbacks shall be a minimum often (10) feet from the temporary easement for
18       the dwelling and fifteen (15) feet for the associated garage. Front setbacks from access and right-of-
19       way easements intendedto be permanent shall continue to be as stipulated by the applicable zoning
20       district standard.

21       4. Setbacks from both streets on through lots shall be considered front setbacks. However, if one
22       (1) of the streets has restricted access,the setback from the restricted access street shall be considered
23       a rear setback. Access is considered restricted to a street if any of the conditions below exist:

24            a. A plat containsa note prohibitingaccessto one (1) ofthe abuttingstreets;
25           b. The road is clearly determined to be a restricted access road, such as but not limited to 1-5, I-
26           205, SR-14, SR-500, and PaddenExpressway;

27           c. A covenant permanently restricting access to one (1) of the abutting streets (with the county
28           being a party to the recorded document) is recorded individually for a through lot; or

29           d. Topographic constraints exist that prohibit access to the rear street based upon the
30           responsible official's judgment.

 1   EXHIBIT10 Do not apply Exhibit 10 per October 13, 2026, County Council decision.
 2
 3   40.210.010. FOREST,AGRICULTUREAND AGRICULTURAL-WILDLIFEDISTRICTS(FR-80,
 4   FR-40, AG-20, AG-WL).
 5           A.    Purpose.
 6                 1. Forest 80 District. The purpose of the Forest 80 district is to maintain and enhance
 7                      resource-based industries, encourage the conservation of productive forest lands and
 8                      discourage incompatible uses consistent with the Forest I policies of the
 9                      comprehensive plan. The Forest 80 district applies to lands which have been
10                      designated as Forest Tier 1 on the comprehensive plan. Nothing in this chapter shall be
11                      construed in a manner inconsistent with the Washington Forest Practices Act.
12                 2.   Forest 40 District. The purpose of the Forest 40 district is to encourage the
13                      conservation of lands which have the physical characteristics that are capable of
14                      management for the long-termproductionofcoiiunercially significantforestproducts
15                      and other natural resources, such as minerals.
16                3.    Agriculture 20 District. The purpose of the Agriculture 20 district is to encourage the
17                      conservation oflandswhichhavethe growingcapacity,productivity, soil composition,
18                      and surrounding land use to have long-term commercial significance for agriculture
19                      and associatedresource production.
20                4.    Agricultural-Wildlife. Thepuqiose ofthe AG-WLdistrict is to encouragethe
21                      preservation of agricultural and wildlife use on land which is suited for agricultural
22                      production, and to protect agricultural areas that are highly valuable seasonal wildlife
23                      habitat from incompatible uses. The district provides for activities which can be
24                      considered accessory only to agricultural, game, or wildlife habitat management, or
25                      recreational uses. Nothing m this chapter shall be constmed to restrict normal
26                      agricultural practices.
27           B.   Uses.
28                The uses set out in Table 40. 210. 010-1 are examples of uses allowable in the various
29                resource zone districts. The appropriate review authority is mandatory.
30
31                 .   "P" - Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.
32                     "R/A" - Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40. 520. 020.
33                     "C" - Conditional uses whichmay be permitted subject to the approval of a
34                     conditional use pennit as set forth in Section 40. 520. 030.
35                     "X" - Uses specificallyprohibited.
36                 Where there are special use standards or restrictions for a listed use, the applicable code
37                 section(s) in Chapter40.260, SpecialUses and Standards,or other applicablechapteris
38                 noted in the "Special Standards"column.

 1   3.   Previous Land Divisions.
 2        a.   Withinthe FR-80, PR-40 andAG-20 districts, until the affected property is included
 3             within an urban growth boundary, no remainder lot of a previously approved
 4             agriculture or forest district "cluster"land divisionor lot reconfigurationshall be:
 5              (1) Further subdivided or reduced in size below seventy percent (70%) of the total
 6                  developable area of the original parent parcel constituting the cluster
 7                   subdivision; or
 8             (2) Reducedby a total ofmore than one (1) acre.
 9        b.   Applications for reduction in remainderlot size consistent withthis provisionshall be
10             processed as a plat alteration pursuant to Section 40. 540. 120.
11        c.   Exceptions to subsections (C)(3)(a) and (C)(3)(b) of this section. A remainder lot with
12             an existing residence may be short platted further to contain the residence on its own
13             lot, subject to the following:
14             (1) Process. Creationofthe new lot is subjectto the requirements of Section
15                  40. 540. 030.
16             (2) Lot Size.The newlot shall be sizedto requirethe minimum reductionin the
17                  remainder lot, but still meet minimum requirements of this section and for on-site
18                  sewage disposal as required by dark County Public Health.
19             (3) The new lot may not include critical areasunless no other alternative exists. If no
20                  alternative is available, encroachment into these areas shall be limited to the least
21                  amount possible consistent with applicable critical areas ordinances.
22             (4) A building envelope containingthe existingresidenceand accessorybuildings
23                  shall be established within the new lot, subject to the following:
24                  (a) A minimum one hundred (100) foot setback between the envelope and the
25                        remainder parcel is maintained, unless it can be shown that a lesser setback
26                       with existing or proposed landscaping or existing vegetation will provide
27                       the same or greater buffering. In no case shall a setback less than fifty (50)
28                        feet be approved.
29                  (b) A minimum twenty (20) foot setbackbetweenthe envelope and other
30                       cluster lots is maintained.
31             (5) A note shall be placed on the plat stating the following:
32                 The residential property is adjacent to agricultural or forest lands
33                 on which a variety ofresource-related activities may occur that are not
34                  compatible withresidential development. Potential discomforts or inconvenience
35                  may include, but are not limited to: noise, odors, fumes, dust, smoke, insects,
36                  operation ofmachinery (includingaircraft) duringanytwenty-four(24) hour
37                  period, storage and disposal of manure, and the application by spraying or
38                  otherwise of chemical fertilizers, soil amendments, herbicides and pesticides.
39             (6) An open space, farm or forest managementplanis requiredfor the remainder
40                 parcel, whichshallprohibitadditionalresidential development. The plan shall be
41                  submitted and approved with the preliminary application. The plan shall identify
42                  penmitted uses and management of the parcel so that it maintains its open space
43                  or other designated functions and provides for the protection of all critical areas.
44                  The management plan shall identify the responsibility for maintaining the
45                  remainder parcel. The plan shall also include any construction activities (trails,
46                  fencing, agricultural buildings) and vegetation clearing that may occur on site.
47                  All subsequent activities must be conducted in conformance with the approved
48                  managementplan. Managementplans may bemodifiedthrougha Type II
49               process. A note shall be placed on the plat and a restrictive covenant shall be
50               recorded that clearly states that only the above uses are permitted on the
51               remainder parcel. The note and covenant shall also incorporate the management
52               plan, as described above.
53   4.   Nonconforming lots may be reconfigured pursuant to Section 40. 2 10. 010(D).
54   5.   Outdoorlights, except for seasonalholidaylights, must be directedor shieldedto               75
      1                      minimize glare visible from streets, nearby dwellings, and above the fixtures.
      2       D. NonconformingLots - Lot ReconfigurationStandards.
      3          1. Purpose. It is in the public interest to encouragethe protection ofsensitive lands, expand
      4                 the amount of commercially viable resource land under single ownership, reduce the
      5                 amount of road and utility construction and, withinthe FR-80, FR-40 andAG-20 districts,
      6                 to protect and buffer designated resource lands.
      7            2.   Lot Reconfiguration. Except for previously approved agricultural or forest zoned clusters or
      8                 rural residential planned unit developments, these substandard lots may be modified where
      9                 consistent with the following criteria. Parcels whichmeet all ofthe following criteria are
     10                 eligible for reconfiguration and reduction in size subject to a Type II review:
     11                 a. Existing parcel(s) is:
     12                        (1) Smallerthanthe minimum lot size establishedfor new lots in the applicable
   13                            zoning district. Parcels which meet the minimum lot size
14  may be adjusted as a part of this process, but may not be decreased below the established minimum lot size.
15                             (2) Determinedto be legally created, andbereasonablybuildable. Withinthe FR-80,FR-
16                                   40 and AG-20 districts, this section authorizes lot reconfiguration only where existing
17                                   divisions are determined to have a reasonable probability of developing. For the
18                                   purposes ofthis section the review authority shall determine whetherthe existing lots
19                                   are reasonably buildable by considering the following: road access, septic suitability,
20                                   topography, costs of providing infrastructure and the presence of sensitive land.
21                      b.     Proposedparcel(s) results in the following:
22                           (1) No additionalparcels;
23                           (2) Have septic suitability approval;
24                           (3) Have adequatepotable waterat the time ofoccupancy, subject to Section40.370.020;
25                           (4) Eachresulting legal nonconformingparcel shall be at least one (1) acre in size with a
26                                minimum width of at least one hundred forty
27                                 (140) feet; and
28                           (5) In addition, within the FR-80, FR-40 and AG-20 districts:
29                                 (a) The locationofthe resulting reconfiguredlots shall havethe least impacton
30                                       sensitive and resource lands;
31                                 (b) Accessto reconfiguredlots shallmeetthe minimum standardsnecessaryto obtain
32                                       a building pennit;
33                                 (c) The remainderlot shall not be further subdividedor reducedin sizeunlessthe
34                                       affected property is included within an urban growth boundary;
35                                 (d) Reconfiguredlots shall not be further adjustedby boundaryline adjustment
36                                       without approval under this section.
37                      c.      Reconfigured lots shall result in achieving one (1) or more of the identified public interest
38                              issues in Section 40. 210. 010(D)(1).
39                 3.   Lot Requirements. The setback, dimensional, use andheight standardsfor these lots shallbe as
40                      establishedfor the Rural-5 (R-5) district except that reductions in side andrear setbacks shall be
41                      granted where necessary to pennit construction of a dwelling on the parcel; providing, when the
42                      parcel is abutting, or surrounded by, property zoned for resource uses, the minimum setback
43                      from those property lines shall be fifty (50) feet for all structures.
44                 4.   Thereviewauthoritymay impose conditions onthe lot reconfigurationtofurtherthe purposes of
45                      this section.
46                 5.   Lot reconfigurations shall be finalized upon the filing of a record of survey or covenant.
47           E.    Land Divisions in the AG-20 and FR-40 Zones.
48                 1.   Applicability.
49                      a.      The provisions of this subsection shall apply to all land divisions in the AG-20 and FR-40
50                              zoning districts after July 1, 2016.
51                      b.      Available options for land divisionare authorized:
52                              (^ Pursuantto Chapter40.540;WT
53                              (S)-Pursuant to Chapter 10. 5'K) und b\ uning the cluiiU'r pro\'i;<ionii in Section    yg

 1         c.       In the AG-20 zoning district:
 2                   (1) Land divisions that result in parcels twenty (20) acres(or lots capableofbeing
 3                              described as one-thirty-second (V^) of a section) in size or larger are allowed under
 4                              the exemption provisions of Section 40. 540. 020(B)(4)(b).
 5                   (2) Land divisionsthat result in parcels lessthantwenty (20) acres in sizemust be
 6                              platted and meet the additional requirements of this chapter.
 7         d.       In the FR-40 zoningdistrict, land divisionsthat result in parcels less thanforty (40) acres

 9         e.   Previously approved cluster or lot reconfiguration remainder lots are not eligible to use the
10              provisions of this section.

 2   40.220.010 LOW-DENSITYResidentialDistricts (Rl-20, Rl-10, Rl-7.5, Rl-6 and Rl-5)
 3   A. Purpose.

          1.   The Rl-20, Rl-10, Rl-7. 5, Rl-6 and Rl-5 districts are intended to:

 5              a. Provide for a mix of single-family and middle housing types in a low-density context at a
 6              neighborhoodscale.

                b. Establish higher densities where a full range of community services and facilities are present or will
                be present at the time of development.
 9              c. Provide for additional related uses such as schools, parks and utility uses necessary to serve immediate
10             residential areas.

11   B.   Uses.

12     The uses set out in Table 40. 220. 010-1 are examples of uses allowable in single-family residential zone districts.
13   The appropriate review authority is mandatory.

14             "P" Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.

15             "R/A" Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40. 520. 020.

16             "C" Conditional uses which may be permitted subject to the approval of a conditional use permit as set
17             forth in Section 40. 520. 030.

18             "X" Uses specifically prohibited.

19   Where there are special use standards or restrictions for a listed use, the applicable code section(s) in Chapter
20   40. 260, Special Uses and Standards, or other applicable chapter is noted in the "Special Standards" column.

 9   C. Development Standards.

10      1. New lots and structures and additions to structures subject to this chapter shall comply with the applicable
11      standards for lots, building height and setbacks in Tables 40. 220. 010-2 and 40. 220. 010-3, subject to the
12      provisions ofChapter40.200 and Section40.550.020, exceptthat single-family attached(townhouse) lots and
13      structures shall comply with the applicable standards in Table 40. 220. 010-4.

14      2. An exceptionto the maximum averagelot sizemay be granted for a shortplat creatinglot(s) for an
15      existing legal residence(s) and one (1) remainder lot subject to the following:

16           a. For a two (2) lot short plat with one (1) existing residence, neither the lot with the residence nor the
17           remainder lot must meet the maximum average lot area.

18          b. Whenthree (3) or more lots are created, only those lots with existing residences are exempted from
19          maximum lot area average calculations.

20           c. Theresultingplat shall containa plat note specifyingthat this exceptionmay not beusedfor any
21          further divisions of the subject lots.

22      3. Lots created for drainage facilities, parks, open space, wetlands and buffers or utilities shall not be subject
23      to maximum lot size requirements.

 6   4. Density Transfer for Single-FamilyDetachedDwellings.

 7       a. Purpose. To achievethe density goals ofthe comprehensive plan with respect to the urban area, while
 8       preserving environmentally sensitive lands and the livability ofthe low-densityresidential neighborhoods,
 9       while also maintainingcompatibility with existing residences.

10       b. The density for property developed in low-density zone districts for single-family detacheddwellings,
11       if encumbered by land identified as sensitive due to the presence of steep slopes, unstable land, historical
12       or archaeological sites, wetlands and buffers, regional stormwater facilities, or other permanent physical
13       development limitations as may be determined by the responsible official or land voluntarily set aside for
14       open space or commons as approved by the responsible official, from the gross acreage may be transferred
15       to the remaining unencumbered land areas on the same development site, subject to the following
16       limitations:

17          (1) Easements established for utility transmission lines such as Bonneville Power Administration
18          (BPA), PacifiCorp a. k. a. Pacific Power (fonnally known as PP&L), dark Public Utilities, and NW
19          Natural can not be utilized for density transfer.

20          (2) Maximum Number. The maximum number of units that canbe achieved on the site is based on
21          the density in Table 40. 220. 010-5, multiplied by the gross acreage of the entire site, both encumbered
22          and unencumbered, without deducting for road easements or right-of-way. The potential maximum
23          number of lots will be dependent upon site characteristics and the lot requirements of Table
24          40.220.010-5.

25          (3) The minimum lot depth of any lot abutting environmentally sensitive lands shall be fifty-five (55)
26          feet.

27          (4) For parent parcels larger thantwo and one-half(2. 5) acres:

28                (a) The resulting lots which abut Rl -5, Rl-6, Rl-7. 5, Rl-10 or Rl-20 zoned lots or parcels
29                shall:

30                      (i)   Be at least ninety percent (90%) of the minimum lot area standard for the subject parcel;

31                      (ii) Have a lot depth of not less than eighty percent (80%) of the minimum lot depth of the
32                      subject parcel;

33                      (iii) Have a minimum lot width not less than ten (10) feet from the minimum lot width of
34                      the subject parcel.

35                (b) The resulting lots which are interior (not a part ofthe parent parcel abutting an adjacent
36                property line) to the site shall conform to the lot requirements set out in Table 40. 220. 010-4.

37          (5) For parent parcels two and one-half(2. 5) acres or less, all lots, both exterior and interior, to be
38          created shall conform to the lot requirements in Table 40.220.010-5.




                                                                                                                 90
 1               (6) This density transfer development provision may not be used in association with the planned unit
 2               development provisions of Section 40. 520. 080, the compact lot development provisions of Section
 3               40. 260. 072, or the cottage housing provisions of Section 40. 260. 073.

 4               (7) A recorded covenant shall be placed on those areas or tracts from which density is transferred
 5               prohibiting any development of the parcel or tract inconsistent with its intended use.
 6               (8) Subdivisionlots shall be subjectto the maximum averagelot areaprovisions ofthe underlying
 7               zone.

 2   40. 220. 020     Residential and Office Residential Districts (R, OR)
 3   A. Purpose.

 4          1. The residential (R 12. R 18, R 22, R 30 and R 43 R-24 R-36 R-50 districts are intended to provide for
 5          medium andhigherdensityresidential development baseduponconsistency withthe comprehensiveplan and
 6          compatibility with surroundinglanduses. The following factors will be consideredin the applicationofone (1)
 7          ofthese districts to a particular site:

 8              a. Properties designated urban medium density residential on the comprehensive plan should not exceed
 9              u density of R 22 be zoned for R-24 or R-36 districts. Urban high density residential areas are
10              appropriate for densities in the R 30 and R 43 districts the R-50 district.

11              b. Proximity to major streets and the available capacity of these streets, adequacy of public water and
12              sewer, vehicular and pedestrian traffic circulation m the area, proximity to commercial services and
13              proximityto public openspace andrecreationopportunities. Developmentwithinthese districts will be
14              reviewed to ensure compatibility with adjacent uses including such considerations as privacy, noise,
15              lighting and design.

16          2. The office residential (OR 15, OK 18, OR 22, OR 30, OR 13 OR-24 OR-J6 OR-50) districts are
17          intendedto provide for residential andprofessional office development basedupon consistencywith the
18         comprehensive plan and compatibility with adjacent land uses. It is intended that office developments within
19         these districts will be of a higher standard in recognition of their residential setting. The following factors will
20         be considered in the application of one (1) of these districts to a particular site:

21              a.   Pro erties desi nated urban medium densi          residential on the cum rchensive lan should be
22              zoned for OR-24 or OR-36 districts, llrhan hi h densi          residential areas arc a     r« riate for the
23              OR-50 district.

24              a b. Proximity to major streets and the available capacity of these streets;

25              fe c. Availability of public water and sewer;

26              e 4. Vehicularandpedestriantraffic circulationin the area;
27              d ^. Proximity to commercial services; and

28              e f. Proximity to public open space and recreation opportunities.

29        Developmentwithinthese districtswill be reviewedto ensure compatibility withadjacentuses, includingsuch
30   considerations as privacy, noise, lighting and design.

31   B.     Uses.

32      The uses set out in Table 40. 220. 020-1 are examples of uses allowable in residential and office residential zone
33   districts. The appropriate review authority is mandatory.

34             "P" Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.

35             "R/A" Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40. 520. 020.

     EXHIBIT 13



 2   40.230.010 Commercial Districts (NC, CC, GC)
 3   A. Purpose.

 4          1. NeighborhoodCommercial (NC) District. These commercial areas of limited size are intendedto provide
 5          for the convenience shopping needs of the immediate neighborhood. This district is pennitted under the
 6          neighborhoodcommercial and mixed use comprehensive plan designations.

 7          2. Community Commercial (CC) District. These commercial areas are intended to provide for the regular
 8          shopping and service needs for several adjacent neighborhoods. This district is only permitted in areas
 9          designated as community commercial or mixed use on the comprehensive plan.

10          3. General Commercial (GC) District. These commercial areas provide a full range ofgoods and services
11          necessary to serve large areas of the county and the traveling public. This district is limited to the general
12          commercial comprehensive plan designation.

13         (Amended: Ord. 2008-12-15;Ord. 2019-07-01;Ord. 2020-03-08;Ord. 2024-12-16)
14   B.     Definitions.

15        For the purposes of this section, the following definitions shall apply:

16          1.    Streets. In commercial zones, the definition of "street" shall be limited to public rights-of-way

17         (Amended: Ord. 2019-07-01; Ord. 2020-03-08; Ord. 2024-12-16)
18   C. Uses.

19     The uses set out in Table 40. 230. 010-1 are examples of uses allowable in the various zone districts. The
20   appropriate review authority is mandatory.

21 .             "P" - Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.

22 .             "R7A" - Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40. 520. 020.

23 .             "C" - Conditional uses which may be pemiitted subject to the approval of a conditional use pennit as set
24               forth in Section 40. 520. 030.

25 .             "X" - Uses specifically prohibited.

26   Where there are special use standards or restrictions for a listed use, the applicable code section(s) in Chapter
27   40. 260, Special Uses and Standards, or other applicable chapter is noted in the "Special Standards" column.

16        shall containsuitabletree speciesplanted every twenty-four(24) feet to provide for a continuous tree

     EXHIBIT 14



 2   40. 230. 020 Mixed Use District (MX)
 3   A. Purpose.

 4      The mixed use (MX) district requires mixed use developments to provide the community with a mix of mutually
 5   supporting retail, service, office and residential uses. It promotes cohesive site planning and design which integrates
 6   and interconnects two (2) or more land uses into a development that is mutually supportive. It provides incentives
 7   to develop a higher-density, active, urban environment than generally found in a suburban community, and which is
 8   further expected to:

 9        1. Achieve goals and objectives of the community framework plan and the comprehensive plan;

10        2. Enhance livability, environmental quality and economic vitality;

11        3. Accommodate and respect surrounding land uses by providing a gradual transition adjacent to lower
12        density neighborhoods that may encircle a potential mixed use site;

13        4. Maximizeefficient use ofpublic facilities and services;

14        5. Provide a variety of housing types and densities;

15        6. Reduce the number of automobile trips and encourage alternative modes of transportation; and

16        7. Create a safe, attractive and convenient environment for living, working, recreating andtraveling.

17   B. Applicability.

18        1. General. The provisions of this section shall be applied to parcels or groups of contiguous parcels
19        designatedmixed use on the zoningmap.

20        2. Interpretation. If the requirements of the mixed use district conflict with other regulations, the more
21        stringent ofthe two (2) shall apply as determined by the responsible official.

22   C.   Definitions.

23     For the purposes of this section, the following definitions shall apply:

24        1. "Mixeduse development" shall mean a group of structures planned as a single entity and containing within
25        and/or among them a variety of complementary and/or mutually supporting uses (such as housing, offices,
26        retail, public service or entertainment).

27        2. "Developable area" means that portion ofthe site whichis unencumbered by environmentally sensitive
28        lands to be protected and their respective buffers, designated parks and open space, public right-of-way, and
29        road easements.

30        3. "Building footprint" means the area contamed within the exterior building walls of the ground floor of a
31        building plus the area of any portion of upper floors that extend outside of the footprint of the ground floor

32        4;-"("ottuge housing" moans a collection of small singlc-family detached dwelling units arranged
33        around a common open space.




                                                                                                                   122
         5 4. "Mixed use building" means any building that combines both permitted residential and nonresidential
         uses.


         6 5. "Net ground floor area" means nonresidential floor area and excludes parking areas.

 4       ^ 6. "Senior housing" means dwelling units specifically for occupancy by persons fifty-five (55) years of age
 5       or older.

 6   D. Uses. The uses set out in Table 40. 230. 020-1 are permitted or conditional in the mixed use (MX) district.
 7   Residentialuses are permitted so long as the minimum required densities ofthis section are met. The appropriate
 8   review authority is mandatory.

 9       .   "P" Uses allowed subject to approval of applicable permits.

10       .   "R7A" Uses permitted upon review and approval as set forth in Section 40. 520. 020.

11       .   "C" Conditional uses which may be pennitted subject to the approval of a conditional use permit as set
12           forth in Section 40. 520. 030.


13           "X" Uses specifically prohibited.

11                 ceiling height of thirteen (13) feet to provide the opportunity for future conversion to nonresidential

 1
 2   H. Approval Process.
 3      1. Site Plan Review Required. Mixed use developments shall require submittal of a site plan, as required
 4      under Section 40. 520. 040, Site Plan Review, and Table 40. 510. 050-1, Application Submittal Requirements.
 5      The mixed use site plan shall be processed under the Type III review process, per Section 40. 510. 030.

 6          a. The applicant for a proposed mixed use development shall certify that two (2) or more of the
 7          following have been involved with the preparation of the preliminary mixed use development plan:

 8                (1) An architect licensed in the state of Washington; and/or

 9                (2) A landscape architect licensed in the state of Washington; and/or

10                (3) A registered civil engineer or a registered land surveyor licensed in the state of Washington;
11                and/or

12                (4) A certified arborist, if a vegetation management plan is required.

13          b. One of the above professionals shall be designated by the applicant to be responsible for submitting
14          materials to and communicating with the responsible official with respect to the concept and details of the
15          development plan. This applicant designee shall act as a liaison between the responsible official, the
16          design team and the applicant. The selection of this liaison shall not prevent the applicant or any member
17          of the design team from conferring with the responsible official or presenting material to the responsible
18          official. The responsible official may require that the expertise of other professionals be used in the
19          planning and development of the mixed use development if it is determined that the site merits special
20          consideration due to particularly unusual or adverse features or conditions.

21          c. All plans and specifications required for the development shall be prepared and designed by engineers
22          and/or architects licensed in the state of Washington.

23      2. Neighborhood Meeting Required. A neighborhood meeting shall be held prior to submission of an
24      application for a mixed use development. The applicant shall hold a public meeting to offer owners of property
25      adjacent to the affected property an opportunity to participate in the development process. A pre-application




                                                                                                                  132
 1   conference is not a substitute for the required neighborhoodmeeting. The applicant shall follow the
 2   neighborhood meeting guidelines established by the county.

          a.    The neighborhood meeting shall be held within ninety (90) days prior to submitting the application.

 4       b.     The applicant shall send a notice of the meeting at least fifteen (15) days prior to the neighborhood
 5       meeting to:

 6             (1) The official representative(s) of the county-recognized neighborhood associations), if applicable,
 7             in whose boundaries the affected property is located, based on the list of official neighborhood
 8             associations kept by the responsible official; and

 9             (2) Residents and property owners of record of property within a radius of five hundred (500) feet of
10             the subject property. The records ofthe County Assessor shall be used for determiningthe property
11             owners ofrecord. The mailing list used for notification shall be based on the most recent property tax
12             assessment rolls within thirty (30) days of mailing of the dark County Assessor. At the request of the
13             applicant, and upon payment of an applicable fee, the county will provide the required mailing list.

14             (3) The responsible official.

15       c. The notice must identify the date, time and place of the meeting and provide a brief description of the
16       proposed development.

17       d. A copy of the notice, proposed development plan as presented at the meeting, the mailing list, meeting
18       summary and the sign-in sheet from the meeting shall be submitted with the application.

19   3. Master Plan. All developments with proposed phasing of uses shall submit a master plan pursuant to
20   Section 40. 520. 070.

     EXHIBIT 19



 2   40.260.072 Compact Lot Developments
 3   A. Purpose.

 4     The purposes ofthe compactlot development provisions are:
 5        1. To encourage development of underutilized and challenged parcels within developed portions of the low-
 6        density residential districts by providing flexibility for smaller, more efficient housing.

 7        2. To diversify the county's housing stock by providing a variety of housing types, sizes and price points that
 8       respondsto changinghouseholdsizesandages, such asretirees, small families, and single-parenthouseholds.
 9        3. To encouragedevelopment in existingresidential areasby allowinga density bonusanddesignflexibility.
10       4. To mitigate potential impacts of density and narrow lots through site design balancing community
11       connections and privacy.

12       5. To integrate developments into existing neighborhoods.

13       (Added: Ord. 2024-03-02)

14   B. Applicability.

15       1. Compact lot developments are permitted on parcels created prior to the adoption date of the ordinance
16       codified in this section that meet all ofthe following criteria:

17            a. The parcel is three (3) acres or smaller in area, excludingany critical areas,public rights-of-way,
18            private road easements, or street tracts; and

19            b. The proposed development can andwill be servedby urbanservices at the time offinal plat or
20            development approval. For the purposes of this chapter, "urban services" shall mean public water and
21            sewer service as describedin Chapter 40. 370.

22       2. Compact lot developments may not be used in conjunction with a planned unit development per Section
23       40. 520. 080.

24       3. Compact lot developments that meet the a          licabili standards of Section 40.260. 155 B shall also be
25       subject to tlw-aerrow lot standards of Section 40. 260. 155 Narron Lots

26       (Added: Ord. 2024-03-02)

27   C. Review Required.

28     A site plan is required that meets the requirements of Section 40. 260. 155(C)(1). The site plan shall be submitted
29   with the preliminary land division application.

30       (Added: Ord. 2024-03-02)
31   D. Development Standards.

32       1. Compact lot developments may include single-family detached dwellings at up to two hundred percent
33       (200%) of the maximum density of the underlying zone, including any accessory dwelling units.




                                                                                                                   139
2. Minimum lot area and lot dimensions for single-family detached dwellings shall be as follows:

14   E.   Main Entrances.

15      Main entrances shall be visible from the street and must have a porch or entry set back no more than eight (8)
16   feet from the longest street-facing wall of the structure. The entry must: face the street, open onto the porch, or be
17   oriented at an angle of not more than forty-five (45) degrees from the street-facing facade. Corner lot homes may be
18   oriented to either street.

22   F. Garages.

23     The width of the garage door facing the street may be up to sixty percent (60%) of the lot width or ten (10) feet,
24   whichever is greater. Garage width limitations do not apply to residential development accessed through rear alleys,
25   or where the garage is located in the rear of the lot.

 2   40.260. 160 Nursery Schools, Preschools, Kindergartens, Commcrciul ^jyld^Day Care Centers, and Family
 3   Day Care Providers
 4   A. Nursery schools, preschools, kindergartens and commercial dnld. day care centers shall comply with the
 5   following criteria:

 6       1. Minimum site size shall be ten thousand (10, 000) square feet, except, when a preschool, kindergarten or
 7       commercial child day care center is designed as a part of an integrated industrial, commercial or multifamily
 8       development, in whichcasethe minimum lot sizemay be reducedby the review authority, provided all other
 9       applicable code requirements are met.

10       2. Provide and maintain outdoor play areas with a minimum area of one hundred (100) square feet per
11       individual basedupon total capacity.

12            a. The outdoor play area requirement shall not apply to strictly "drop-in facilities" where the individuals
13            cared for are not on the premises for more than three (3) hours in a twenty-four (24) hour period; provided,
14            that the requirements of the Washington Administrative Code are met.

15            b. Facilities with a capacity of forty (40) individuals or more, under the licensing authority of the state
16            Department of Social and Health Services (DSHS), and with an approved "shifting schedule" for the use
17            of outdoor play area by DSHS, may calculate the outdoor play area based on one hundred (100) square
18            feet per individual using the outdoor area at any one (1) time; however, a minimum of four thousand
19            (4, 000) square feet of outdoor play area must be provided.

20            c. Facilitieswith a capacity ofthirty-nine (39) or less, or whichdo not qualify with a "shiftingschedule"
21            as stated above, may count up to fifty (50) square feet of dedicated indoor play area per individual of
22            capacity toward the outdoor play area requirements.

23       3. The play area shall be abutting the indoor facility.

24       4. A sight-obscuring fence of at least four (4) feet, but not more than six (6) feet in height, shall be provided
25       around the outdoor play area.

26       5. Adequateoff-streetparkingandloading spaceshallbe providedpursuant to Chapter40.340.
27       (Amended: Ord. 2008-06-02)
28   B. Family day care facilities roviders shall comply with the following criteria:

29       1. Whenlocated in a resource, rural or residential zone (Rl-5, Rl-6, Rl-7. 5, Rl-10, Rl-20, R 12. R 18, R
30       22, II 30, R 43, OR 15, OR 18, OR 22, OR 30, OR 13 R-24 R-36 R-5U OR-24 OR-36 OR-50, R-5, R-
31       10, R-20, FR-80, FR-40, AG-20, and AG-WLdistricts), no exterior structural or decorative alteration which
32       will alter the residential character of a residence is permitted.

33       2. Adequate off-street parking and loading space shall be provided pursuant to Chapter 40. 340.

34       3. Two (2) nonresident or non-family member employees are permitted if located within a resource, rural or
35       residential zone.

36       4. Signage shall be limited to one (1) sign, not to exceed two (2) square feet in area, for identification
37       purposes only.

     EXHIBIT 24



 2   40.260.260 Zero Lot Line Developments
 3   A. Purpose.

 4      Zero lot line provisions are intended to provide flexibility for the placement of structures in new residential land
 5   divisions in order to increase usable yard area. Zero lot line developments are different from townhouse
 6   developments in that structures are not attached at property lines.

 7       (Amended: Ord. 2024-03-02)

 8   B. Applicability.

 9     These provisions can be used as an alternative to standard side or rear setback requirements in the Rl -5, Rl -6
10   and Rl-7. 5, R 12, OR 12, R 18, OR 18, R 22 and OR 22 zoning districts, including within a compact lot
11   development subject to Section 40. 260. 072, when the requirements of this section are applied in conjunction with a
12   land division application.

13       (Amended: Ord. 2024-03-02)

14   C. Requirements.

15       1. Preliminary plats using zero lot line provisions may not be approved without approval of a submitted site
16       plan. Both the site plan and preliminary plat shall be consistent with the standards of this section and all other
17       applicable requirements.

18       2. Conditions may be imposed to ensure that development on the resultant lots is consistent with the
19       approved site plan.

20       3. Building permits for structures may only be approved when consistent with the approved site plan and land
21       division.

22       4. Developments meeting all requirements of this section are exempt from separate site plan review under
23       Section 40. 520.040.

24       5. Zero setbacks may be applied to a side or rear property line; provided, that the setback on the lot abutting a
25       zero setback lot line shall be at least ten (10) feet in the applicable single-family residential districts, and eight
26       (8) feet in the applicable residential or office residential district, except that the setback shall be at least six (6)
27       feet in a compact lot development in any residential district.

28       6. Structures less than three (3) feet to a property line must meet fire resistive provisions of the residential
29       building code.

30       7. A note shall be placed on the final plat, and covenants running with the land shall be approved by the
31       Prosecuting Attorney and recorded with the County Auditor to guarantee that the required setbacks are kept
32       perpetually free of structures.

33       8. Easements for such purposes as maintenance, or for building projection encroachments beyond a zero
34       setback line, shall be shown on the plat.

35       (Amended: Ord. 2024-03-02)
36   D. Figure 40. 260. 260-1.

                                                    Zero setback lot lines

 1   EXHIBIT25
 2

 3

 4   40. 310. 010     Sign Standards

 5   A.      Purpose.
 6           The purpose of this section is to add sign requirements common to the several zoning districts for the
 7           preservation of the character of the areas, structures, and uses; the needs of residential, commercial,
 8           industrial and agricultural potential; the need for healthful, safe, and convenient use of all lands, and the
 9           conservation and promulgation of values and resources. These requirements include, but are not limited
10           to, standards relating to the number, size, placement and physical characteristics of signs. In addition, the
11           purpose of this section is to provide an effective administrative process for the review and enforcement of
12           these standards to protect and improve the aesthetic quality of the community.

13   B.      Scope.
14           The signage covered under this section includes, but is not limited to: all commercial signs, and wall
15           graphics; professional and business signs; home business signs; banners, balloons, flags and other
16           temporary signage. It is not intended to regulate traffic signs or other governmental street signs, doorway
17           identification nameplates, holiday decorations, informational signs pursuant to Chapter 13. 20, temporary
18           interior window signage or memorial signs. Also, it is not intended to regulate signage which is not visible
19           from adjacent properties or from public rights-of-way. Further, it is not intended to regulate the display of
20           the national or state flag.
21           Three other sections of the dark County Code contain additional requirements for sign codes, namely:
22           (1) Section 40. 260. 1 15(E)(5), which pertains to marijuana facilities, (2) Appendix A (F), which pertains to
23           sign standards for mixed use development, and (3) Appendix F, Chapter 8. 4, which pertains to sign
24           standards for development in the Highway 99 Overlay District.

25   C.      Definitions.
26           The following definitions and terms shall be used in the interpretation of this section:
27
             Banner                    "Banner" means an on-site sign such as those used to announce an
                                       open house, a grand opening, or to make a special announcement.
                                       Normally, it is constructed ofcloth, canvas, or similar material and
                                       is without a rigid frame. It will be considered either as a fascia or
                                       freestanding sign, depending on the method of attachments, and
                                       will have to comply with the nomial zone requirements.


             Billboard                 "Billboard"shall mean the same as "off-premises sign." See
                                       definition of'off-premises sign."

             Business complex          "Business complex" means two (2) or more commercial
                                       businesses on a lot or abutting lots with common access and
                                       parking.

             Business complex          "Businesscomplex sign"means a sign whichis designedto
             sign                      identify a business complex where no single business
                                       identification and/or advertisement occupies more than fifity
                                       percent (50%) ofthe sign area.




                                                                                                                    155
Directional sign     "Directional sign" means any sign which is designed and erected
                     solely for the purpose of traffic or pedestrian direction, and which
                     is placed on the property to which the public is directed. See also
                     "off-premises directional sign."

Doorway              "Doorway identification sign" means a non-illuminated sign which
identificationsign   is limited to the name, address, and number of the building,
                     institution, or person andto the activity carried on in the building
                     or institution, or the occupancy of the person; provided, that the
                     lettering, excluding numbers, of each sign shall not exceed two (2)
                     square feet.

Electronic           "Electronicmessage center" means a sign on whichthe copy
message center       changes automatically on a lampbank or through mechanical
                     means, such as electrical or electronic time andtemperature units.


Fasciasign           "Fascia sign" means a flat sign which projects one and one-half
                     (11/2) feet or less horizontally from the vertical face of the wall of a
                     building, or vertical face of a canopy awning or parapetupon
                     which it is affixed, painted, or attached, running parallel for its
                     whole length to the face or wall ofthe building and which does not
                     extend beyondthe horizontal width of such wall, awningor
                     parapet.

Freestanding sign    "Freestandingsign"meansa signnot attachedto or forming a part
                     of a building.

Height of sign       "Height of sign" means the vertical distance measured from grade
                     at the point of support, or average grade for multiple supports and
                     monument signs, to the highest point on the sign and sign
                     structure.

Industrial           "Industrial complex" means two (2) or more industrial businesses
complex              on a lot or abutting lots with common access and parkmg.


Industrial           "Industrial complex sign" means a sign which is designed to
complex sign         identify an industrial complex where no single business
                     identification and advertisement occupies more than fifty percent
                     (50%) of the sign.

Monument sign        "Monument sign" means a sign and supporting structure which has
                     similar top andbottom dimensions and is constructed as a solid
                     structure or one whichgives the appearance of a continuous, non-
                     hollow, unbroken, unfenestrated mass. Further, similar top and
                     bottom dimensions shall mean dimensions which are within ten
                     percent (10%) of each other.




                                                                                                156
Nonconfomiing sign "Nonconforming sign" means a sign which was erected legally but
                   which does not comply with currently applicable sign restrictions
                   and regulations; provided, a sign which does not conform to the
                   currently applicable sign code requirements, but for which a
                   variance or conditional use permit was issued, shall not be
                   considered a nonconforming sign.


Off-premises sign   "Off-premises sign" means a sign that advertises products, services
                    or facilities or directs persons to a premises different from where
                    the sign is installed.

Off-premises        "Off-premises directional sign" means a sign which directs persons
directionalsign     to a premises different from where the sign is located. These signs
                    typically include an address or street name or direction such as
                    "two blocks north on the right."

On-premises sign    "On-premises sign" means a sign which carries only
                    advertisements strictly relatedto a lawful use ofthe premises on
                    which it is located, including signs or sign devices indicating the
                    business transacted, services rendered, goods sold or produced on
                    the premises, name of the business, and name of the person, firm or
                    corporation occupying the premises. "On-premises sign" also
                    means a sign which contains religious, political, social and other
                    noncommercial messages.


Portable sign       "Portable sign" means any sign designedto move or be moved by
                    trailer or vehicle to advertise at different locations. Further, these
                    signs include any sign which is not fmnly fastened to a building or
                    the ground.

Premises            "Premises" for the purposes of this section, means one (1) of the
                    following:
                     a. A legal lot; or
                     b. A combination of contiguous legal lots under one (1)
                     ownership; or
                     c. A group of legal lots with common access, parking and
                     signage.
                    If more than one (1) definition of "premises" can be applied to a
                    group of lots, the choice of which definition applies shall be the
                    owner(s) of the lots or the applicant for a sign permit representing
                    the owner(s); provided, only one (1) definition may be applied at
                    one (1) time to a group of lots; and provided further, all signs
                    within the premises or subsequent revision to the premises, shall
                    comply with the provisions of the sign code.




                                                                                             157
Projecting sign   "Projecting sign" means a sign which projects more than one and
                  one-half(ll/2) feet horizontally from the vertical face of a building,
                  awning, canopy or parapet.

Rooftop sign      "Rooftop sign" means a sign erected upon the roof of a building or
                  canopy, or a sign attached to a building which projects vertically
                  above the roof, eave, awning or parapet; however, this does not
                  include signs attached to the vertical face of a parapet, awning or
                  canopy; provided, the sign does not project above the vertical face
                  of the parapet, awning or canopy to which it is attached.


Rotatingsign      "Rotatingsign"meansa signin whichthe signitselfor any portion
                  of the sign moves in a revolving or similar manner.

Sigii             "Sign" means a display or device affixed to the ground, attached
                  to a building, or other structure using graphics, symbols, and/or
                  written copy designed specifically for the display of a commercial
                  or other advertisementto the public.

Sign area         "Sign area" means:
                   a. The area of a freestanding sign or structure not using an
                   integral part of the building for its background means the largest
                   cross-sectional area of the sign measured to a line encompassing
                   all portions ofthe sign structure, includingtubing used in
                   lighting such sign or structure, but excluding posts without
                   attached lighting. Further, the base on which a monument-type
                   sign is set may be excluded; provided, there is no attached
                   lighting.
                  b. The areaofa double-faced sign(i. e., a signpainted ontwo
                  (2) sides, or signs which are erected in a "V" configuration with an
                  angle between the two (2) faces not exceeding thirty
                  (30) degrees), shall be the largest area on one (1) side of the sign.
                  Further, these types of signs shall be considered one (1) sign for the
                  purpose of determining the number of signs allowed.
                  c. The area of any sign or structure using an integral part of the
                  building or awningas a backgroundmeans the areawithin the
                  shortest line drawn to include all letters, design and tubing which
                  are a part of the sign or structure; provided, that for illuminated
                  awnings the area shall be limited to the area within the shortest line
                  drawn to include all copy and graphics, excluding illuminated
                  areas outside of these lines.




Street frontage   "Street frontage" means the linear frontage of a parcel of property
                  abutting a single public street.




                                                                                           158
          Temporary sign         "Temporary sign" means any sign which is not permanently
                                 installed or affixedto any sign stmcture or building, and not
                                 displayed for longer than thirty (30) days. In case of construction
                                 project signs, they may be maintained for the duration of the
                                 construction. Temporary signs shall also include balloons attached
                                 to buildings or property.


 1   D.   Signs Prohibited.
 2        Erection or maintenance of signs having any of the following characteristics is prohibited in dark
 3        County:

 4        1. Signs whichbear or contain statements, words or pictures of an obscenenature;

 5        2. Signs advertising activities that are illegal under state or federal laws or regulations in effect at the
 6           location of such signs or at the locations of such activities;

 7        3. A sign which does not bear the names of the owner or person responsible for the maintenance of the
 8           advertising sign;
 9        4. Signs artificially illuminated which are of such intensity or placed in such manner as to interfere
10           with, or impair the vision ofthe driver of a motor vehicle, or otherwise interfere with any driver's
11           operation of a motor vehicle;

12        5. Signs which attempt or appear to attempt to direct the movement of traffic by interfering with,
13           imitating or resembling any official traffic sign, signal or device;

14        6. Signs which prevent the driver of a vehicle from having a clear and unobstructed view of official
15           signs and approaching or merging traffic as defined in Section 40. 350. 030(B)(8);

16        7. Signs which exceed a height of thuly-five (35) feet, except as authorized under Section
17           40. 310. 010(0). Signs authorized by the district standards under Section 40. 310. 010(F)(3) and
18           Tables 40. 310. 010-2 through 40. 310. 010-6 shall comply with the height restrictions contained
19            therein;

20        8. Signs located or projecting within the county right-of-way unless a written street use permit has
21           been obtained;

22        9. Rooftop and rotating signs;

23        10. Portable signs, temporary signs, flags and banners unless a temporary sign permit has been
24            approved by the responsible official;

25        11. Signs containing strobe lights which are visible from beyond the property line;

26        12. Any sign not specifically permitted by this section, excludingthose signs identified in the scope
27            of this chapter;

28        13. Signs which contain flashing lights which exceed more than ten percent (10%) of the area of the
29            sign;

30        14. Off-premises signs (billboards), except for off-premises directional signs as allowed under Table
31            40.310.010-1 of this section.

32   E.   SignPermits Required.
33        Sign permits pursuant to Section 40. 520. 050 are required for all signs which are authorized under

 2       for real estate signs and agricultural signs in Table 40. 310. 010-3 through Table 40. 310. 010-6.

4    F   Requirements for Signs - General and by Zoning Districts.

 5       1.   Temporary Signs in Certain Commercial Zones (GC, CR-1, CR-2, NC, CC, BP, and A Zones).

6             a.    A permit for temporary sign(s) and/or banner(s) for a grand opening or other special event
7                   may be approved by the responsible official. The temporary sign permit shall be subject to
8                   the following conditions:
9             b.    The permit shall be for a maximum thirty (30) day period.

10                  (1) No morethantwo(2) temporarypermits shallbeissuedto a businessper year.
11                  (2) A cashor surety bond in the amount ofonethousand dollars ($1,000) or other assurances
12                         may be required from the responsible official to ensure the removal of the temporary
13                         sign(s) at the end of the above stated period.

14                  (3) The cash or surety bond shall be forfeited if (all) the temporary sign(s) is (are) not removed
15                      at above stated required time.

16                  (4) Forfeitureofthe cashor surety bond shallnot relieve the pennittee from compliancewith
17                         the removal of the sign(s).

10   Limited Access Highway- RestrictedAdvertising.
11   The provisions of this subsection shall be applicable to all land abutting or adjoining Interstate 5 (1-5) and
12   Interstate 205 (1-205) and within three hundred (300) feet of the edge of the right-of-way, and with-in
13   commercial or employment zoning districts.

14   1. Restrictions.

15          a. No sign or sign structure, or portion thereof, shall exceed a height of fifty
16              (50) feet;
17          b. The erection or remodeling of signs directed at limited access highways shall be approved by the
18             responsible official, only after consideration has been given to the location, size, and orientation of
19             such signs, as provided for in this subsection;

20          c. The sign shall be designed to be viewed by a person of normal visual acuity traveling on 1-5 or I-
21             205;




                                                                                                               167
 1            d. Signs shall only contain advertising display specifically and directly related to products produced,
 2               sold, used, or otherwise handled, or services rendered on the real property;
 3            e. Not more than one (1) limited access highway advertising sign shall be located on the premises.
 4                 The total number of signs allowed on the premises shall not exceed the district standards of
 5                 40. 310. 010(F) and Tables 40. 310. 010-2 through 40. 310. 010-6;
 6            f.   The area of the sign shall comply with the standards of the district in which it is located.
 7         2. Freestanding signs oriented toward Interstate 5 (1-5) or Interstate 205 (1-205) that arenot oriented
 8            toward the abutting roadway are considered limited access highway signs, and are subject to this
 9            section. (See Figure 40.310.010-1.)

10         3. Conformance to State Law. Nothing in this subsection shall be construed to permit the erection
11            of signs which are prohibited by state law or any amendments thereto.

16   H.   Nonconforming Signs.
17        Nonconforming signs shall be subject to the conditions set forth below:

18        1. A nonconforming sign or sign structure shall not be altered or enlarged in any manner unless
19           such alteration or enlargement would bring the sign into conformity with the signs permitted in
20           the zoning district in which it is located; provided, the restriction against alteration does not apply
21           to copy or panel changes where the sign area and shape is maintained.

22        2. Any nonconforming sign or sign structure may be maintained with ordinary care.

23        3. All nonconforming temporary signs, portable signs, flags, pendants and banners shall be
24            removed.
