                                                                                                                       168
 1        Mobile Manufactured Home Park Signs.

 2        Mobile Manufactured home park signs shall be pennitted as follows:

 3        1. Identificationsignsadvertisingmobile manufactured home parks; provided, that suchsigns shall have
 4            a maximum area ofthirty-two (32) square feet and no more than one (1) sign allowed per street
 5            frontage;

 6        2. Only nonilluminated and indirectly illuminated signs shall be permitted in R 12, R 18, R 22, R 30, R
 7            13, OR 15, OR 18, OR 22, OR JO, OR 4J R-24 R-36 R-50 OR-24 OR-36 OR-50, and PUD
 8           zones. No flashing or intennittent illumination shall be permitted;

          3. No sign or sign structure shall exceed fifteen (15) feet in height;

10        4. Signs over five (5) feet in height shall conform to the minimum setback designated for structures in that
11            zone;

12        5. Incidentalsigns for the informationand convenience oftenants andthe public relative to parking, traffic
13            movement, office, restrooms, etc., are allowed; provided, such signs do not exceed four (4) square feet
14            in area; andprovided further, nameplates do not exceedeight (8) by twelve (12) inches(8" x 12")in
15            area;

16        6. One (1) sign advertising the sale of each mobilu manufactured home located on a mobile
17            manufactured home lot.

18        Signs Facing Residential Districts.
19        A sign advertising a businesswhichis not conducted on the premises, or a commodity or servicewhichis
20        not the preliminary product of sale or services on the premises, shall not face or be oriented toward any
21        adjoiningor abuttingresidentialdistrictwithintwo hundred(200) feet ofthe premises onwhichthe signis
22        to be placed.

23   K.   On-Site Interference.
24        The location and structural design of freestanding signs shall not interfere with the safe and efficient use
25        of off-street parking and loading areas, including aisle ways and access driveways.

26   L.   Lighted Signs as Nuisance.
27        Illuminatedsignsshall be placed so as notto be a nuisanceto anyresidentsor future residents of adjacent
28        residentially zoned property within two hundred (200) feet of the sign. A nuisance shall be defined as
29        flashing lights or lights of such intensity whichmay interfere with the residents' peaceful occupancy of
30        their home. As part of a sign permit or site plan review process, the planning manager may require signs to
31        be screened, shielded, relocated or the lighting adjusted or other measures to mitigate a potential
32        interference with abutting residentially zoned property.

33   M.   Enforcement.
34        Upon presentation of proper credentials, the responsible official or a duly authorized representative may
35        enter at reasonable times a building or structure, or upon any premises in dark County, to perform any
36        dutyimposedbythis section. Theresponsibleofficialmay inspectorreinspectall signsathis/herdiscretion.
37   N.   Removal of Signs in Violation of This Section.
38        If the responsible official finds that any advertisingsign is erected or maintainedin violation ofthe prior
39        sign regulations, or is erected or maintained in violation of the provisions of this section, the responsible
40        official may institute appropriate action or proceedingsto prevent, restrain, correct or abatethe violation,
41        including fines and enforcement pursuant to Title 32 of this code.




                                                                                                                 169
 1   0.       Maintenanceand Appearance of Signs.
 2            All advertising signs, together with all of their supports, braces, guys, and anchors, shall be kept in good
 3            repairand maintained in a safe condition. All advertisingsigns and the sites upon whichthey are located
 4            shall be maintained in a neat, clear, and attractive condition, and advertising signs shall be kept free from
 5            excessive rust, corrosion, peeling paint or other surface deterioration. The display surface of all outdoor
 6            advertising structures shall be kept neatly painted or posted.

 7   P        Abandoned Signs.
 8            Exceptasprovidedinthis section, anypersonwhoownsor leasesa signshallremovesuchsignwhen
 9            either the function has discontinued or business it advertises has discontinued on the premises on
10            which the sign is located; or when the sign is no longer properly repaired or maintained as required
11            by this section.

 4          40. 320.010 Landscapingand Screeningon Private Property

 5         A. Applicability.
 6                The following standards apply to landscapingand screening on private property. Landscapingand
 7                screening within public rights-of-way shall comply with the applicable provisions in Section
 8                40.320.020.
 9         B.     Landscaping and Screening Design Standards.

10                1.   LI, General Landscaping.

11                     a.   Intent. The LI standard is for open areas. It is intended to be used where distance is the
12                          principal means of separating uses or development, and landscaping enhances the area
13                          between them. The LI standard consists principally of groundcover plants, trees, and
14                          shrubs.

15                     b.   RequiredMaterials. There aretwo (2) waysto providetrees andshrubsto comply with an
16                          LI standard. Shrubs and trees may be grouped. Groundcover plants, grass lawn or
17                          approved flowers must fully cover the landscaped area not in shrubs and trees. See Figure
18                          40. 320. 010-1 for conventional and LID cross-sections that comply with the LI standard.

19                          (1) Where the area to be landscaped is less than ten (10) feet deep, one
20                                (1) tree shall be providedperthirty (30) linear feet oflandscaped area.
21                          (2) Wherethe areais ten(10) feetdeepor greater,one(1) tree shall beprovidedper eight
22                               hundred(800) square feet andeithertwo (2) high shrubs or three (3) low shrubs shall
23                               be provided per four hundred (400) square feet of landscaped area.

24                     c.   Within the commercial districts where a building is to be placed at the buffer line for a
25                          front setback, permeable pavement may be used in place of the required groundcover for
26                          the length of the building for the front setback only; provided, the required trees are still
27                          supplied, the paved area is connected to the public sidewalk, and pedestrian amenities are
28                          provided such as benches or pedestrian plazas. The building need not be placed at the
29                          required buffer line to utilize this section if the areabetween the buffer line and the building
30                          is devoted entirely to pedestrian only areas.

31                2.   L2, Low Screen.

32                     a.   Intent. The L2 standarduses a combinationofdistanceand low-level screeningto separate
33                          uses or development. The standard is applied where a low level of screening sufficiently
34                          reduces the impact of a use or development, or where visibility between areas is more
35                          important than a greater visual screen.
36                     b.   Required Materials. The L2 standard requires enough low shrubs to form a
37                           continuous screen three (3) feet high and ninety-five percent (95%) opaque year-
38                           round. In addition, one (1) tree is required per thirty (30) lineal feet of landscaped
39                           area or as appropriate to provide a tree canopy over the landscaped area.
40                           Groundcover plants must fully cover the remainder of the landscaped area with the
41                           exception of energy dissipation points at the locations of stormwater inlets. LID
42                           bioretention facility plantings may be used in combination with perimeter shrubs,



                                                                                                                      171
 1               provided a continuous screen three (3) feet high and ninety-five percent (95%)
 2               opaque year-round can be achieved within two (2) years of planting. A three (3)
 3               foot high masonry wall or fence at an F2 standard or a benn may be substituted for
 4               shrubs, but the trees and groundcover plants are still required. When applied along
 5               street lot lines, the screen or wall is to be placed along the interior side of the
 6               landscaped area. See Figure 40. 320. 010-2.

     3.   L3, High Screen.
 8         a.   Intent. The L3 standard provides physical and visual separation between uses or
 9              development principally using screening. It is used where such separation is
10              warranted by a proposed development, notwithstanding loss of direct views.

11        b.    Required Materials. TheL3 standardrequires enoughhigh shrubsto form a screen
12              six (6) feet high and ninety-five percent (95%) opaque year-round. In addition, one
13              (1) tree is required per thirty (30) lineal feet of landscaped area or as appropriate to
14              provide a tree canopy over the landscaped area. Groundcover plants must fully cover
15              the remainder of the landscaped area with the exception of energy dissipation points
16              at the locations ofstormwater inlets. LIDbioretentionfacility plantingsmay be used
17              in combination with perimeter shrubs, provided a continuous screen six (6) feet high
18              and ninety-five percent (95%) opaque year-round can be achieved within two (2)
19              years ofplanting. A six (6) foot high wall or fence that complies with the F2 standard
20              (Figure40.320.010-7)withor without a berm may be substitutedfor shrubs, but the
21              trees and groundcover plants are still required. When applied along street lot lines,
22              the screen or wall is to be placed along the interior sideofthe landscapedarea. See
23              Figure 40. 320. 010-3.
24   4.   L4, High Wall.
25        a.    Intent. The L4 standard is used where extensive screening of visual and noise
26              impacts is needed to protect abutting sensitive uses and/or there is little space for
27              separationbetween uses.
28              b.      RequiredMaterials.TheL4standardrequiresa six(6) foothighwallthatcomplies
29              with the site obscuring F2 standard for walls (Figure 40. 320. 010-7). When abutting another
30              property, the wall shallabutthe property line. Whenabuttinga streetor roadright-of-way,
31              the wall shall be on the interior side of the landscaped area. One (1) tree is required per
32              thirty (30) lineal feet ofwall or as appropriate to provide a tree canopy over the landscaped
33              area with the exception of energy dissipation points at the locations of stormwater inlets.
34              LID bioretention facility plantings may be used, and are encouraged, to satisfy plant
35              requirements. In addition, four (4) foot high shrubs are required per thirty (30) lineal
36              feet of wall. Ground-cover plants must fully cover the remainder of the landscaped area.
37              See Figure 40. 320. 010-4.

38   5.   L5, HighBerm.

39        a.    Intent. The L5 standard can be used instead of the L4 standardwhere extensive screening
40              is warrantedand more space is available for separationbetweenuses.

41        b.    Required Materials. The L5 standard requires a berm four (4) to six (6) feet high. Ifthe
42              berm is less than six (6) feet high, low shrubs that comply with the L2 standardmust be
43              planted on top of the berm so that the overall screen height is six (6) feet. In addition, one
44              (1) tree is required per thirty



                                                                                                        172
      (30) lineal feet of berm or as appropriate to provide a tree canopy over the landscaped area.
      Groundcover plants must fully cover the remainder of the landscaped area. See Figure 40. 320. 010-5.

          6.   Fl, Partially Sight-Obscuring Fence.

 4             a.   Intent. The Fl fence standard provides partial visual separation. The standard is applied
 5                  where a proposed use or development has little impact, or where visibility between areas
 6                  is more important than a total visual screen and the installation of fencing will not interfere
 7                  with the implementation of LID stormwater management on the site.

               b.   RequiredMaterials.A fenceor wall thatcomplieswiththe Fl standardshall besix(6) feet
 9                  high and at least fifty percent (50%) sight-obscuring. Fences may be made of wood, metal,
10                  chainlink withslats, bricks, masonry or otherpermanentmaterials. SeeFigure40.320.010-
11                  6.

12        7.   F2, Fully Sight-Obscuring Fence.

13             a.   Intent. The F2 fence standard provides visual separation where complete screening is
14                  needed to protect abutting uses, and landscaping alone cannot provide that separation.

15             b.   RequiredMaterials.A fenceor wall thatcomplieswiththeF2 standardshallbesix(6) feet
16                  high and one hundred percent (100%) sight-obscuring. Fences may be made of wood,
17                  metal, bricks, masonry or other pennanent materials. This shall not include chain link
18                  fenceswith slats or similar construction. See Figure 40. 320.010-7.

19   C.   LandscapingandScreeningApproval Standards- General.
20        1.   A landscape plan shall contain landscaping and screening consistent with the
21             applicabledesignstandards,basedonTable40.320.010-1andotherapplicableprovisions ofthis
22             section.


23        2.   The applicant may provide landscaping and screening that exceeds the standards in this
24             section; provided:

25             a.   A fence or wall (or a combination of a berm and fence or wall) may not exceed a
26                  height of six (6) feet above the finished grade at the base of the fence or wall (or at
27                  the base ofaberm, if combined with one) unless the review authority finds additional
28                  height is necessary to mitigate potential adverse effects of the proposed use or other
29                  uses in the vicinity; and

30             b.   Landscaping and screening shall not obstruct sight distance at intersections as
31                  provided in Section 40. 350. 030 of the UDC.

32        3.   The responsible official may approve use of existing vegetation to fulfill landscaping and
33             screening requirements of this section if that existing landscaping provides at least an
34             equivalent level of screening as the standard required for the development in question.

35             As a condition ofapproval ofa conditional use or the expansion or alteration of an existing
36             conditional use or planned unit development, the county may require an applicant to
37             provide landscapingand screening that differs from the standards in Table 40. 320.010-1
38             and Section 40. 320. 010(C)(2) where necessary to comply with the other applicable
39             approval standards for the use or development.




                                                                                                             173
 1        5.    Landscapedareasrequired for stormwatermanagementpurposes may be used to satisfy
 2              the landscaping area requirements of this section even though those areas may be
 3              inundated by surface water. All stonnwater facility designs shall meet the standards as
 4              defined in Chapter 40. 386.

 5        6.   Required landscapingand screening shall be located on the perimeter of a lot or parcel.
 6             Required landscaping and screening shall not be located on a public right-of-way or
 7             private street easement, unless authorizedunder Section 40. 320.020.

 8        7.   The responsible official may approve and condition an alternative landscape design and
 9             buffer standardthat is compatible with existing, abutting landscapingthat still meets the
10             intent of the required buffer type, such as shared buffers between users.

11        8.   Required landscaping buffers shall not apply between buildings in common wall
12             construction situations.


13        9.   The exception to setback allowances under Section 40. 200. 070 shall not apply to
14             landscape buffers except as follows:
15             a.   Eaves that are sixteen (16) feet or more above grade may project twenty percent
16                  (20%) of the width of the buffer, up to a maximum of two (2)feet.

17             b.   Eaves seven (7) feet or more above grade may project ten percent (10%) of the width of
18                  the buffer, up to a maximum oftwo (2) feet.

21   D.   Landscape and Screening Standards for Storage and Equipment Areas.

22        1.   Storage and equipment areas shall be screened from property used or zoned for residential
23             purposes or a public road right-of-wayto at least an F2 or L3 standard if within one hundred
24             (100) feet of the property or right-of-way and to at least an Fl standard if equal to or more than
25             one hundred (100) feet from the property or right-of-way. Storage areas include storage of solid
26             waste and recyclables from the site and, where permitted, storage of goods, materials or
27             equipment.

28        2.   Rooftop and ground-level exterior equipment shall be screened from abutting property used or
29             zoned for residential purposes or from an abutting public road right-of-way to at least an F2 or
30             L3 standard if visible at grade from the property or right-of-way.

31   E.   Landscaping and Screening Standards for Parking, Loading, and Drive-Through Areas.



                                                                                                           174
 1        1.   A minimum five (5) foot wide strip landscaped to at least anL2 standard shall be provided where
 2             vehicle parking, loading, or drive-through facilities abut a public road right-of-way.

 3        2.   Wherea vehicleparkingor loadingareaabutsa property withzoningor landusesotherthanthe
 4             proposed land use, the area shall be landscaped and screened as provided in Table 40. 320. 010-
 5             1 abutting the other property.
 6        3.   Parkingareasthat containat least seven(7) spacesshall containlandscapeislands equally
 7              distributed at a ratio of one (1) island for every seven (7) parking spaces. A landscape island
 8              shall contain at least twenty-five (25) square feet, shall be at least four (4) feet wide, and
 9              shall prevent vehicles from damaging trees, such as by using a wheel stop or curb. Islands
10              may include storm-water facility design components, such as bioretention features.

11        4.    At least one (1) tree shall be planted in eachlandscapeisland. Trees in landscape islands
12              shall reach a mature height of thirty (30) feet or more, cast moderate to dense shade in the
13              summer, live at least sixty (60) years, require little maintenance (such as by being insect-
14              , disease- and drought-resistant and not producing fruit), and be suited for use in the
15             proposedlocation (suchasby beingtolerant ofpollution and direct andreflectedheat).
16   P.   Establishing Setback Standards for Retaining Walls and Fences.

17        1.   This sectionregulatestheheightofretainingwallsandfencesalongtheperimeterofsites.
18             Buildingcodes specifythe circumstancesunderwhichretainingwalls andfencesrequire
19             building and gradingpermits. Changesto stormwaterrunoffresulting from construction
20              ofretainingwalls are subjectto Chapter 40. 386.

21        2.    Constructionofprivateretainingwallsor fenceswithinpublicrights-of-wayis prohibited.
22             Exceptions to this prohibition require approval ofthe Public Works Director.

23        3.   The construction ofretaining walls four (4) feet or less in height and fences seven (7) feet
24              or less in height may be constructed within easements. This subsectiondoesnot exempt
25             retaining walls or fences from otherwise applicable easement provisions.

26        4.   The construction ofretaining walls in excess of four (4) feet in height and fences in excess
27             of seven (7) feet in height shall meet the setback requirements of the underlying zone,
28             except as authorized below. The height of a fence on top of retaining walls shall be
29             measured to the grade at the bottom of the wall. Each of the following is an exception-to
30             the heightandsetbackrequirements of Section40.320.010(F)(4):
31             a.    Retaining walls taller than four (4) feet may be placed within setbacks by using a
32                   series ofretaining walls four (4) feet high or less that are separated a minimum ofeight
33                   (8) feet, provided the area between walls is maintained in ground cover or shrubs.
34                   Thetotalheightofa seriesofwalls withinthebuildingsetbackshallnot exceedeight
35                   (8) feet. Landscaping shall be maintained consistent with Section 40. 320. 010(G)(6).
36                   Fences are allowed on top of such walls consistent with Section 40. 320. 010(F)(4)(h)
37                   andFigure 40.320.010-8;
38             b.   The exposed faces of retaining walls over four (4) feet in height must be directed
39                  towardthe interior ofthe lot;

40             c.    The retaining walls and/or fences are constructed as part of the site improvements
41                  prior to a final plat, and located between lots within the development. Retaining
42                  wallsontheperimeteroftheplatmaynotusethis exception,exceptasallowedunder



                                                                                                                175
                     subsection (F)(4)(d) ofthis section;
 2             d.    Retainingwalls and/orfencesabuttinga roadright-of-wayor roadeasement;subjectto the
 3                   following:

                     (1) The wall or fencedoesnot blockrequired sightdistance;
 5                   (2) Walls and/or fences over twelve (12) feet in height will be reviewed for potential
 6                        shading and visual impacts beyond the right-of-way or easement. The Community
 7                        Development Director may impose conditions on the design and setbacks of such
 8                        walls if neededto mitigate impacts;

               e.   The retaining wall and/or fence is constructed between lots under the same ownership;

10             f.   Permissionto exceedthe height limits within the setback is granted in writing from the
11                  abutting property owner;

12                  The retaining wall and/or fence is abutting commercial or industrial zoned property or
13                  legally permitted nonresidential uses;

14             h.   Non-sight-obscuringfences such as chainlink or wrought iron seven (7) feet highor less
15                  and sight-obscuringfences forty-two (42) inches high on top of retaining walls no greater
16                  than four (4) feet tail are allowed within setbacks per Figure 40. 320.010-8.

17        5.   Building codes for retainingwalls may require setbacksthat are greaterthan those required by
18             Section 40. 320. 010(F)(4).
19        6.   These height and setback limitations do not apply to fences required by state law to enclose
20             public utilities, or to chain link fences enclosing school grounds or public recreation areas.

23   G. Timing, Selection,Installation, andMaintenanceStandards.
24        1.   Timing. That applicant shall install landscaping and screening required by this section
25             consistent with the approved site plan or an approved modification thereto before the
26             county issues an occupancy permit or final inspection for the development in question;



                                                                                                            176
 1        provided, the responsible official may defer installation of plant materials for up to six
 2        (6) months after the county issues an occupancy permit or final inspection for the
 3        development in question if the responsible official finds doing so increases the likely
 4        survival of plants.

 5   2.   Shrubs and Groundcover Selection. All required groundcover plants and shrubs must be
 6        of sufficient size and number to meet the required standards within three (3) years of
 7        planting. Mulch (as a groundcover) must be confined to areas underneath plants and is not
          a substitutefor livinggroundcoverplants, lawn or approvedflowers.
 9         a.    Shrubs shall be supplied in a minimum of three (3) gallon containers or equivalent
10               burlap balls, with a minimum spread of eighteen (18) inches to meet the L2 buffer
11               requirement, andminimum of five (5) gallon containers or equivalentburlapballs
12               with a minimum spread of thirty (30) inches to meet the L3 buffer requirements.
13               Reduction in the minimum size may be permitted if certified by a registered
14               landscape architect that the reduction shall not diminish the intended effect or the
15               likelihood the plants will survive.

16         b.    Groundcover plants shall be placednot more thanthirty (30) inches on center and
17               thirty (30) inches between rows. Rows of plants shall be staggered for a more
18               effective covering. Groundcover shall be supplied in a minimum four (4) inch size
19               container or a two and one-quarter (2%)
20               inch container or equivalent if planted eighteen (18) inches on center. Reduction in the
21               minimum size may be pennitted if certified by a registered landscape architect that the
22               reduction shall not diminish the intended effect or the likelihood the plants will survive.
23               A lawn or flower bed of flowers approved by the responsible official may be substituted
24               for groundcover plants.

25   3.   Tree Selection. Trees may be deciduous or evergreen. The required tree height shall be measured
26        from the ground level at final planting to the top of the tree.

27        a.    Required trees for parking and loading areas shall be a minimum caliper of two (2) inches
28              and a minimum height often (10) feet at the time of planting.

29        b.    Requireddeciduoustrees (otherthanstreettrees) shallbe fully branched,havea minimum
30              caliper of one and one-half(l Vi) inches, and a minimum height of eight (8) feet at the time
31              of planting.

32        c.    Required evergreen trees (other than sti-eet trees) shall be fully branched and a minimum
33              of six (6) feet high at the time of planting.

34        d.    If the responsible official decides reducing the minimum size oftrees will not detract from
35              the desired effect of the trees, the minimum size of trees (other than street trees) may be
36              reduced if the applicant submits a written statement by a landscape architect registered in
37              Washington or expert in the growing of the tree(s) in question certifying that the reduction
38              in size at planting will not decrease the likelihood the trees will survive.

39        e.    See Section 40. 320. 010(E)(4) regarding trees in landscape islands in parking lots.

40        f.    See Section 40.320.020 regarding street trees and vegetation in the right-of-way. Such
41              requiredtrees andvegetationmaybedeterminedbytheresponsibleofficialtomeetaspects
42              of the landscape buffer requirements of this section.




                                                                                                        177
 1        4.    Selection Generally. Landscapematerials should be selected and sited to produce a hardyand
 2              drought-resistantlandscapearea. Selectionshouldinclude considerationof soil type and depth,
 3              the amount ofmaintenancerequired, spacing, exposureto sun and wind, the slope and contours
 4              of the site, compatibility with existing native vegetation preserved on the site, water
 5              conservation where needed, and the impact of landscaping on visibility of the site for purposes
 6              ofpublic safety andsurveillance. Landscapingmaterials shall be selected in accordancewith a
 7              list of plant materials found in the Standard Details Manual.

          5.    Installation Standards. The applicant shall show and comply with the following:

                a.      Plant materials will be installed to cun-ent nursery industry standards.
10               b.     Plantmaterials shall be properly supported to ensure survival. Supportdevicessuch
11                      as guy wires or stakes shall not interfere with vehicular or pedestrian movement.

12               c.     Existing trees and plant materials to be retained shall be protected during
13                      construction, such as by use of chain link or other sturdy fence placed at the dripline
14                      of trees to be retained. Grading, topsoil storage, construction material storage,
15                      vehicles and equipment shall not be allowed within the dripline of trees to be
16                      retained.

17        6.    Maintenance. Maintenance of landscaped areas is the ongoing responsibility of the
18              property owner. Required landscaping must be continuously maintained in a healthy
19              manner. Plants that die must be replaced with in-kind materials unless otherwise
20              authorized by the responsible official. Vegetation shall be controlled by pruning,
21              trimming or otherwise so that it will not interfere with the maintenance or repair of any
22              public utility, restrict pedestrian or vehicular access, or obstruct sight distance at
23              intersections as provided in Section 40.320.020.

24   H.   Plant List.
25        The county shall maintaina plant list to assistm administrationofthis chapter(seethe
26        Standard Details Manual).

27   I.   Verification of the Installation of Required Landscape.
28        Prior to the issuance of an approval of occupancy for a site plan, the applicant shall provide
29        verification in accordance with Section 40. 320. 030(B) that the required landscape has been
30        installed in accordance with the approved landscape plan(s).

 4   40. 340. 010 Parking and Loading Standards

     A.    General.

 6         1.   Applicability. Except as otherwise provided by the UDC, required off-street parking and loading spaces
 7              shall be improved and maintained as set forth in this section for all uses in all zoning districts.

 8         2.   Timing. Parking and loading spaces required for a given use or development shall be provided
 9              consistent with the approved site plan before the county issues an occupancy permit or final inspection
10              for the use or development in question.

11         3.   Availability.
12              a.    Parking spaces required for a given use or development shall be available for the parking of
13                    operable passenger automobiles of residents, customers, patrons and employees only, and shall
14                    not be used for the storage ofvehicles or materials, or for the oarkine oftrucks used in conducting
15                    the business or use.

16              b.    Loading spaces required for a given use or development shall be available for loading and
17                    unloading oftrucks and similar vehicles.

18              c.    Required off-street parking spaces may not be used for loading or unloading unless the responsible
19                    official finds that loading and unloading in those spaces will occur during hours of the day when
20                    the spaces are not needed for parking.

21         4    LocationofParkingandLoadingFacilities.
22              a.    Off-street parking spaces for dwellings shall be located on the same lot as the dwelling.

23              b.    Off-streetparking spaces for other uses shall be located on the same lot as the use or on another
24                    lot not more than three hundred (300) feet from the building or use they are intended to serve,
25                    measured in a straight line from the building; provided, where required parking for a use or
26                    development will be located on a lot other than the lot on which the use it serves is located, then,
27                    before the county issues a building permit for the use or development, the applicant shall submit
28                    an agreement executed by the owner of the lot where the off-site parking is proposed authorizing
29                    use of the lot for the proposed parking spaces for the use in question.

30              c.    Loading spaces for a given use shall be situated on the same lot as the use it serves, except as
31                    provided for joint use of facilities.

32              d.    Parking andloadingspaces shall not be located in a required front setback, except in the case of
33                    single-family or duplex dwellings. Access drives and maneuvering areas for parking and loading
34                    spaces shall not be located in a required front setback, except to the minimum extent




                                                                                                                   181
                practicable for access to the site.
 2        c.    Parkin   s ciccs for residenlia) develo menl ma    be arran ed in landem \\ith    l\\o   or more \ chicles
 3              one in from of or behind the others \\ilti a sin Ie means ot'in re.ss and e res'' al iheraie oi'one s ace
 4              t'ureve t\\en     20 linear feet \\ hh an neces.s      rovi.siuns fur lurnin radius.

 5   5.   Joint Use of Facilities. The responsible official may authorize use of given off-street parking
 6        and/or loading spaces by two (2) or more uses if:

 7         a.    The responsible official finds the applicant has shown that the uses that share the parking
 8               and/or loading do not require that parking and/or loading at the same time; and

 9        b.    Where sharedparking or loading for a use or development will be located on a lot other
10              than the lot on whichthe use it serves is located, then, before the county issues a building
11              permit for the uses or developments on one (1) lot that will be served by the shared parking
12              and/or loading on another lot, the applicant shall submit an agreement executed by the
13              owner of the off-site lot where parking and/or loading is proposed authorizing use of the
14              lot for the proposed parking and/or loading spaces for the use or development in question
15              subject to tenns and conditions that assure parking and/or loading will be available for
16              each use it serves as provided in Section 40. 340. 010(A)(5)(a).

17   6.    Change or Expansionof a Use. A site plan that changesthe use of an existing sti-ucture or lot
18         shall provide off-street parking and loading for the new use as required by this section. A site
19         plan that enlarges an existing stmcture or use shall provide for additionalparkingandloading
20         based only on the parking and loading requirements of the expansion, except as otherwise
21         providedin Chapter40.530.
22   7.    Lighting.Lightfixtures in parkingor loadingareasshall be consistentwithRCW47.36.180 on
23         public roadways and not cast significant light or glare off site on adjacent properties.

24   8.    Surfacing. All parking and loading spaces and related access drives, maneuvering, and vehicle
25         storage areas shall be paved to standards, including the use of permeable pavements and rass
26         block avers, as approved by the responsible official except as follows:

27         a.    Driveways leading to parking and maneuvering areas for unoccupied utility and wireless
28               communication facilities need not be paved, except as required by Section 40. 350. 030(B)
29               (7)(c) (this still requires the first twenty (20) to twenty-five (25) feet of driveway to be
30               paved so gravel does not enter the paved road);

31         b.    Three (3) or fewer parking spaces serving unoccupied utility and wireless communication
32               facilities need not be paved;

33         c.    Transitional uses such as coffee and food stands approved under Section 40. 260. 055;

34         d.    Driveways used only for fire access purposes;
35         e.    Parking areas for uses that receive access from unpaved roads;

36         f.    Reuse of existing legally placed parking areas in the rural area of the county; provided, that
37               proposed new parking and required ADA parking spaces and ADA accessways are paved;

38         g.    Agricultural stands and markets as allowed under Section 40. 260. 025;

39         h.    Rural kennels as allowed under Section 40.260. 110;




                                                                                                                  182
                i.    Wineries as allowed under Section 40. 260. 245; ami

 2              j.    I'se ofexistin Ie all     laced arkin areas for residential develo ment u to a maximum of six
 3                    (6) parking spaces: and
 4
 5              jk. Other uses as approved by the responsible official.

 6        9.    Drainage. Stormwater drainage facilities for parking and loading spaces and related access drives and
 7              maneuvering areas shall comply with Chapter 40. 386.

          10. WheelStops andCurbs.
 9              a.    Parking and/or loading spaces on the perimeter of a parking lot or abutting interior landscaped
10                    areas or sidewalks shall include a wheel stop or curb at least four (4) inches high located three
11                    (3) feet back from the front of the parking and/or loading space. Include breaks in curbs, as
12                    necessary, to allow flow of stormwater to LID stormwater facilities.

13              b.    The front three (3) feet of a parking space may be improved with a low-growing vegetated LID
14                    feature, insteadofasphaltor concretepavement. However,this areashallnotbe countedtoward
15                    landscape or open space area requirements unless it is part of a LID storm-water feature.

16              c.    The perimeter of a parking or loading area and access and maneuvering drives associated with
17                   them shall be improved with a curb, rail or equivalent so that vehicles do not extend over a
18                   property line, sidewalk or public or private street. Breaks must be provided along the perimeter
19                   feature to allow stormwater movement to LID stormwater features.

20         11. Maintenance of Parking and Loading Areas. Required parking and loading spaces and associated
21             access and maneuvering drives shall be maintained in good repair at all times.

22   B.    Calculation of Parking Requirements.
23         A site plan for a givenuse or uses shall showthat at leastthe numberofparkingspacesrequiredby
24         Table40.340.010-4will beprovidedconsistentwiththis section unless otherwiseconsistentwiththe
25         UDC.
26         1.   Where Table 40.340.010-4 requires a certain number of parking spaces based on the area of a
27              building, the area shall be the gross floor area of the structure, as defined in Section 40. 100. 070
28              ("floor area, gross") excluding the area of a building that encloses parking or loading spaces.
29        2.    Where more than one (1) use occupies a given structure or parcel of land or where a given
30              business includes a combination ofuses, the minimum required number ofparking spaces shall
31              be the sum of the requirements for each use, except to the extent the uses comply with the
32              requirements of this section for shared parking.

33        3.    Where a building may be used for more than one (1) purpose and the applicant does not limit
34              the permitted uses in the building, parking spaces shall be provided based on the possible use(s)
35              that require the most parking spaces.

36        4.    Where Table 40. 340. 010-4 does not list the parking requirements for a proposed use, the
37              responsible official shall determine the minimum parking requirements for the use, based on
38              requirements in Table 40.340.010-4 for other similar uses, if any, or on substantial evidence
39              of parking needs for similar uses in other, similar locations.

40        5     Up to thirty percent (30%) of required parking spaces and all parking spaces in excess of
41              minimum requirements may comply with the standards for compact cars in Table 40. 340. 010-



                                                                                                                    183
                 5.

 2        6.    All parking areas shall comply with applicable local, state and federal standards regarding
 3              parking for disabledpersons.

 4        7.    The review authority may reduce the required number of parking spaces to less than that
 5              requiredin Table40.340.010-4aspart ofsiteplanreviewapplicationor other applicationif the
 6              reviewauthorityfindsthat a lessernumberofoff-streetparkingspaceswill beenoughto fulfill
 7              all parking needs of the use or development, based on substantial evidence in the application,
 8              such as an adequate survey of parking demand at similar uses under similar conditions. The
 9              number of parking spaces for disabled persons may not be reduced under this subsection.

10        8.    The review authority may allow parking credits established through a neighborhood parking
11              plan adopted pursuant to Section 40. 340. 030 to meet the number of spaces required by Table
12              40. 340.010-4. The number of credits for an individual parcel shall be as established in the
13              approved neighborhood parking plan.

14   C. ParkingDesignStandards. Off-streetparking spaces shall comply withthe standards for stalls and
15        aisles, as set forth in Table 40. 340. 010-5 andFigure 40.340.010-1

17        1.    Commercial, industrial and public utility buildings that have a gross floor area of five thousand

       3.        LoadingBerthDesignStandards.
 2              a.       All required loading berths shall meet the minimum dimensional standards shown in Table
 3                       40. 340.010-3. Where the vehicles generally used for loading and unloading exceed these
 4                       dimensions, the required length of these berths shall be increased.

 5              b.       In additionto the length ofthe loadingberth, additionalmaneuveringspacemayberequiredby
 6                      the director for any uses with over ten thousand (10, 000) square feet of gross floor area with
 7                      loading berth access from a collector arterial or local access street, especially if located across
 8                      the street from another high-demand use. When required, the additional maneuvering space
 9                      shall be designed and arranged to allow the most efficient use of all required loading berths by
10                      motor vehicles of the types typically employed by the activities served. The minimum
11                      maneuvering width between the outermost point of the loading berth and the limit of the
12                      maneuvering area shall be fifty (50) feet.

13              c.      Loading spaces shall be located so that trucks shall not obstruct pedestrian or vehicle traffic
14                      movement or project into any public right-of-way. All loading space areas shall be separated
15                      from parking areas and shall be designatedas truck loading spaces.

16         4.        Loadingberths on the perimeter ofthe site shall be screenedto a minimum L4
17               landscape standards. If the adjoiningproperty is within an industrial or commercial district
18               and contains similar uses that are compatible with loading facilities then this standards may
19               not be required. Any loading space located within one hundred (100) feet of areas zoned for
20               residential use shall be screened and operated as necessary to reduce noise and visual
21               impacts. Noise mitigation measures may include architectural or structural barriers, beams,
22               walls, or restrictions on the hours of operation.

23         5.    The review authority may reduce the number or size ofrequired loading spaces to less than that
24               required as part of site plan review or other application if the review authority finds that a
25               lesser number or size of loading spaces will be adequate to serve the expected needs of the
26               development.

 1   EXHIBIT 28
 2
 3   40.350. 030 Street and Road Standards

            A.    Overview.

 5                1.   Purpose. It is the purpose of this section to establishminimum standards for public and
 6                     private transportation facilities for vehicles, public transit, pedestrians, and bicycles,
 7                     hereafter constructed or improved as a condition of county approval of a development, or
 8                     a transportation project constructed by the county. These standards are intended to preserve
 9                     the community's quality of life and to minimize total costs over the life of the
10                     transportation facility.

11                2.   Applicability. This section applies to any subdivision, short plat, site plan application, or
12                     conditional use permit; provided, that for the purposes of Sections 40. 350. 030(B)(4) and
13                     (B)(8), it shall also apply to applications for building permits or other applications for
14                     access to a public road, or to projects within the public right-of-way. Unoccupied utility
15                     and wireless communication facilities shall only be subject to the provisions of Sections
16                     40. 350.030(B)(4)(c) - (e) and (B)(8). Private bridges are addressedin Section 40.350.040.

17                3.   Relationshipto ComprehensivePlan.
18                     a.     dark County is required by RCW 36. 70A. 040(3) to ensure that any development
19                            regulations adopted subsequent to the comprehensive plan "... are consistent with and
20                            implement the comprehensive plan..."

21                     b.     This section is consistent with and implements the goals and policies listed in the
22                            comprehensiveplan. Particular attentionhas beenpaid to Chapter 5, Transportation
23                            Element.

24                     c.     Interpretations of this section shall be consistent with the effective Arterial Atlas. The
25                            Arterial Atlas identifies all arterials and collectors and specifies the design of these
26                            facilities in general tenns.

27                     d.     This section implements the pedestrian and bikeways system plan and the Arterial
28                            Atlas. The atlas requires pedestrianand/orbicycle facilitiesto be included as part of
29                            certain arterial and collector road cross-sections where the pedestrian and bikeways
30                            system plan indicates such facilities are to be located. This section requires the
31                            inclusionofpedestrianandbikewayfacilitiesin frontageimprovements basedon the
32                            functional classification adopted in the Arterial Atlas.

33                4.    Functional Classifications- Purpose. The purpose of a functional classification system
34                      for county roads is to define varying levels and types of transportation facilities that
35                      provide for the safe and efficient movement of people and goods, while preserving
36                      residential areas and maintaining the economic vitality of commercial and industrial
37                      areas. The system classifies transportation facilities as either urban or rural roads. Both
38                      urban and rural roads are further divided into arterials, collectors, and access roads. There
39                      is also an urban commercial/industrial category of roads. Existing and proposed arterials
40                      and collectors are shown on the current Arterial Atlas. The county's functional
41                      classification system for arterials is intended to be in compliance with the federal
42                      classificationsystem.

43                5    Functional Classifications - Urban Roads. Urban roads are classified as outlined below:




                                                                                                                           190
     a,   Arterials.

 2        (1) Parkway Arterial. "Parkway arterial" is the highest classification within the county's
 3            functional classification system. The purpose of this county road is to carry high
 4            volumes oftraffic through the urban area and between major regional activity centers.
 5            This class of roadway is of primary importance in the regional transportation system
 6            as it carries a high proportion of the total urban-area travel. Access is normally
 7            limited to intersections with other arterials. Direct land access is prohibited.

 8        (2) Principal Arterial. "Principal arterial" is the basic element of the county's road
 9              system. All other functional classifications supplement the principal arterial network.
10              It carries large volumes of traffic over long distances. Access is generally limited to
11              intersections with other arterials and collectors. Signalized intersection spacing is
12              regulated. Direct land access is minimal and managed, but is less restrictive than
13              access from parkway arterials. Spacing is typically two (2) to five (5) miles.

14        (3) Minor Arterial. "Minor arterial" collects and distributes traffic from principal
15              arterials to streets of lower classificationsandmay allow for traffic to directly access
16              destinations. Minor arterials provide for movement within subareas of the county,
17              whose boundaries are largely defined by principal arterial roadways. They serve
18              through traffic and provide direct access to large commercial, industrial, office and
19              multifamily development but, generally, not to single-family residential properties.
20              Spacing is typically less than two (2) miles.

21   b.   Collectors - Urban Collector. "Urban collector" provides for land access and traffic
22        circulation within and betweenresidential neighborhoods, and commercial and industrial
23        areas. The collector street also collects traffic from local streets and channels it into the
24        arterial system. Direct access to adjacent land uses, however, is still subordinate to traffic
25        movement. Access to abutting properties is controlled through driveway spacing and
26        pavement markings. Typically, collectors are not continuous for any great length,
27        nor do they form a connected network by themselves. Spacing is typically less than
28        two (2) miles.

29   c.   Access Roads.

30        (1) Neighborhood Circulator. "Neighborhood circulator" serves to distribute
31             traffic from collectors and provides direct access for abutting properties.
32             Throughtrips are discouraged and parking is allowed. In general, these streets
33             connect local access streets to collectors.

34        (2) Local Access. "Local access" streets provide direct access to adjoining
35             properties within a neighborhood. Through trips are discouraged and parking
36             is usually allowed. In general, these streets do not directly connect to arterials
37             or collectors.

38        (3) Short Cul-de-Sac. "Short cul-de-sac" streets are a maximum one hundred fifty
39            (150) feet in length and serve no more than eighteen
40            (18) dwelling units. Parking is allowed. The use of shared driveways off of short
41            cul-de-sacs requires Fire Marshal approval.

42        (4) Alley. "Alley"streetsaresecondaryaccessestothebacksideoflots. Thisallows
43             streets at the front of properties not to be encumbered with driveways. Alleys




                                                                                                    191
                     are analternative to frontage access.Parking is not allowed.

 2        d.   Urban Commercial/Industrial.      "Urban commercial/industrial"      streets serve to
 3             distribute traffic from arterials and provide direct access to abutting commercial or
 4             industrial properties. Through trips are discouraged and parking is optional. Bike
 5             lanes may be required when the projected average daily trips exceed three thousand
 6             (3, 000).
 7             A "storefront street" is a pedestrian-oriented street type that can be used in Mixed-
 8             Use andHighway 99 commercial districts where building setbacks are zero (0) to
 9             five (5) feet. Drive-way access is not permitted; wide sidewalks, curb bulb-outs
10             and parking are required.

11   6.   Functional Classifications - Rural Roads. Rural roads are classified as follows:

12        a.   Rural Arterial. "Rural arterial"roads are rural extensions ofurbanprincipal arterials
13             and some urban minor arterials. They provide adequate right-of-way for future urban
14             arterial routes. The provision of land access remains subordinate to providing for
15             traffic movement. Parking is not allowed,

16        b.   Collectors.

17             (1) Rural Major Collector. "Rural major collector" roads are rural extensions
18                  ofurbanminor arterials and some urbancollectors. Theirprimary purpose is to
19                  link rural centers with nearby towns and cities and with state arterial routes.
20                  The provision of land access remains subordinate to providing for traffic
21                  movement. Parking is not allowed.

22             (2) Rural Minor Collector. "Rural minor collector" roads connect local traffic to rural
23                   major collectors and state arterial routes and may be rural extensions of urban minor
24                   arterials or urban collectors. They are spaced so as to be accessible to all developed
25                   areas within the county. The provision of land access is given the same priority as the
26                   provision oftrafficmovement. Parkingis not allowed.
27        c.   Access Roads.

28             (1) Rural Local Access. "Rural local access" roads provide access from parcels to the
29                   rural collector system. Parking is not allowed.

30   7.   Scenic Routes.

31        a.   Scenic routes are roadwayswith unique scenic or historical features, officially designated
32             by Council. Scenic routes seek to enhance, preserve and facilitate the enjoyment of those
33             scenic or historical features unique to eachroute.

34        b.   Scenic route design may allow reduced design speed and modified roadway and right-of-
35             way widths to preserve naturally occurring scenic beauty unique to the location of the
36             route. When possible, existing alignment and roadway sections shall be used. Special
37             features, such as vehicle turnouts for vista areas or bicycle/pedestrian facilities, may be
38             provided. Urban or rural collector standards shall be used for right-of-way and roadway
39             sections. The Public Works Director may modify the standards to accommodate unique
40             scenic or historic design considerations.

41        c.   A traffic analysis to determine the impacts on arterials, collectors, and access roads shall




                                                                                                       192
                      be completed prior to designating a facility a scenic route.

 2              Urban Reserve, Urban Holding Areas and Rural Centers. The following are special applications
 3              of the functional classifications. Chapter 5 of the comprehensive plan lists additional
 4              transportation improvements required in specific geographic areas.

 5              a.    New developments permitted outright within the urban holding and urban reserve areas of
 6                    the county shall meet rural road standards, except that the right-of-way for rural local access
 7                    roads shall be a minimum offifty-four
 8                    (54) feet to allow for a future neighborhood circulator street.

 9              b.    Compliance with the urban road standards for right-of-way dedication and frontage
10                    improvements shall be required for:
11                    (1) Conditionaluses in urbanholdingareas;and
12                    (2) Conditional uses that are urban in character, within urban reserve areas.

13              c.    Whereurban frontage improvements are required andthe road to be improved has a
14                    rural classification. Table 40.350. 030-1 shall be used to convert rural classifications
15                    to urban.

16              d.    New developments within rural centers shall meet rural road standards. All public
17                    and private roads shall be paved and constructed with sidewalks.

19   B.   StandardsforDevelopmentReview.
20        1.   Transportation Impact Study. The requirements for a transportation impact study are
21             stated in Section 40.350.020(D).

22        2.   Circulation Plan.

23             a.    Purpose and Applicability. The purpose of this section is to ensure adequate cross-
24                   circulation in a manner which allows subsequent developments to meet these
25                   standards, and to provide a mechanism for integrating various streets into an efficient
26                   and safe transportation network.
27                   Developments that are required to conduct a transportation impact study or
28                   construct frontage improvements shall meet the requirements of this section.

29             b.    InformationRequirementsfora CirculationPlan.Applicantsshallsubmita circulation
30                   plan which includes the subject site and all adjacent parcels. Proposed streets must be
31                   shown to the point of connection with the existing street system within six hundred



                                                                                                               193
 1        (600) feet. The circulation plan shall demonstrate feasibility with development of
 2        adjacent properties, or may revise the off-site portion of prior approved plans.
 3        Circulation plans shall also be consistent with the Arterial Atlas, as amended. A
 4        circulation plan shall be submitted at application. Draft circulation plans may be
 5        submitted at preapplication.
 6        (1) Information Requirements for Developments in Urban Area. Urban circulation plans
 7            shall be schematic in nature and to an engineering scale (e. g., 1" = 100', 1"= 200', I"
 8            = 400'). The plan shouldinclude sufficient off-site and on-site conditionsto evaluate
 9            it against the review criteria. It shall include:

10              (a) Proposedproject boundary;
11              (b) Existing and proposed streets, transit routes and facilities, and other
12                    pedestrian/bicycle destinations within six hundred (600) feet of the project
13                    boundary;

14              (c) Site accesspoints for vehicles, pedestrians,bicycles, andtransit; and
15              (d) Sensitive lands (wetlands, shoreline, geologic hazard, floodplain, etc.), if they
16                   are containedin the county's information package.
17              The circulation plan should be prepared on eight and one-half (8'A) inch by eleven
18              (11) inch (8'/2" x 11") or eleven (11) inch by seventeen (17) inch (11" x 17") or
19              twenty-four (24) inch by thirty-six (36)inch (24"x 36") format, and can be
20              superimposed on the "arterials, C-Tran routes, parks and trails" and "elevation
21              contours" pages provided with the developer's CIS packet. Additional explanation
22              or an additional legend may be required to adequately show proposed on-site
23              facilities.

24        (2) InformationRequirements for Developments in Rural Area. Rural circulation plans
25             shall be schematic in nature and based on the appropriate quarter-section map. The
26             plan should include sufficient on-site and off-site conditions to evaluate it against the
27             review criteria. Rural circulation plans shall include:

28              (a) Proposedproject boundary;
29              (b) Howthe project site connectsto the existing street system;
30             (c) Any arterials identifiedin the Arterial Atlas, as amended, within eight hundred
31                    (800) feet ofthe site.

32   c.   Review Criteria for an Urban Circulation Plan.

33        (1) Cross-Circulation. Cross-circulation shall be provided in a manner that meets these
34             standardsand, wherepossible, thatwill allow subsequentdevelopments to meetthese
35             standards:

36             (a) Block Length. Block lengths shall be between one hundred
37                    (100) to eight hundred (800) feet; provided, that where a block is partially
38                    defined by an arterial or industrial road the block lengths along the arterial
39                    shall be no less than the minimum full
40                    access intersection spacing specified in Table 40. 350. 030-3. For the
41                    purpose of this section, "block length" is the distance between public




                                                                                                   194
                              road intersections as measured along the nearside right-of-way line.

 2                    (b) Block Perimeter. The block perimeter shall not exceed three thousand
 3                            two hundred (3, 200) feet unless accessway(s) for pedestrian or bicycle
 4                            circulation are provided or where topographic or other physical
 5                            constraints preclude achieving this standard.

                (2) Access Street System. The accessstreet system shall:
 7                    (a) Provide convenient parcel access to and from adjacent arterials and/or
 8                            collectors;

                      (b) Be designed to discourage external traffic from short-cutting;
10                    (c) Be designed to discourage vehicular speeds in excess of legal speed
11                            limits;

12                    (d) Be designed for convenient circulation ofinternal traffic without reliance
13                            on the arterial systems;

14                    (e) Supportdirect travel by pedestrians,bicyclists, andtransit users; and
15                    (f) Discourageunnecessarystreets andhardsurfaces.
16   3.   Transportation Design Criteria. The design criteria set out in Tables 40. 350. 030-2 and
17        40.350.030-3 are adopted as a portion ofthe dark County standardspecifications. Such
18        criteria are applicable to roads located within and adjacent to a development. These criteria
19        are intendedfor normal conditions. The responsible officialmay require higherstandards
20        for unusual site conditions.
21        All urbanroads except alleys consist ofa core road section anda flex zone section.
22        a.   The core road consists of the traveled-way portion ofthe road, as well as medians and
23             turning lanes on higher classification roads. Core road features as shown on the
24             Standard Details Manual drawings allow little, if any, variation unless a road
25             modificationrequest is approved.

26              (1) Travel and turning lanes require impervious pavement on all rural roads, and
27                   urban arterials, collectors, industrial/commercial, and neighborhood circulator
28                   roads.
29              (2) Permanent median areas may utilize stormwater low impact development
30                   features including, but not limited to, bioretention swales and permeable
31                   pavement. Such features shall be subject toapproval by the Public Works
32                   Director and shall be designed to ensure adequatepublic safety.
33        b.    The flex zone consists of that portion of the roadway outside of the core road. Flex zone
34              features can include stormwater best management practice features, parking and bike
35              lanes, sidewalks,andplanter andutility strips, dependingonthe roadclassification.These
36              features may be designed with considerable flexibility subject to engineering approval by
37              the county; however, all features applicable to the road classification shall be provided.
38              Some flex zone features may require more right-of-way than is noted in Table 40. 350. 030-
39              2.

40              (1) StonnwaterFeatures. Stormwater low impact development features as found in the



                                                                                                          195
 1        dark County Stormwater Manual are allowed with approval from the Public Works
 2        Director. Stormwater features shall be designed and constructed to ensure adequate
 3        public safety. Right-of-way in excess of that required in Table 40. 350. 030-2 may be
 4        neededto accommodate stormwater features.

          (a) PermeablePavement.Permeablepavementmaybeusedforthe following:
               (i)   Sidewalksandseparatedbike lanes on all road classifications;
 7             (ii) Parking and nonseparated bike lanes of all urban access roads, including
 8                   neighborhood circulators; and

               (iii) Travel lanes ofall urbanaccessroads exceptneighborhoodcirculators.
10             (iv) Prior to acceptance of pemieable pavement surfaces by the county, the
11                  applicant shall sign a Permeable Pavement Testing and Acceptance
12                  Agreement in a form acceptable by the Public Works Director.

13       (b) Infiltration Systems. Infiltration systems such as bioretention systems, rain
14             gardens and infiltration planters are subject to the following limitations:

15             (i)   Infiltration from stormwater features shall not threaten the safety or
16                   integrity of underground utilities. The Public Works Director shall require
17                   that infiltration features located in close proximity to utilities be designed
18                   to accommodate the utilities;

19             (ii) Infiltration from stonnwater features shall not adversely affect the
20                   function and integrity of the core road.

21   (2) On-Street Parking. Where required, on-street parking shall be
22       subjectto the following:

23        (a) Angled "head-in"parking may be allowed with approval of the Public
24             Works Director; provided, that backing over sidewalks or bicycle lanes
25             is prohibited;

26        (b) Curb extensions containing bioretention facilities are allowed in parking
27             lanes subject to the following:

28             (i)   Curb extensions shall not extend more than seven (7) feet into the
29                   parking lane, and shall not interrupt required bike lanes.

30             (ii) On commercial/industrial roads, the length of mid-block curb
31                   extensions is limited to twenty percent (20%) of the curb length of
32                   a block, or in the case of a partial block development, the road
33                   frontage of the site.

34             (iii) Mid-blockcurbextensionsareprohibitedonarterials,collectors and
35                   store-front roads.

36            (iv) Mid-block curb extensions on all urban access roads, including
37                   neighborhoodcirculators, are allowedwithrestrictions inresidential
38                   subdivisionsunder one (1) ofthe following options:




                                                                                             196
 1                      [a] Curb extensions can occupy up to ten percent ( 10%) ofthe curb
 2                           length of a block, or in the case of a partial block development,
 3                           the road frontage of the site;
 4                      [b] Curb extensions can occupy up to twenty-five percent (25%)
 5                           of the curb length of a block, or in the case of a partial block
 6                           development, the road frontage of the site; provided, that the
 7                           applicant demonstrates on the plat that fifty (50) foot long two
 8                           (2) waypassingopportunities are providedat onehundredfifty
 9                           (150) foot maximum intervals along the roadway for urban
10                           local access roads. Acceptable methods include the use of
11                           abutting driveways, twenty (20) foot wide travel-way clear
12                           zones, or other methods as may be approved by the Public
13                           Works Director; or

14                      [c] Curb extensions can occupy more than twenty-five percent
15                           (25%) of the block length; provided, that the applicant
16                           demonstrates on the plat that at least one (1) on-street parking
17                           space per lot is maintained. Single on-street parallel parking
18                           spaces between driveways shall be at least seventeen (17) feet
19                           in length, measured from five (5) feet from the top ofthe slope
20                           of the driveway wing or end of the curb radius. Two
21                           (2) or more consecutive on-street parallel parking spaces shall
22                           require two (2) additional feet between each space.

23            (v) Curb extensions up to fifty (50) feet long are allowed at intersections of
24                     local access roads and short cul-de-sacs, or combinations thereof. Curb
25                     extensions at intersections may be required for storefront streets, subject
26                     to Section
27                     9. 1. 2 of the Highway 99 Overlay District Standards in Appendix F to
28                     Title 40, or for mixed-use developments. Such extensions shall not count
29                     toward the mid-block percentage requirements in this subsection.
30                     Approval of such curb extensions shall require demonstration of adequate
31                     vehicular turning movement capability.

32            (vi) Measurements. The lengthofmid-blockcurbextensionsshallbemeasured
33                     betweenthe tangents ofthe standardcurb. The length of curb extensions at
34                     intersections shall be measured between the tangent of the standard curb
35                     and the face of the curb of the intersecting street. "Block length" is the
36                     distance between road intersections as measured along the nearside right-
37                     of-way or easement line.

38   (3) Bike Lanes.Bike lanes, whenrequiredby the road classification, are subject to the
39        following:

40        (a) Bike lanes may abut travel lanes or be located outside of the curb line,
41            depending on the feasibility of existing and future connections.

42        (b) Whencombinedwithpedestrianpaths,the combinedwidth shallbe a minimum
43             often (10) feet wide.

44        (c) Bike lanes shall be continuous through a development, i.e., cannot be



                                                                                                 197
                interrupted by curb extensionsor other features.
 2   (4) Sidewalks. Sidewalksshall be provided on both sides of all public streets in urban
 3        areas and within rural centers, subject to the following:

 4        (a) Attached and detached sidewalk widths shall be as noted in Table 40. 350. 030-
 5            2.

 6        (b) Whencombinedwithbike lanes, the combined widthshall be at least ten (10)
 7              feet.

 8        (c)   Sidewalks shall be continuous through a development, i. e., cannot be
 9              interrupted by curb extensionsor other features.
10        (d) Other than for sidewalks in rural center zones where the standard right-of-way
11            dedication is insufficient, sidewalks may be allowed within easements only
12              where it is demonstrated that such sidewalks cannot be located within the
13              established public right-of-way or private roadway tract due to natural features
14              (e.g., significanttrees, rock outcroppings, steep topography, etc.) that should be
15              preserved, or irregular lot configuration.

16   (5) Utilities. For new developments, the Public Works Director shall require that
17       underground utilities be designed to accommodate infiltration features that are in close
18       proximity to the utilities.

19   (6) Curbandgutter, whenrequired,shallbea minimum ofeighteen(18) incheswide.When
20       curbs are required, vertical curb is required for all roads except for cul-de-sac bulbs.
