                                                                                                                                                                                                               202
     4.   AccessManagement.
 2        a.   Applicability. As noted in Section 40. 350. 030(A)(2), this subsection also applies to
 3             applications for building pennits and applications for access to public roads.
 4             Additionally, paving of driveways in the urban and rural areas shall follow the
 5             requirements of Section 40. 350. 030(B)(7)(c).

 6        b.   Accessto Local Access Roads.

 7             (1) Driveway Spacing.
 8                    (a) Excepting the bulbs of cul-de-sacs, driveways providing access onto
 9                         nonarterial and noncollector streets serving single-family or duplex
10                         residential structures shall be located a minimum of five (5) feet from an
11                         interior side property line or zero (0) feet as a shared driveway approach.
12                         Where two (2) driveways are permitted, a minimum separation of fifty
13                         (50) feet shall be required between the driveways, measured from near
14                         edge to near edge.

15                   (b) Comer lot driveways shall be a minimum of forty (40) feet from the
16                         projected curb line or edge of pavement, as measured to the nearest edge
17                         of the driveway, or in cases where this may be impractical, the driveway
18                         may be limited to twenty (20) feet in width and located five (5) feet from
19                         the property line away from the intersection or as a twenty-five (25) foot
20                         wide shared driveway at this property line. Where a residential corner lot
21                         is located at the intersection ofanonarterial or noncollector street with an
22                         arterial street, the comer clearance requirements of Section
23                         40. 350. 030(B)(4)(c)(2)(f) shall apply to the nonarterial or noncollector
24                         street.


25                   (c) Existingflag lots andjoint driveways serving betweentwo (2) and four
26                         (4) lots are exempt from the requirements of this subsection.

27                   (d) Nonresidential driveways are prohibited from taking access from an
28                         urban access road as defined in Table 40. 350. 030-2 unless no access
29                         exists or can be provided to a collector.

30             (2) Driveway Number and Width. A maximum of two (2) driveways may be
31                  pennitted to a residential lot or individual duplex unit meeting the spacing
32                  requirements of Section 40. 350. 030(B)(4)(b). Joint-use driveways may be
33                  allowed and will count as a driveway for each residential lot or duplex unit. For
34                  a joint-use driveway, a minimum of a twenty (20) foot wide easement is
35                  required. Driveways shall have a minimum width of twelve (12) feet of clear,
36                  unobstructed all-weather driving surface and an overhead clearance of thirteen
37                  (13) feet, six (6) inches. The first, or only, driveway shall be twelve (12) feet to
38                  thirty-five (35) feet in width. If a second driveway is allowed, the maximum
39                  width of the second driveway will be fifteen (15) feet.

40             (3) Driveway Length.

41                  (a) All portions of shared driveways off of short cul-de-sac roads shall be within
42                      three hundred (300) feet of the intersection of the short cul-de-sac with a higher
43                      classified road as measured to the intersecting street's face of curb; provided,



                                                                                                       203
              that such driveways are subject to Fire Marshal approval.

 2       (b) All new modified or extended driveways longer than tltree hundred (300) feet
 3           shall be providedwithan approvedturnaround at the terminus. There shall also
 4            be approved turnouts constructed such that the maximum distance from turnout
 5            to turnout, or from turnout to turnarounds, does not exceed five hundred (500)
 6            feet. Turnouts shall comply withthe StandardDetails Manual. Construction of
 7            roads and driveways within the wild land urban interface/intermix area shall
 8            conform to Section 15. 13.030.

     (4) Maximum DwellingUnits Servedby AccessRoads.
10        (a) No road may serve more than one hundred (100) lots or dwellingunits unless
11            thatroadis connectedby a secondvehicle accessroadto the same"feeder"road
12             at a different location, or to another "feeder"road that functions at a level equal
13             to at least an urban local residential access road or a rural local access road. The
14             second vehicle access road may be a county emergency-only access road, if it
15             serves less than two hundred
16             (200) lots or dwelling units.
17             The second access may be satisfied through the use of an existing roadway
18             network in the existing adjacent neighborhood if:

19             (i)     An existing road was previously stubbed indicating intent for future
20                     access; or

21             (ii) An easement has been dedicated specifically for such purpose, and a
22                  roadwayhasbeenbuiltto countystandardsor will be constructedwiththe
23                     development to county standards.
24                     In either case, the increase in traffic volume on the existing roadway
25                     network must not cause the traffic volume to exceed the design volume
26                     of the existing roadway network.
27        (b) Urbanneighborhoodcirculator roads within a development whichmeet
28             the access requirements above may serve up to three hundred (300) lots
29             or units if approved by the review authority. However, the review
30             authority may require a traffic circulation study showing a balanced
31             traffic flow of less than two thousand (2, 000) vehicles per day past any
32             dwelling unit accessing on a neighborhood circulator road or lesser
33             classification upon full buildout.

34        (c) When required emergency or regular secondary access roads cannot be
35            installed due to location on property, topography, waterways,
36            nonnegotiable grades or other similar conditions, the County Fire
37            Marshal may require additional fire protection as specified in Title 15.

38        (d) Thestandardscontainedin Section40.350.030(B)(4)(b)(4) arewaivedin
39             their entirety for developments in rural areas.

40   (5) Exceptions.

41       (a) The review authority may grant an exception to the requirements of
42            Section 40. 350.030(B)(4)(b)(3) to extendthe maximum distancebetween
43            turnouts/turn-arounds or allow other appropriate relief where it is




                                                                                            204
 1                   impractical or excessively costly to meet these requirements due to
 2                   topography, sensitive areas, natural features, or where application ofthese
 3                   standards would be disproportional.

 4              (b) The review authority may grant an exception to the requirements of
 5                  Section 40. 350.030(B)(4)(b)(4)(a) in the case of a subdivision with more
 6                  than one (1) phase, when it can be shownthat the other necessary access
 7                  roads will be constructed in a future phase of the same subdivision. Street
                    stubs built to the property line of property not under the developer's
                    control do not qualify for such an exception.

10   c.   Access to Collectors.

11        (1) In order to limit the number of residential roads intersecting with collectors
12              while providing adequate neighborhood circulation, residential roads
13              intersecting with collectors shall be classified and constructed to standards
14              applicable to local residential access roads unless the review authority finds
15              that a lesser classification adequately provides for the circulation needs of the
16              surrounding area. Road approach permits not associated with development
17              shall be reviewed using a Type I process.

18        (2) Driveways.
19             (a) Urban Collectors. No residential driveways in the urban area
20                   will be permitted to access collectors unless no other access to the site exists or
21                   can be made available.

22             (b) Rural Collectors. Residentialdrivewaysin therural areawill not bepermittedto
23                   access collectors if direct lot access is available to an existing rural access road
24                   as defined in Tables 40.350.030-2 and 40.350.030-3.

25             (c) Spacing. When driveways on collectors are permitted, they shall be spaced in
26                   accordance with Table 40.350.030-4. The distance between adjacent one (1)
27                   way driveways with the inbound drive upstream from the outbound drive may
28                   be one-half (!/2) the distance shown. Where raised channelization exists, only
29                   those driveways on the development side of the road will be considered for
30                   mimmum separation requirements.

31             (d) Number of Driveways. The number of driveways and driveway lanes shall be
32                 based upon an estimate of site traffic generation in accordance with Table
33                 40.350.030-5. Multiple driveways are not permitted until the estimated ADT
34                 exceeds the number shown in the second column for the different types of land
35                 use. Then, an additional driveway is allowed each time the estimated ADT
36                 increases above the previous maximum ADT for each driveway as shown in the
37                 third column; provided, that the additional driveways meet the spacing
38                 requirements specified in Table 40. 350. 030-4. Two (2) driveway exit lanes are
39                 allowed when the ADT exceeds seven hundred (700).

40              (e) Width. A single-family residential driveway onto a collector shall be fifteen
41                   (15) to thirty-five (35) feet in width; provided, that ajoint-use driveway serving
42                   two (2) residential lots shall not exceed thirty-six (36) feet in width. A
43                   nonresidential two




                                                                                                  205
 1                     (2) way driveway onto a collector shall be twenty-four (24) to forty (40) feet
 2                     in width.

 3              (f)    Corner Clearance. To provide adequate corner clearance, the tangent curb
 4                     length betweenthe nearest edge ofa drivewayon an intersecting side street and
 5                     a collector roadway, or a driveway on a collector roadway and an intersection
 6                     with a cross street, shall be fifty (50) feet. Where the intersection is signalized
 7                     or is planned for signalization, driveways shall be limited to right-turn
                       movements if located within one hundred twenty-five (125) feet of a collector
                       or as provided for in Section 40. 350. 030(B)(4)(d)(2).

10              (g) Temporary Driveway. A temporary driveway may be allowed when, due to
11                     conditions beyond the control of the applicant, minimum driveway separation
12                     cannot be achieved at the time ofapplication. The review authority may approve
13                     a temporary
14                     driveway when an access plan shows future removal of the temporary
15                     driveway and a new drivewaywhich meets the spacingstandards
16                     shown above.

17        (3) AdditionalImprovements. The installationofother improvements suchas left-
18             turn lanes, right-turn lanes and traffic signals may be required by the County
19             Engineerwherefoundnecessaryonthebasis ofa traffic engineeringstudy. The
20             study shall address operations of the driveway or intersection, including the
21             adequacy of vehicular turning movements.

22   d.   Accessto Arterials. Inorderto limitthenumberofresidentialroadsintersectingwith
23        arterials while providing adequate neighborhood circulation, residential roads
24        intersecting with urban arterials shall be classified and constructed to standards
25        applicable to local residential access or collector roads unless the review authority
26        finds that a lesser classification adequately provides for the circulation needs of the
27        surrounding area. In those cases in which an urban access street is less than thirty-
28        six (36) feet wide, such street shall have a minimum width ofthirty-six (36) feet at
29        the intersection with the arterial and shall be tapered according to accepted
30        engineering practices and supplemental standards in Section 40. 350. 030(C)(l)(b) as
31        determined by the Public Works Director. Road approach permits not associated
32        with development shall be reviewed using a Type I process.

33        (1) Driveways. No driveways will be permitted to access onto urban or rural
34             arterials unless no other access to the site exists or can be provided.

35             (a) Spacing. Whendrivewayson arterials are permitted, they shall be spaced
36                    in accordance with Table 40. 350. 030-4.

37            (b) Number of Driveways. Where permitted, the number of driveways and
38                    driveway lanes on arterials shall be based upon an estimate of site traffic
39                    generation in accordance with Table 40. 350. 030-6.
40                    (i)   Multiple driveways are not permitted until the estimatedADT
41                          exceeds the number shown in the second column for the different
42                          type of land use. Then, an additional driveway is allowed each
43                          time the estimated ADT increases above the previous maximum
44                          ADT for each driveway as shown in the columns for minor
45                          arterials and principal arterials; provided, that the additional



                                                                                                    206
 1                   driveways meet the spacing requirements specified in Table
 2                   40. 350. 030-4. As an example, a commercial land use on a minor
 3                   arterial has one (1) driveway up to two thousand (2, 000) ADT,
 4                   then two (2) driveways for two thousand one (2, 001) to five
 5                   thousand five hundred (5, 500) ADT,three (3) driveways for five
 6                   thousand five hundred one (5, 501) to nine thousand (9, 000)
 7                   ADT, and so on.

 8              (ii) A permit for exclusiveuse ofa truck drivewayin additionto thenontruck
 9                   traffic may be granted for commercial uses that exceed thirty thousand
10                   (30,000) square feet ofgross floor space.
11              (iii) Two (2) driveway exit lanes are allowed when the ADT exceeds one
12                   thousand (1, 000).

13        (c)   Width. A single-family residential driveway onto an urban arterial shall be
14              fifteen (15) to thirty-five (35) feet in width; provided, that ajoint-use driveway
15              serving two (2) residential lots shall not exceed thirty-six (36) feet in width. A
16              commercial and multifamily two (2) way driveway onto an arterial shall be
17              twenty-four (24) to forty (40) feet in width.

18        (d) Comer Clearance. To provide adequate comer clearance, the tangent curb
19              length between the nearest edge of a driveway on an intersecting side street and
20              an arterial roadway, or a driveway on an arterial roadway and an intersection
21              with a cross street, shall be fifty (50) feet. Where the intersection is signalized
22              or is planned for signalization, driveways shall be limited to right-turn
23              movements if locatedwithintwo hundredfifty (250) feet onminor andprincipal
24              arterials or as provided for in Section 40. 350. 030(B)(4)(d)(2).

25        (e) Temporary Driveway. A temporary driveway may be allowed when, due to
26              temporary conditions beyond the control of the applicant, minimum driveway
27              separationcannotbe achievedat the time of application. The review authority
28              may approve a temporarydrivewaywhenanaccessplanshowsfuture removal
29              of the temporary driveway and a new driveway which meets the spacing
30              standards shown above is assured to be constructed.

31   (2) Additional Improvements. The installation of other improvements such as left-turn
32        lanes, right-turn lanes and traffic signals may be required by the County Engineer
33        where found necessary on the basis of a traffic engineering study. The study shall
34        address operations of the driveway or intersection, including the adequacy of
35        vehicular turning movements.

36   (3) Medians and Channelization Policy. In order to preserve capacity and promote
37        safety, urban arterials shall generally include raised medians to restrict cross
38        traffic movements. In general, full-access intersections, signalized and
39        nonsignalized, on arterials will be permitted only with other county, state and
40        city roads as are designated on the Arterial Atlas, as amended. Circulation from
41        such intersections in most cases will satisfy the access needs of adjacent land.
42        The County Engineer is solely responsible for decisions regarding the
43        placement of raised medians on county roads. Any decision by the county that
44        a raised median will not be installed on an arterial will require a specific
45        determination by the County Engineer that (a) the roadway will operate safely




                                                                                             207
 1               without a raised median and (b) that the roadway will operate at the required
 2               capacitywithouta raisedmedian. Otherfactorsto be consideredby the County
 3               Engineerinclude, but are not limitedto, the traffic volumes and speeds on the
 4               roadway, the crash history on the roadway, roadway geometric factors and
 5               other characteristics, and the adequacy of sight lines and distances.
 6               Additionally, the number offronting driveways, the traffic volumes on those
 7               driveways, the likely adverse impacts on fronting and other properties, the
 8               extent of access control along the arterial, and the number and location of other
 9               accesses available to those properties will be considered.

10               For commercial zones, raised medians will not be required on arterials where
11               the averageweekdaytraffic is projectedto be less thantwentythousand
12               (20,000)vehiclesperdayoverthenextfive (5) yearperiodandwherethe
13               CountyEngineermakesthedeterminationthatthe roadwaywill operate safely
14               and without operational problems.

15   e.     Access to State Routes. If the access serving a development is onto a state road or
16          highway, required dedication and/or improvements thereto must meet the
17          requirements ofthe WashingtonDepartmentofTransportation.

     5.   FrontageRoads/Improvement.
 3        a.   General Requirement. Unless already fully developed to the transportation standards and
 4             subject to the limitations set forth in this section and in Sections 40. 350. 030(B)(15) and
 5             40. 550. 010, a partial-width road shall be established and constructed to the applicable right-
 6             of-way or easement and improvement standards set out in Section
 7             40. 350. 030 to that portion ofa frontage public or private road which abuts a parcel being
 8             developed as a condition of development approval.

 9             (1) The right-of-way or easement width shall be a minimum of one-half C/2) of that
10                  specified in Tables 40. 350. 030-2 and 40. 350. 030-3; provided, that such minimum
11                  width may be increased where necessary to accommodate the minimum roadway
12                  improvement provided below to allow a minimum three (3) feet of right-of-way
13                  beyond the back of the sidewalk for urban public roads unless the sidewalk is
14                  detached from the curb with sufficient room to provide for utilities and signing, or for
15                  neededconstruction clearance, slopes or other features.
16                  In the case of a development containing an urban arterial or fronting on an urban
17                  arterial street, the developer shall only be required to construct improvements up
18                  to forty-four (44) feet in width, or twenty-two(22) feetona partial-widthfrontage,
19                  together with curbs and sidewalks, unless a wider section is necessary to
20                  accommodate




                                                                                                        209
 1              the development.
 2              Sufficient right-of-way and easement for a partial-width road must be
 3              provided to accommodate all necessary appurtenances required for
 4              construction including, but not limited to, approved cut or fill slopes or
 5              retaining structures if needed. If sufficient right-of-way is not available, slope
 6              easements from neighboringproperties are an acceptable alternative. Such
 7              easements shall be recorded with the final plat. The county may require the
 8              proposed road cross-sections showing neighboring topography be submitted in
 9              order to determine if the partial-width road can be constructed as required.

10        (2)   Partial-width roadways shall be a minimum oftwenty (20) feet wide; provided,
11              that      curb      extensions,      when       allowed     under     Section
12              40. 350. 030(B)(3)(b)(2)(b)(iv)(c), may reduce the minimum traveled way width
13              on urban local roads to no less than sixteen (16) feet.

14        (3)   New partial-width roads will be allowed in commercial, office or industrial
15              developments only afiter a traffic study verifies the adequacy of the roadway
16              for clearance andturning movements.

17        (4) Wherephysical obstructions or development constraints preclude or limit full
18              completion of the frontage road on the abutting property, the partial-width
19              roads may be allowed.

20        (5) Parkingshall beprohibitedalongpartial-widthroads,withsignsandpavement
21              markings being the responsibility of the developer unless the partial-width road
22              is at least twenty-four (24) feet wide to provide a clear sixteen (16) foot wide
23              traveled way in additionto an eight (8) foot wide parking lane.

24        (6) Wherefrontageimprovements are required, the county will perform pavement
25              deflection testing to determine the adequacy of the existing pavement. Where
26              remaining life of the pavement is less than five (5) years, the developer shall
27              constmct the roadway to current standards to the centerline or twenty-two (22)
28              feet, whichever is less. If remaining life is greater than five (5) years, the road
29              shall be cut back to a location where the structure is sound and the widening
30              constructed. However, in no case shall the reconstruction be less than four (4)
31              feet in width from the existing edge of pavement to the new edge of pavement
32              or face of curb. The county may require reconstruction to the centeriine or
33              twenty-two
34              (22) feet, whicheveris less, if the review authority determines the geometries
35              or other existing features are inadequate.

36   b.   Exceptions.
37        (1) The right-of-way and easement requirements of Section 40.350.030(B)(5)(a)
38              shall not apply to the construction, remodeling or enlargement of any Group R-
39              Division 3 (single-family or duplex) or Group U occupancy (as defined in the
40              International Building Code), to the construction of any accessory residential
41              structure, to any sign, or to the structural addition, alteration or repair to any
42              existing stmcture within any twelve (12) month period which neither exceeds
43              twenty-five percent (25%) of the value of the existing structure nor increases
44              the total floor space of the structure by more than ten percent (10%),




                                                                                                     210
               (2) The roadway frontage improvement requirements of Section 40. 350. 030(B)(5)(a)
                   shall not apply to:

                     (a) Rural developmentsnot locatedwithinrural centers; or
 4                   (b) Urban or rural center developments which the County Engineer finds, based
 5                         upon an engineering traffic study, will not result in an increase of total site trip
 6                         generations during the p.m. peak hour of more than ten percent (10%);
 7                         provided, that such otherwise exempt developments shall be required to make
 8                         intersection and sight distance improvements in accordance with Sections
 9                         40. 350. 030(B)(7) and (8) and such frontage road improvements as are necessary
10                         in order to provide minimally safe access to the development.

11        c.   Deferral.

12             (1) In the event that requiredfrontage road unprovements are included as a portion of a
13                   county road project on the county's six (6) year transportation improvement program
14                   scheduled to be undertaken within six (6) years, the developer, in lieu of constructing
15                   or guaranteeing the construction pursuant to Section 40.350.030(C)(4)(i) of such
16                   frontage improvements, may be pennitted to contribute a proportionate share towards
17                   the cost of such county road project by an agreement consistent with the requirements
18                   ofROW82.02.020.

19             (2) The development approval authority may defer frontage road improvements, in
20                   whole or in part, where the current development proposal is for multiple lots in the
21                   Rl-5, Rl-6, Rl-7. 5, Rl-10 or Rl-20 zoning districts larger than one (1) acre and a
22                   covenant running with the land is recorded requiring such improvements to be
23                   undertaken when redivision is proposed at an urban density.

24             (3) The development approval authority may defer frontage road improvements, m
25                   whole or in part, where the development proposal is located in a rural center, and a
26                   covenant running with the land is recorded requiring the owner to contribute their
27                   share to a larger road or frontage improvement project when undertaken by the
28                   county. For the purposes of this subsection, "share" of the cost of deferred frontage
29                   improvements includes the then-current cost of engineering and frontage
30                   improvements required at preliminary development approval divided by the
31                   number of lots. Deferrals of improvements under this subsection may be denied
32                   when:

33                  (a)    Conditions are, or will be caused to be, inadequate to provide a minimum
34                         level of service as specified in Section 40. 350. 020; or

35                  (b) A significant traffic or safety hazard would be caused or materially
36                         aggravatedby the proposed development; or

37                  (c)    The development must provide safe walking conditions per RCW
38                         58. 17. 110.

39   6.   Off-Site Road Improvement.

40        a.   General. Nothing in this section shall be construed to preclude denial of a proposed
41             development where off-site road conditions are inadequate to provide a minimum
42             level of service as specified in Section 40. 350. 020 or a significant traffic or safety



                                                                                                          211
 1        hazard would be caused or materially aggravated by the proposed development;
 2        provided, that the applicant may voluntarily agree to mitigate such direct impacts in
 3        accordancewith the provisions ofRCW 82.02. 020.

 4   b.   Requirements for Off-SiteAccess Road Improvements. All roads providingaccess
 5        to parcels being developed, whether such roads are to be public or private, shall at a
 6        minimum:

 7        (1) Within the urban area, have an unobstructed aiid paved roadway width of
 8            twenty (20) feet, or eighteen (18) feet ofpavement with one
 9            (1) foot wide shoulders. Any preexisting roadwaynarrowerthan eighteen(18)
10            feet with one (1) foot shoulders shall be widened to the full twenty (20) foot
11            standard. Those portions of a roadway reduced in width due to curb
12            extensions allowed under Section 40. 350. 030(B)(5)(a)(2) are not required to
13            meet the above requirement, provided a minimum of sixteen (16) feet of
14            pavement is provided.

15        (2) Withintherural area,off-sitepublicaccessroads shallmeetthe samestandards
16             as Section 40. 350.030(B)(6)(b)(l). Off-site private access roads are not
17             required to be paved but shall have an all-weather driving surface, with an
18             unobstructed roadway width of twenty (20) feet; except in those cases where
19             the preexisting road is eighteen (18) feet wide, additional wideningto twenty
20             (20) feet is not necessary. Any preexisting roadway narrower than eighteen (18)
21             feet shall be widened to the full twenty (20) foot standard.

22        (3) Have an unobstructed vertical clearance ofnot less thanthirteen (13) feet six (6)
23             inches (13' 6").
24        (4) Notwithstanding the foregoing, roads providing access to legal lots created prior to
25            July 9, 1996, being developed with a Group R-Division 3 structure or residential
26            mobile home, may be constructed with a minimum twelve (12) foot wide
27            unobstructed all-weather driving surface.

28        (5) Off-siteprivateroadsprovidingaccessto lots beingdevelopedshallhavea minimum
29             easement partial width oftwenty-five (25) feet from such lot to a public road.

30   c.   Requirements for Off-Site Intersection Improvement. The owners of a parcel being
31        developed shall enter into a signal participation agreement to contribute a proportionate
32        share towards the cost of a traffic signal when:

33        (1) An intersection impacted bythe proposed development is designated by the county for
34            installation of a traffic signal; and

35        (2) The parcel being developed is not located within a traffic impact fee (TIF) service
36             area; and

37        (3) Duringthe peak hour, the development generates a minimum ofthree percent (3%)
38             increase of traffic on the intersection approach leg impacted by the development, or
39             five (5) trips on a minor leg (those legs of the intersection that have the smaller
40             approach volume), or twenty (20) trips on a major leg (those legs of the intersection
41             that have the larger approach volumes); and

42        (4) The peak hour level of service at the leg of the intersection impacted by the site-
43            generated traffic is at or will fall below the minimum level of service standard for



                                                                                                  212
 1                     that intersection as defined in Section 40. 350. 020.

 2   7     Intersection Design.

 3         a.    Intersection Geometry. Private and public roads shall be laid out so as to intersect at an
 4               angle as near to a right angle as practicable, but in no case less than seventy-five (75)
 5               degrees for roads intersecting collectors and arterials and no less than sixty (60) degrees
 6               for access roads, unless modified pursuant to Section 40. 550. 010. Opposing roads
 7               accessing an intersection shall either be aligned or will be separated by a minimum
 8               intersection spacing, as specified in Table 40. 350. 030-3. Depending on the width of the
 9               intersectionopening, anoffsetgreaterthanten (10) feetfor accessroadsor five (5) feetfor
10               collectors and arterials is not allowed.

11         b.    Intersection Right-of-Way. Intersections shall have a minimum corner radius often (10)
12               feet alongthe right-of-waylines for accessroads anda minimum comerradius oftwenty-
13               five (25) feet along the right-of-way lines for collectors and arterials, unless road
14               improvements require a greater radius.
15               On collectors and arterials, the dedication ofright-of-way on comers shall include
16               the chord ofthe radius. The county will accept an easement for this chordinstead
17               of dedication of right-of-way. For arterials intersecting with other arterials, an
18               additional six (6) feet of right-of-way may be required on both sides of the
19               roadway if a future turning lane is required, basedon transportation impact study,
20               within twenty (20) years from the time an application is submitted. The length of
21               the additionalright-of-way shall be determined basedon the transportation impact
22               study.

23         c.    Paving of Intersecting Area. Where connecting to a paved street, whether public or
24               private, the connecting road or driveway (excluding driveways in rural area) shall
25               be paved twenty-five (25) feet back from the nearest edge of the traveled lane, or
26               shall be equal to the minimum intersection radii as specified in Table 40. 350. 030-3,
27               whichever is greater.
28               Driveways in rural areas connecting with paved public roads shall be paved from
29               the edge of the public road to the right-of-way or to twenty
30               (20) feet from the edge, whichever is greater.
31               Rural paving shall be done in accordance with the equivalent base structural
32               requirements ofthe gravel road section as noted in the Standard Details Manual.

33   8.   SightDistances.As notedin subsection (A)(2) ofthis section,this subsectionalso applies
34        to applications for building permits and applications for access to public roads. Unless
35        modified pursuant to Section 40. 550.010, public and private roads shall comply with the
36        following sight distance requirements:

37        a.    Stopping Sight Distance. Intersection sight distance and stopping sight distance
38              values are based on the default assumption of level grades, normally intersecting
39              roadways, and with passenger cars as the design vehicle. When deviating from the
40              default assumptions, the engineer shall take the roadway grades, intersection skew,
41              and design vehicle classification into consideration when calculating the required
42              intersection sight distance and/or stopping sight distance.
43              Public roads shall have minimum stopping sight distance, as measured from a
44              height of three and one-half (3^) feet to a target on the roadway nominally two (2)
45              feet in height, in accordance with Table 40. 350. 030-7. The effect of grades on
46              stopping sight distance shall be calculated using the most current version of the



                                                                                                          213
 1        Washington State Department of Transportation's Design Manual.
 2        For unpostedroadways,the legal maximum speedlimit shall be fifty (50) miles per
 3        hour per the "basicrule" under RCW 46.61.400.
 4

                            Table 40.350.030-7. Stopping SightDistance
                         Speed (mph)                   Minimum Stopping Distance (feet)
                              25                                        155
                              30                                       200
                              35                                       250
                              40                                       305
                              45                                       360
                              50                                       425

 5   b.   Controlled Intersection and Driveway Sight Distance Triangle. Traffic entering an
 6        uncontrolled public road from stop-controlled public roads, or from private roads or private
 7        driveways, shall have minimum intersection sight distances, as shown in Table
 8        40. 350. 030-8. Sight distance shall be measured from an eye height of three and one-half
 9        (3'/2) feet above the controlled road pavement surface and fifteen (15) feet from the edge of
10        the vehicle traveled way of the uncontrolled public road. The object height on the
11        uncontrolled public road shall be three and one-half(3'/2) feet above the pavement surface
12        located four (4) feet to the right of the striped or assumed centerline of the roadway. For
13        multilane highways, the object on the uncontrolled roadway shall be located on the
14        approach lane closest to the controlled side street. Sight distance triangles shall be clear of
15        all obstructions, including, but not limited to, landscaping, fences, structures and earth
16        berms between the heights of three (3) and eight and one-half(8y2 ) feet, as measured from
17        the pavement surface.

19   c.   Yield-Controlled Intersections. For roads with a posted speed of twenty-five (25) mph or
20        less, traffic entering an uncontrolled public road from a yield-controlled public road shall
21        have minimum intersection sight distance of two hundred fifty (250) feet. The intersection




                                                                                                  214
 1               sight distance
 2               shall be measured at one hundredthirty (130) feet back on the yield-controlled
 3               approach from the line that is four (4) feet from the uncontrolled roadway center,
 4               in drivers' direction, for both approaches.

 5         d.    Uncontrolled Intersections. Uncontrolled intersections for access roads in urban and
 6               rural areas with a posted speed limit of twenty-five (25) mph or less shall have an
 7               unobstructed intersection sight distance triangle per Section 40. 350. 030(B)(8)(b) of
 8               one hundred (100) feet on both approaches. This requirement may be reduced to
 9               eighty (80) feet for intersections abutting comer lots in an urban residential
10               subdivision. The intersection sight distance shall be measured along the lines four
11               (4) feet from the roadway center, in drivers' direction, for both approaches.

12         e.   New Urban and Rural Residential Driveways. New urban and rural residential
13              driveways accessing roads with a speed limit of over twenty-five (25) mph are
14              subject to Table 40. 350. 030-8.

15   9.   Street Extensions.

16        a.    General Requirements. Where a public or private road has been constructed, created
17              or stubbed in such a manner as to be able to be extended or widened in accordance
18              with the dark County Arterial Atlas, other requirements of this section, or prior
19              approved development, the following shall apply:

20              (1) Connection with Adjacent Areas. All residences, buildings or structures shall
21                    be constructed in such a position on the property that they will not interfere with
22                    the extension or widening of the roadway to adjacent areas and shall be so
23                    situated that such extension will make orderly and planned development for
24                    additional road installations to meet the reasonable minimum requirements of
25                    good and safe traffic circulation, consistent with applicable zoning setbacks.

26              (2) Right-of-Way for Street Extensions. Right-of-way or private easements
27                   necessary to such extension or widening and falling within parcels being
28                   developed shall be granted or created as a condition of development approval.

29        b.    UrbanDevelopments.
30              (1) Provisions for Future Extensions. Any street within the urban area for which
31                  an extension in the future is planned shall be extended to the edge of the
32                  property being developed through the plat, short plat or site plan approval
33                  process, unless otherwise approved by the review authority. The street stub
34                  shall be a full street section, including sidewalks.

35              (2) Use of Temporary Turnaround. If a road serving more than eighteen (18)
36                   dwelling units or more than one hundred fifty (150) feet inlength temporarily
37                   terminates at a property boundary, a temporary turnaround cul-de-sac bulb
38                   consistent with this standard shall be constructed near the plat boundary. The bulb
39                   shallbepavedandshallbeeighty(80)feetindiameter,whichmayincludethewidth
40                   ofthe roadway with side-walks, where required, termmating at the point where the
41                   bulb radius begins. Removal of the temporary turnaround and extension of the
42                   sidewalk shall be the responsibility ofthe developer who extends the road (see the
43                   Standard Details Manual). The easement for a temporary turnaround may be




                                                                                                           215
 1                   extinguished without county approval after the temporary himaround is determined
 2                   to be no longer necessaryby the county.

 3             (3) Barricades, for placement of temporary and permanent barricades, see Section
 4                 40.350.030(C)(4)(f).
 5       c.    Rural Developments. For any road in the rural area for which an extension is planned, the
 6             right-of-way falling within parcels being developed shall be dedicated where the existing
 7             platting pattern, the development under review and the potential for development of
 8             adjacent lots demonstrate a need for the dedication.

     10. Private Roads.

10        a.   Purpose. The purpose of private road standards is to provide an option to retain rural
11             character, reduce costs to serve large rural lots, and allow more control, security, and sense
12             of identity when public roads are not neededfor public circulation.
13             For private road maintenance agreement, private road inspection, and developer
14             maintenance obligation for private roads, see Section 40. 350. 030(C)(4)(g).

15       b.    Approval CriteriaandRequirements.

17                   area:


18                   (a)      When they conflict with the Arterial Atlas, as amended; or

20                   (c) Whenthey connect two (2) public roads (except for commercial or industrial
21                           uses in urban areas); or

22                   (d) Whentheyaretoservemorethanfifty(50)potentialresidentiallots inrural areas
23                           or one hundred (100) lots in urban areas, created after April 12, 1994, except
24                           within a planned unit development; provided, that where expansion of a public
25                           road is not presently feasible, the limitations of this subsection shall not apply
26                           if the affected internal and frontage roads are
27                            improved to public standards (if otherwise required) and dedicatedto
28                            the county, with the acceptance of such dedication(s) being deferred
29                            until extension of a public road allows connection.

30             (2) Additional Requirements for Urban Private Roads. Private roads within
31                  developments may be allowed, provided they meet the following additional
32                  criteria:

33                  (a)      Structural sections shall be the same as for public roads of equivalent
34                           classification;

35                  (b) A pedestrianaccessplan shall be approved;
36                  (c) Internal traffic calming measures or devices suchas speedhumps or traffic
37                           circles may be required.

39             site plan, or within the Developer Covenants to dark County for any subdivision or

11   12. Cul-de-Sacs and Turnarounds.

12        a.    Cul-de-Sacs.

13             (1) ShortCul-de-Sacs.Theminimumright-of-waydiameteracrossthebulbsection
14                   is seventy (70) feet, with a minimum pavement diameter across the bulb section
15                   of sixty (60) feet. Rolled curb and gutter and thickened sidewalk shall be
16                   constructed in accordance with the Standard Details Manual.

17             (2) Whenevera residential urban cul-de-sac street is more than one hundredfifty
18                   (150) feet long, a bulb or hammerhead shall be constmcted as follows:

19                   (a)   Standard Cul-de-Sac Turnarounds. The minimum right-of-way diameter
20                         across the bulb section is one hundred (100) feet, with a minimum
21                         pavement diameter across the bulb of ninety (90)
22                         feet. Alternatively, right-of-way may be reduced to eighty (80) feet, with a
23                         paved diameter across the bulb of seventy (70) feet, provided rolled curb and
24                         gutter withthickenedsidewalkis constructed in accordancewiththe Standard
25                         Details Manual.

26                   (b) Temporary Cul-de-Sacs. Temporary cul-de-sacs shall provide at minimum
27                         paved diameterofeighty (80) feet forthe bulb. Thoseportions ofthe bulbarea
28                         lying outside the straight-street right-of-way shall be provided as a temporary
29                         easementpendingextensionofthe street.
30                   (c) UrbanCul-de-SacorEyebrowIsland.Optionalfeaturefor any cul-de-sacwhen
31                         the bulb's paved diameter is ninety (90) feet or less; mandatory when the bulb's
32                         paved diameter exceeds ninety (90) feet. If provided, islands shall have full-
33                         depth vertical curbs. Minimum island diameter shall be twenty (20) feet and
34                         there shall be at least twenty-two (22) feet of paved traveled way in a shoulder
35                         type section; thirty (30) feet ofpaved traveled way in a curb type section around
36                         the circumference. Islands shall be grassed or landscaped. Islands shall be
37                         maintained by the adjoining lot owners. Islands are required on eyebrows with
38                         a radius greater than thirty (30) feet. The minimum island diameter shall be ten
39                         (10) feet.
40             (3) In the urbanandurbanholdingareasofthe county, a permanentcul-de-sacshall not
41                   be longer than six hundred (600) feet measured from curb line of intersecting street to
42                   the center ofthe bulb section. Proposedmodificationsto this rule will be considered
43                   by the review authority, based on pertinent traffic planning factors such as



                                                                                                       217
          topography, sensitive areas and existing development.

 2   (4) In the rural areaofthe county, there is no limitation to the length ofa dead-endroad
 3        or cul-de-sac; provided, however, that approved turnarounds on roads greater than
 4        one-half ('/2) mile long are provided every one thousand three hundred twenty (1, 320)
 5        feet or as close to that distance as practical considering topography, natural features
 6        and existing manmade structures. Approved turnarounds may include cul-de-sacs,
 7        which may include the width of the roadway, intersecting public or private roads,

24   13. Urban Neighborhood Traffic Management.

25         a.    Purposes. Urban neighborhoodtraffic management is intended to manage traffic
26              speeds within residential neighborhoods and to discourage external traffic cutting
27              through residential neighborhoods.

28         b.   Applicability. The provisions ofthis section shall apply only to access roads within
29               a development intheRl-5, Rl-6, Rl-7.5, Rl-10, Rl-20, R 12, R 18, R 22. R 30,
30               R 43, OR 15, OR 18, OR 22, OR 30. OR 13 R-24 R-3(» R-50 OR-24 OR-36
31               OR-50, CR-1, CR-2, or MX zonmg districts which meet one (1) of the following
32              conditions:

33              (1) Projected average daily trip of greater than six hundred (600) and less than
34                  two thousand (2, 000) motor vehicles and a sight distance in excess of six
35                  hundred (600) feet; or

36              (2) Determined by the County Engineer, not his or her designee, that traffic
37                    calming measures and/or traffic calming devices are warranted.
38                     Thereviewauthoritymay waivethe requirements ofthis sectionfor Type I and
39                     Type II applications where the conditions listed above will not occur.

40        c.    Standards and Requirements. If the condition in Section 40.350.030(B)(13)(b) occurs,
41              traffic calming measures and/or traffic calming devices shall be required:



                                                                                                               219
 1                   (1) Traffic Calming Measures. Traffic calming measures, such as "T" intersections,
 2                          street trees, curvilinear streets, or entry treatments, shall be mcorporated into the
 3                          overall development design to manage traffic speeds.
 4                   (2) Traffic CalmingDevices. Traffic calming devices, suchas speedbumps/humpsand
 5                          the devices shown in the Standard Details Manual, or as approved by the review
 6                          authority, shall be installed.

 7        14 UrbanTransit Circulation Standards.Newresidential, commercial andindustrial developments
 8            shall be reviewed with the participation of C-TRAN invited during the development review
 9            process under Subtitle 40. 5 to ensure appropriate design and integration of transit facilities into
10            the development.

11        15 Right-of-WayStandards.Dedicationofarterialright-of-wayshalloccurwithin developments in
12           accordancewiththe transportation element ofthe comprehensiveplan.
13            a.    Public Roads. The minimum right-of-way and roadway widths for all public urban and
14                  rural roads shall be as shown on the design criteria detailed in Section 40. 350. 030(B)(3).
15                  Right-of-way widths, roadway widths and structural sections shall be consistent with
16                  projected trafficvolumes plannedfor twenty (20) years from the time ofdesign.Additional
17                  right-of-way or easement may be required where necessary to accommodate slopes, sight
18                  distance or other features necessary for maintenance or to enhance safety.

19            b.    Urban Planned Unit andMultifamily Developments. When constructed, public roads shall
20                  be constructed according to the design criteria of Section 40.350.030(B)(3); provided,
21                  parking may be deleted when it is shown that four (4) non-tandem off-street parking spaces
22                  per unit are provided and distinct signs and markings show that no parking is permitted;
23                  and sidewalks along internal circulation roadways may be deleted by the review authority
24                  when it is shown that public walkways adequate for pedestrian circulation and meeting or
25                  exceeding the requirements of Section 40. 350. 015 are provided.

26            c.    Commercial, Office Campus and Industrial Areas. When constructed, public roads shall
27                  be constructed according to the design criteria of Section 40. 350. 030(B)(3). The structure
28                  of the road shall be designed according to minor arterial standards.

29   C.   Specifications for Design and Construction.

30                     1.    Transportation StandardSpecifications.
31             a.    Transportation Standards. The standards for dark County roads and bridges, and
32                   all other construction within publicly ownedrights-of-way, shall consist of:

33                  (1) The current published edition ofthe Standard Specifications for Road, Bridge
34                       and Municipal Construction as published by the Washington Department of
35                       Transportation (WSDOT) and the American Public Works Association
36                       (APWA), referred to as standard specifications;

37                  (2) The current StandardPlans for Road and Bridge Constmction as published by
38                       WSDOT and APWA (referred to as standard plans); and

39                  (3) The StandardDetails Manual as defined in Section 40. 100.070, and issued by
40                      the County Engineer, containing typical drawings to implement transportation,
41                      erosion control, drainage, and other engineering standards adopted in the dark




                                                                                                           220
                      County Code.
 2         b.    Supplemental Standards. To implement the above standards, the following
 3               publications and their subsequent revisions are adopted and shall apply:

 4              (1) TheWSDOTDesignManual;
 5              (2) The WSDOT Construction Manual;

                (3) TheWSDOTHydraulicsManual;
 7              (4) A Policy on Geometric Design of Highways and Streets prepared by the
 8                    American Association of State Highway and Transportation Officials
 9                    (AASHTO);
10              (5) The Washington State adopted Manual on Uniform Traffic Control Devices
11                    (MUTCD) prepared by the U. S. Department of Transportation, Federal
12                    HighwayAdministration;

13              (6) Chapter40.386, Stormwater andErosionControl;
14              (7) Chapter 51-304 WAC, state of Washington adoption of the Americans with
15                    Disabilities Act into the International Building Code; and

16              (8) The AASHTO LRFD Bridge Design Specifications, U. S. Customary Units,
17                    including its commentary (refer to Section 40.350.040, Private Bridges, for
18                    exceptions to this manual).
19        c.     Conflict of Standards. In the event of conflict with any of the specifications, the County
20               Engineer shall specify which of the supplemental specifications will apply.

21   2.   Construction Plan Requirements for Transportation and Utility Improvements. The applicant
22        shall submit to the responsible official plans and specifications for street and utility construction
23        for the proposed development. The plans and specifications shall include a vicinity map, a plan
24        and profile, special provisions, and reference to the standard specifications, including the typical
25        sections.


26        a.    The Plan. The plan shall include the road alignment at a scale ofnot less than one (1) inch
27              to fifty (50) feet (where less detail is required, a scale of one (1) inch to one hundred (100)
28               feet may be approved by the responsible official) showing centeriine stationing on all
29              intersecting streets, with bearings on centerlines; curve data on all horizontal curves;
30              right-of-way; relevant topography; existing and proposed utility location; street names in
31              the development and adjoining the development; typical roadway section showing
32              placement of utilities; existing and proposed drainage and water qualit}' appurtenances;
33              sidewalk ramp locations; floodplain and wetland boundaries; signalization,
34              channelization, striping and signing; sufficient topographic data adjacent to the site to
35              identify cut and fill limits from the proposed improvements and such further data as may
36              be required by the responsible official.

37        b.    The Profile. The profile shall show the relevant original ground lines using the same
38              stationing as in the plan, control elevations, grade line showing the proposed grades,
39              vertical curves, all bench marks, the vertical datum, and such further information as may
40              be reasonably required by the responsible official. For new streets, the relevant original
41              ground lines will show the ground line at centerline at a minimum and also at the edges



                                                                                                        221
 1          ofthe right-of-wayif grade differencesare significant(or alternatively, surveyedcontour
 2          linesontheplanview). Foranexisting street, the applicant shall also showthe groundline
 3          at the edge of pavement or face of curb, whichever is applicable. The profile lines for
 4          roads extending to the perimeter of any development shall be extended a minimum of
 5          three hundred (300) feet beyond the perimeter to include any change in contours which
 6          would affect the profile of the extension of the proposed road. If vertical control is
 7          available within one-half ('/2) mile of the project, the applicant shall use the National
 8          Geodetic Vertical Datum of 1929 (1947 adjustment) as a vertical datum. If county
 9          horizontal control, based on the North American Datum of 1983 (1991 adjustment) is
10          available within one-half (i^) mile of the project, the applicant shall make a tie to the grid
11          bearing and coordinate of the datum.

12    c.    Required Project Construction Notes. Any required constmction notes shall be shown or
13          referenced on the plans.
14    d.    Format. The cover sheet of all plans shall include a statement identifying which
15          standard specifications will apply to the project. Plan and profile may be shown on
16          the same sheet with profiles shown on the bottom half of the sheet. Sheets shall
17          measure twenty-two (22) to twenty-four (24) inches in height by thirty-four (34) to
18          thirty-six (36) inches in length with a borderline of one and one-half(l'/2) inches on
19          the left side of the length of the sheet and one-half (Vi) inch on remaining sides.
20          When more than two (2) plan sheets are used, an overall development layout shall
21          be submitted showing the relationship of roads and utilities.

22          (1) A north arrow shall be shown on each plan view sheet ofthe plans and adjacent
23              to any other drawing which is not oriented the same as other drawings on the
24               sheet.

25         (2) Character size shall not be smaller than eight one-hundredths (0. 08) inch high.

26         (3) All detail drawingsshall be includedinthe drawings.
27         (4) A title block shall appear on each sheet ofthe plan set and shall be placedin
28               the lower right-hand corner ofthe sheet, across the bottom edge of the sheet or
29               across the right-handedge ofthe sheet. The title block shall include the name
30               ofthe project, the engineering firm, the sheet title and the owner, if not shown
31               on the first sheet.

32   Transportation Design Specifications. The design criteria set out in Tables 40. 350. 030-2
33   and 40. 350. 030-3 are adopted as a portion of the dark County Standard Specifications.
34   Such criteria are applicable to roads located within and adjacent to a development. These
35   criteria are intended for normal conditions. The responsible official may require higher
36   standards for unusual site conditions.

37   a.    Typical Roadway Section. The typical roadway section shall be as shown on the
38         county standard plans. The roadway section used shall be detailed on the
39         construction plans submitted for each new roadway or improvement to an existing
40         roadway. Any deviation from the standard plans for typical cross-section will require
41         a modification pursuant to Section 40. 550. 010(A).

42   b.    RequiredDetails. Thetypical sectionshall showthe widthofright-of-way,widthof
43         roadway, type and compacted depth of surfacing and paving materials, and such
44         other dimensions as may be necessary or required. The location and width of



                                                                                                   222
          sidewalks, walkways, curbs or curb and gutter shall also be shown, where applicable.

 2   c.   Surfacing - Depth Determination. The depth of surfacing and paving materials shall
 3        be determined from the standard plans. The depth to be used shall be detennined
 4        from the functional use classificationofthe road to be improved and the AASHTO
 5        soil classification as shown in the Soil Survey of dark County, Washington,
 6        published by the U. S. Department of Agriculture, Natural Resources Conservation
 7        Service. In the case where the applicant wishes to submit an alternative surfacing
 8        and paving depth design, the applicant shall provide a design to support the proposed
 9        depths. The County Engineer shall establish and maintain criteria and methodologies
10        for performing alternative surfacing andpavement depth design, includingverified
11        soil engineering characteristics and traffic loadings for approving such alternative
12        designs.

13   d.   Alternate Surface Treatment. Alternate surface treatments, other than permeable
14        pavement, may only be used upon approval of the County Engineer. The applicant shall
15        supply the County Engineerwithspecificationsfor materials and applicationrates as part
16        of the approval. Permeable pavement is allowed under Section 40. 350. 030(B)(3)(b)(l)(a),
17        subject to the specifications in the current edition of the LID Technical Guidance Manual.

18   e.   Steeper Grades. Roads constructed in grades steeper than fifteen percent (15%) shall be
19        constructed with Portland cement concrete, with grooved surfacing. Rural roads are
20        exempted from this requirement.

21   f.   Grades and Drainage. Access road drainage facilities shall be designed to meet the
22        requirements of the county stormwater drainage and erosion control ordinances, and be
23        sufficient to prevent water damage or impairment from normal rain flow or surface water.
24        Erosion control shall be provided during construction to maintain the roadways such that
25        mud and debris are minimized consistent with such ordinances.
26        Drainage details and street profile grades shall be shown on the construction plans. In the
27        urban and urban reserve areas, the centerline profile street grades shall not be less than
28        one percent (1%) unless anintegralcurb andgutter sectionis used, in whichcasethe
29        minimum grade shall not be less than three-tenths percent (0. 3%). If any grading or filling
30        of lots or other areas outside the streets is to be done which exceeds those amounts
31        specifiedin Chapter 14.07, an overall gradingplan shall be submitted as a part ofthe
32        plans. In mral areas, driveway culverts must be a minimum of twelve (12) inches.
33        Culverts larger than the minimum will be sized by identifying the size ofculverts
34        upstream. These standards may be superseded where culverts are placed within waters
35        subject to regulation under Chapter 77. 55 or 77. 57 RCW, or Chapter 220-660 WAC.

36   g.   Passing Sight Distance. County roadways with centerline striping shall have minimum
37        passing sight distance, as measured from a height of three and one-half (3'/2) feet to an
38        object of three and one-half(3'/2) feet, in accordance with Table 40. 350. 030-9. The passing
39        sight distance shall be based on the most current version of the American Association of
40        State Highway and Transportation Officials (AASHTO) "A Policy on
41        Geometric Design ofHighwards and Streets."

42   h.   School Zone Traffic Control. School zone ti-affic control shall be updated when
43        impacted by a project in accordance with the "dark County School Zone Traffic
44        Control Policy."

45   i.   Pedestrian Crossing Treatment. Appropriate pedestrian crossing treatments shall be



                                                                                                 223
               evaluated and provided in accordance with the "dark County Pedestrian Crossing
               Treatment Policy."

         J.    Traffic Control Devices.
 4             (1) Reimbursable. The developer shall reimburse the county for the installation
 5                   and/or modification of all necessary traffic control devices including but not
 6                   limitedto streetname signs, warningandregulatory signs, pavementmarkings
 7                   and traffic signals within county right-of-way. The cost of all the traffic
 8                   control devices will be determined on a time and materials basis.

 9             (2) RoadName Signs (Private Roadto Private Road). Private roadname signs shall
10                 be provided, installed, and maintained by the developer.

11             (3) RoadNameSigns(PrivateRoadtoPublicRoad).Privateroadnamesignsshall
12                   be provided, installed, and maintained in county right-of-way by the county.

13             (4) Exceptions. Except for traffic signal related items, all other traffic control
14                   devices related to private roads shall be provided, installed and maintained by

16                   traffic control devices for private roads, such as stop control, may be installed
